<style>


@import url('https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Bodoni+Moda:ital,wght@0,500;0,600;0,700;1,500&family=Source+Serif+4:ital,wght@0,400;0,600;0,700;1,400&family=Manrope:wght@400;500;600;700;800&display=swap');
/* SILVERWOOD — forest, sage and brass. Bespoke campaign folio. */
.page:is(.sw,#p1) {width:210mm;height:296.8mm;padding:64px 57px 66px;box-sizing:border-box;background:#edf0e6;background-image:none;color:#253d34;font-family:'Source Serif 4',serif;font-size:14px;line-height:1.38;column-count:2;column-gap:27px;position:relative;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1)::before {content:'';position:absolute;top:26px;left:57px;right:57px;height:8px;border-top:1px solid #9b9f7d;border-bottom:1px solid #c5ccb6;background:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1)::after {content:'';position:absolute;left:57px;right:57px;bottom:46px;height:1px;background:#a4ac90;border:0;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) p,.page:is(.sw,#p1) li,.page:is(.sw,#p1) td {font-family:'Source Serif 4',serif;font-size:inherit;line-height:inherit;text-indent:0!important;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) p {margin:0 0 9px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) p:first-letter {font:inherit;float:none;margin:0;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) h1,.page:is(.sw,#p1) h2,.page:is(.sw,#p1) h3 {font-family:'Bodoni Moda',serif;color:#183d30;font-weight:600;font-variant:normal;text-transform:none;letter-spacing:-.35px;line-height:1.1;border:0;padding:0;break-after:avoid;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) h1 {font-size:37px;margin:0 0 15px;column-span:all;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) h2 {font-size:26px;margin:15px 0 12px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) h3 {font-size:21px;margin:14px 0 9px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) h4,.page:is(.sw,#p1) h5 {font-family:'Manrope',sans-serif;font-size:11px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:.7px;color:#254739;line-height:1.3;margin:0 0 8px;text-transform:uppercase;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) strong {font-weight:700;color:inherit;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) ul,.page:is(.sw,#p1) ol {padding-left:17px;margin:0 0 10px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) li {margin:0 0 5px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) a {color:inherit;text-decoration:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) blockquote {background:transparent;border:0;border-left:2px solid #a48d52;padding:4px 0 4px 13px;margin:12px 0;font-size:14px;color:#3b5346;box-shadow:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .note,.page:is(.sw,#p1) .descriptive {border:0;border-radius:0;background:#dce3d2;box-shadow:none;padding:13px 14px;margin:13px 0;break-inside:avoid;border-top:3px solid #65765a;font-family:inherit;font-size:13px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .descriptive {background:#e5ddc7;border-top-color:#a48d52;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .note p:last-child,.page:is(.sw,#p1) .descriptive p:last-child {margin-bottom:0;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) table {font-family:'Source Serif 4',serif;font-size:12px;line-height:1.3;margin:10px 0 14px;border-collapse:collapse;width:100%;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) th {font-family:'Manrope',sans-serif;font-size:10px;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.2px;text-align:left;background:#214838;color:#f5f0db;padding:7px 6px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) td {padding:6px;border-bottom:1px solid #bdc6ae;vertical-align:top;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) tbody tr:nth-child(odd) {background:#dfe5d5;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) tbody tr:nth-child(even) {background:transparent;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .footnote {position:absolute;bottom:27px;left:57px;right:85px;width:auto;text-align:left;font:600 8px 'Manrope',sans-serif;letter-spacing:1.2px;text-transform:uppercase;color:#536952;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .pageNumber {position:absolute;bottom:25px;right:57px;left:auto;font:600 13px 'Manrope',sans-serif;color:#354e3d;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .sweyebrow {font:700 9px 'Manrope',sans-serif;letter-spacing:2px;text-transform:uppercase;color:#718064;margin-bottom:18px;column-span:all;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-town h1 {font-size:41px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-town .swbody>h2:first-of-type {font-family:'Manrope',sans-serif;font-size:12px;font-weight:500;line-height:1.4;letter-spacing:.5px;column-span:all;margin:0 0 24px;color:#6d745d;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-intro,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter,.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1),.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back {column-count:1;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature .columnWrapper,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-intro .columnWrapper,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents .columnWrapper,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .columnWrapper,.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1) .columnWrapper,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back .columnWrapper {column-count:1;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature {padding:80px 85px 85px;font-size:17px;line-height:1.55;background:#e3e9db;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature h1,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature h2:first-child {font-size:42px;margin-bottom:25px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature .swbody {max-width:570px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature::before {top:45px;left:85px;right:85px;border-color:#8b9e7d;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-feature::after {height:100px;bottom:80px;left:auto;right:55px;width:100px;border:1px solid #a9b89b;border-radius:50%;background:repeating-linear-gradient(130deg,transparent 0 8px,#b7c4aa77 9px,transparent 10px 16px);opacity:.5;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-intro {padding:70px 75px;font-size:15px;line-height:1.43;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-intro h1 {font-size:43px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-intro h2 {font-size:24px;margin-top:19px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents {padding:80px 85px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents h1 {font-size:65px;margin-bottom:40px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents ul {padding:0;list-style:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-contents li {border-bottom:1px solid #b8c4ab;padding:13px 0;margin:0;font-family:'Manrope',sans-serif;font-size:14px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swtocnum {float:right;font-weight:700;color:#7c713e;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter {background:#153b2e;color:#e6e9da;padding:90px 80px 80px;font-size:17px;line-height:1.5;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter h1 {font-size:66px;color:#e9dfba;margin:20px 0 24px;letter-spacing:-1.8px;max-width:610px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter h2 {font-family:'Manrope',sans-serif;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:3px;font-size:13px;color:#c2b67f;margin:0 0 35px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter h3 {font-size:26px;color:#e9dfba;margin-top:30px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .sweyebrow {color:#b4bd95;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .note {background:#234b3b;color:#e5eadb;border-color:#b5a66b;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .note h5 {color:#d6c694;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .footnote,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter .pageNumber {color:#b7c3a6;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter::before {border-color:#7b926b;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-chapter::after {height:180px;bottom:65px;left:auto;right:40px;width:180px;border:1px solid #b4ad7038;border-radius:50%;background:repeating-linear-gradient(35deg,transparent 0 12px,#a4ac6b15 13px,transparent 14px 25px);}
.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1),.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back {padding:110px 75px;background:#0d3025;color:#eee7cb;overflow:hidden;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1)::before,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back::before {inset:25px;height:auto;border:1px solid #b3a36770;background:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1)::after,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back::after {left:45px;right:45px;top:45px;bottom:45px;height:auto;border:1px solid #748a5b77;background:none;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1) h1,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back h1 {color:#eee7cb;font-family:'Bodoni Moda',serif;font-size:88px;font-weight:500;letter-spacing:-3px;line-height:1;margin:0;position:relative;z-index:2;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1):is(.sw-cover,#p1) h1:first-of-type,.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back h1:first-of-type {font-size:50px;font-style:italic;letter-spacing:-1px;margin-bottom:14px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swsubtitle {font:400 17px 'Manrope',sans-serif;line-height:1.7;max-width:380px;margin:34px 0;color:#bbc8ad;position:relative;z-index:2;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swcovermeta {position:absolute;bottom:100px;left:75px;right:75px;font:600 11px 'Manrope',sans-serif;letter-spacing:2px;text-transform:uppercase;color:#cfbf87;z-index:2;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swcovermeta p {font:inherit;line-height:1.9;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swgrove {position:absolute;width:480px;height:480px;left:160px;top:440px;border:1px solid #a8a86a55;border-radius:50%;background:radial-gradient(ellipse at 50% 70%,#b1b36c33,transparent 65%),repeating-conic-gradient(from 5deg at 50% 100%,transparent 0deg 6deg,#879c6033 7deg 8deg,transparent 9deg 14deg);transform:rotate(-12deg);box-shadow:0 0 0 25px #84955b0a,0 0 0 55px #84955b08;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swgrove::before {content:'';position:absolute;inset:75px;border:1px solid #c2b77666;border-radius:50%;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swgrove::after {content:'';position:absolute;left:235px;top:50px;bottom:35px;width:1px;background:#c3bd8277;transform:rotate(12deg);}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swbackline {font:italic 29px 'Bodoni Moda',serif;line-height:1.45;width:440px;margin-top:55px;color:#d1d7b9;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1).sw-back .swgrove {opacity:.5;top:470px;}
@media print {.page:is(.sw,#p1) {-webkit-print-color-adjust:exact;print-color-adjust:exact;}}

.page:is(.sw,#p1){column-count:1;}
.page.sw .columnWrapper{column-count:1;}
.page.sw-body .swbody{column-count:2;column-gap:27px;column-fill:balance;}
.page.sw .swbody{break-inside:auto;}
.page.sw .swbody p{font-variant:normal;}
.page.sw .swbody p::first-letter{font:inherit;float:none;margin:0;padding:0;}
.page.sw table{break-inside:auto!important;}
.page.sw tr{break-inside:avoid;}
.page.sw h2,.page.sw h3,.page.sw h4{break-after:avoid;}
.page.sw-wide .swbody{column-count:1;font-size:16px;line-height:1.43;}
.page.sw-wide table{font-size:13px;}
.page.sw-table .swbody{column-count:1;}
.page.sw-table table{font-size:12px;line-height:1.3;}
.page.sw-chapter h1{font-size:56px;}
.page.sw-chapter .swbody{padding-top:65px;}
.page.sw-chapter .sweyebrow{margin-top:40px;}
.page.sw-contents li{padding:17px 0;font-size:14px;}
.page.sw .swsubtitle p{font:400 17px/1.7 'Manrope',sans-serif;}


/* Production pagination: unchanged typefaces, palette, story and numerical rules. */
.page.sw .swbody{column-count:1!important;}
.page.sw .swcolumns{display:flex;gap:27px;align-items:flex-start;}
.page.sw .swcol{width:calc((100% - 27px)/2);min-width:0;flex:none;}
.page.sw .swblock{display:flow-root;margin:0;padding:0;}
.page.sw .swblock>h2:first-child,.page.sw .swblock>h3:first-child{margin-top:14px;}
.page.sw .swblock p:last-child{margin-bottom:9px;}
.page.sw .swportrait{margin:4px 0 16px;padding:0;break-inside:avoid;}
.page.sw .swsymbol{margin:5px 0 15px;}
.page.sw .swsymbol img{display:block;width:100%;height:180px;object-fit:contain;}
.page.sw .swportrait img{display:block;width:100%;height:250px;object-fit:contain;background:#e0e5d7;}
.page.sw figcaption{font:600 10px/1.35 'Manrope',sans-serif;color:#536952;margin-top:7px;}
.page.sw .monster{font-family:'Source Serif 4',serif;font-size:12px;line-height:1.3;background:#e2e7da;background-image:none;border:1px solid #8c9d7d;border-top:3px solid #65765a;box-shadow:none;padding:12px;margin:0 0 15px;width:auto;break-inside:avoid;}
.page.sw .monster:before,.page.sw .monster:after{display:none;}
.page.sw .monster h2{font-size:23px;margin:0 0 7px;}
.page.sw .monster h3{font-size:17px;margin:11px 0 6px;border-bottom:1px solid #a8b29c;}
.page.sw .monster p{font-size:12px;line-height:1.3;margin:0 0 7px;}
.page.sw .monster table{font-size:10px;line-height:1.2;}
.page.sw .monster td,.page.sw .monster th{padding:3px 2px;font-size:10px;}
.page.sw .monster hr{background:#9aa98a;border:0;height:1px;margin:7px 0;}
.page.sw .swpass{display:inline-block;width:31%;vertical-align:top;margin:12px 1% 30px;text-align:center;}
.page.sw .swpass img{width:100%;height:270px;object-fit:contain;}
.page.sw .swmaptitle{font-size:30px;margin:0 0 18px;}
.page.sw .swmapart{display:block;width:100%;height:820px;object-fit:contain;}
.page.sw .swsceneart{display:block;width:100%;height:680px;object-fit:contain;}
.page.sw.sw-image .swbody{column-count:1;font-size:14px;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swcoverart{position:absolute;left:57px;right:57px;top:420px;width:calc(100% - 114px);height:490px;object-fit:contain;}
.page#p1 .swcovermeta{bottom:60px;}
.page.sw .swtocgroup{columns:2;column-gap:35px;}
.page.sw.sw-contents li{font-size:12px;padding:8px 0;break-inside:avoid;}
.page.sw.sw-contents h1{font-size:52px;margin-bottom:25px;}
.page.sw .swwide table{font-size:12px;line-height:1.3;}
.page.sw .swblock table{table-layout:auto;}


/* Fixed manual columns: prevent renderer from pushing tall blocks sideways. */
.page:is(.sw,#p1){column-count:auto!important;column-width:auto!important;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .columnWrapper{column-count:auto!important;column-width:auto!important;height:auto!important;}
.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swbody{position:static!important;display:block!important;width:100%;}
.page#p1 .swblock{position:static!important;}

.page:is(.sw,#p1) .swcovermeta{width:auto!important;}

/* Scoped chapter integration; prior sections keep their established design. */
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper {width:210mm;height:296.8mm;padding:18mm 15.5mm 18mm;background:#edf0e6;background-image:none;color:#253d34;font:14.8px/1.39 'Source Serif 4',serif;column-count:1;box-sizing:border-box;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .columnWrapper{column-count:1!important;display:block;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper p,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper li{font-family:'Source Serif 4',serif;text-indent:0!important;line-height:inherit;margin-bottom:10px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper p:first-letter{font:inherit;float:none;margin:0;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h1,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h2,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h3{font-family:'Bodoni Moda',serif;text-transform:none;font-variant:normal;color:#183d30;border:0;line-height:1.1;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h1{font-size:37px;margin:8px 0 21px;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h2,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h3{font-size:27px;margin:16px 0 12px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h4,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h5{font:700 14px/1.35 'Manrope',sans-serif;color:#183d30;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-flow{display:block;column-count:1;width:100%;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper table{font:13.6px/1.35 'Source Serif 4',serif;border-collapse:collapse;width:100%;margin:10px 0 16px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper th{background:#183d30;color:#edf0e6;font:600 11.7px/1.3 'Manrope',sans-serif;padding:9px 10px;text-align:left;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper td{padding:8px 10px;vertical-align:top;border-bottom:1px solid #bdc6ae;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper tr:nth-child(odd){background:#dfe5d5;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .note,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .descriptive,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-note{background:#dce3d2;border:0;border-top:3px solid #a48d52;border-radius:0;box-shadow:none;padding:14px 16px;break-inside:avoid;font:14.1px/1.39 'Source Serif 4',serif;margin:10px 0 16px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .descriptive,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper blockquote{background:#e5ddc7;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper blockquote{border:0;border-left:2px solid #a48d52;padding:9px 12px;font-style:italic;margin:12px 0;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-eyebrow{font:600 10.7px/1.3 'Manrope',sans-serif;letter-spacing:1px;color:#536952;border-top:1px solid #a48d52;padding-top:10px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-small{font-size:12px;color:#536952;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper a{color:#183d30;text-decoration:underline;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-art{display:grid;grid-template-columns:195px 1fr;gap:12px;align-items:center;max-height:145px;margin:0 0 16px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-art img{height:136px;max-width:185px;object-fit:contain;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-cover img{width:100%;max-height:480px;object-fit:contain;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .footnote{position:absolute;bottom:8mm;left:15.5mm;right:25mm;font:600 9px 'Manrope',sans-serif;color:#536952;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .pageNumber{position:absolute;bottom:8mm;right:15.5mm;font:600 11px 'Manrope',sans-serif;color:#536952;}

/* Browser flow reset: use full page width without inherited columns. */
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper{columns:unset!important;column-width:auto!important;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .columnWrapper{columns:unset!important;column-width:auto!important;height:auto!important;display:block!important;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper p{margin:0 0 10px!important;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper ul,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper ol{margin:0 0 10px;padding-left:20px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper li{margin:0 0 5px!important;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h4,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h5{margin:10px 0 8px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-flow>p:empty{display:none;}

/* Match the review proof spacing while keeping readable body size. */
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h1{margin:0 0 16px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h2,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper h3{margin:10px 0 10px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper p{margin:0 0 8px!important;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper blockquote{padding:6px 12px;margin:10px 0;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper blockquote p:last-child,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .note p:last-child,.page.sw-pilot.sw-copper .swpilot-note p:last-child{margin-bottom:0!important;}

.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver .note,.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver .descriptive,.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver .swpilot-note{background:#dce3d2;border:0;border-top:3px solid #a48d52;border-radius:0;box-shadow:none;padding:14px 16px;break-inside:avoid;font:14.1px/1.39 'Source Serif 4',serif;margin:10px 0 16px;}
.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver .descriptive,.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver blockquote{background:#e5ddc7;}.page.sw-pilot.sw-silver blockquote{border:0;border-left:2px solid #a48d52;padding:9px 12px;font-style:italic;margin:12px 0;}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-HISTORY"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0000"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0001"></div>

*GM Only — Major Story Spoilers*

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0002"></div>

Everything the players may eventually uncover about the five founders, their friendships, the tragedy that divided them and the truth behind Lord Xanthos.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0003"></div>

{{note
##### GM ONLY — HISTORICAL SOURCES
This account draws upon Brother Brolin Ironhammer's ten diary entries and Saraphina's two letters, preserved in the separate Handout Pack.

These documents are discovered within the Founders' Tomb during the Platinum Chapter. **Do not give the diary or letters to players before their characters discover them in the adventure.**

The diary and letters are primary in-world evidence. Their wording and historical details must remain consistent with this account.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0004"></div>

## Before Silverwood

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0005"></div>

In **789 THK**, five adventurers travelled from Aldmere into the mountains to investigate the disappearance of villagers.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0006"></div>

They discovered the Cult of Shadows operating beneath a small forest settlement. Saraphina found the hidden entrance to the cult's lair, and together the companions destroyed it.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0007"></div>

Their victory left the underground passages badly damaged. When goblins later occupied the abandoned tunnels, the adventurers returned to drive them out.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0008"></div>

There, amongst the broken stone, they discovered veins of silver.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0009"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0010"></div>

The mountain held other mysteries too: ancient ruins, a sealed tower of unknown origin, an obelisk associated with forgotten dragon riders, and a great forest that had long since died.

The five adventurers had come to save a village.

They would stay to build a town.

}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0011"></div>

## The Five Founders

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0012"></div>

<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-032-vartius-xanthos.webp" alt="Vartius Xanthos"/><div><b>Vartius Xanthos (human wizard).</b> The leader of the five companions and their most gifted arcane mind. Intelligent, determined and confident in the power of his own magic, he trusted no god and believed that almost anything could be accomplished through knowledge and persistence. He worked alongside Lysander to restore the dead woodland, but his attention was repeatedly drawn towards the ancient sealed tower the founders had discovered. Where the others saw a mystery best left alone, Vartius saw knowledge waiting to be unlocked.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0013"></div>

<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-033-lysander.webp" alt="Lysander"/><div><b>Lysander</b> <i>(human druid).</i> Vartius&#x27;s partner. She brought the <b>dead woods back to life</b>, and with Vartius she enchanted them to feed the town. Her heart belonged to life, nature and abundance: the ways of <b>Sylvara</b>.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0014"></div>

<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-034-brother-brolin-ironhammer.webp" alt="Brother Brolin Ironhammer"/><div><b>Brother Brolin Ironhammer</b> <i>(dwarf priest of Orathen).</i> The oldest of the five. He believed every soul deserves its natural rest. He founded the first temple, and he kept the <b>diary</b> that tells this story.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0015"></div>

<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-035-elara.webp" alt="Elara"/><div><b>Elara</b> <i>(human ranger and brawler).</i> Bubbly, loud, the heart of every room, and quite happy to punch a hill giant in the face for a laugh. She founded the <b>Adventurers&#x27; Guild</b> and the <b>fighting pits</b>, which still run beneath Tormund&#x27;s shop today. Everyone loved her. She lived for the thrill of the hunt and the fight: the ways of <b>Kharrek</b>.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0016"></div>

<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-036-saraphina-canon.webp" alt="Saraphina"/><div><b>Saraphina</b> <i>(half-elf rogue).</i> The youngest of the five, and the string that tied everything together. She built the <b>trade links</b> to Aldmere (silver out, food in), kept the peace and handled the diplomacy. She believed in order: the ways of <b>Veyrath</b>. A businesswoman at heart. The people she truly loved were her friends in the party, and above all <b>Lysander</b>, though she never said so.</div></div>

}}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-FOUNDING"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0017"></div>

## The Founding of Silverwood

The discovery of silver transformed the settlement.

Merchants arrived from Aldmere, followed by miners, craftsmen and families hoping to make their fortunes. The village grew rapidly, and the companions who had saved it found themselves responsible for something far greater than defeating a few monsters.

**Saraphina** established the trade routes that kept the settlement supplied. Silver travelled out, while food, building materials and other necessities came in. Her diplomacy and commercial instincts helped the growing population avoid descending into disputes over wealth and opportunity.

**Elara** established the Adventurers' Guild. Goblins still occupied the surrounding hills, and dangerous creatures threatened the roads. The Guild offered protection, employment and a means for adventurers to earn their living. She also founded the fighting pits, where warriors could test their skills and entertain the townsfolk.

**Brother Brolin Ironhammer** built the first temple to Orathen. The oldest of the companions, he became a source of guidance and comfort to the people. He also began recording the settlement's history, never imagining how important his diary would eventually become.

**Lysander** turned her attention towards the dead woodland west of the settlement. Through years of cultivation and powerful druidic magic, she brought life back to the barren ground.

**Vartius Xanthos**, whose talents lay in arcane magic, worked alongside her. Together they established the enchanted obelisks that sustained the restored forest, allowing it to provide food for a growing town that otherwise had little farmland.

Yet Vartius was also fascinated by the ancient tower discovered during their earliest adventures. The structure was sealed by powerful wards, and a forgotten route through the caves beneath it offered the only known way inside. While the others devoted themselves to Silverwood, Vartius never entirely abandoned his desire to understand it.

Around **800 THK**, as the newly restored woodland flourished, Vartius and Lysander married.

They had fought together, travelled together and now helped build a home together. For a time, their happiness seemed as enduring as the forest they had restored.

Elara, too, had begun a family. Around **799 THK**, she welcomed her first child, the first of four.

Silverwood prospered. Houses rose along the mountainside, trade caravans arrived regularly and the mines brought new wealth into the settlement.

The five adventurers had become its founders.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-LOSS-LYSANDER"></div>

## The Loss of Lysander

By **809 THK**, Silverwood was thriving, but the companions' lives had begun to take different paths.

Saraphina had married Victor Valtair, a wealthy nobleman, and moved away. His position brought influence and opportunity, though the marriage lacked the love she had felt for her old companions, particularly Lysander.

Elara raised her children while continuing to oversee the Guild. Brolin remained devoted to his temple.

Vartius and Lysander continued their work within the enchanted forest.

Then Lysander became pregnant.

Vartius had never wanted a child. The disagreement strained their marriage, and Elara tried to make peace between them.

In **810–811 THK**, after six days of labour, Lysander died giving birth to their son, Eamon.

The child survived. His mother did not.

Overcome with grief, Vartius begged Brolin to restore her. But Orathen would not permit it. Brolin refused, and when Vartius attempted to force the matter, the priest had to restrain his old friend with magic.

Vartius's grief erupted in a surge of uncontrolled power, damaging the enchantments that protected the forest. It was an accident, but the decline of the woodland began that day.

Elara took Eamon into her home, raising him alongside her own children.

Vartius retreated into the ancient tower.

He could not accept that his wife was gone.

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-BLACK-PEARLS"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0020"></div>

## The Black Pearls

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0021"></div>

Saraphina had never stopped loving Lysander.

When news of her death reached her, the loss reopened feelings she had never dared express. She could not bear the thought that Lysander, who had restored life to an entire forest, should have her own life taken so cruelly.

In the years that followed, grief became determination.

Around **814–815 THK**, Saraphina persuaded Victor to make a bargain with a demon. The fiend offered them a necklace of black pearls, promising a power capable of raising the dead.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0022"></div>

But Saraphina did not fully understand what she had obtained.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 6}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

The bargain marked her bloodline. Her children were born tieflings, and only Victor's blood descendants could wield the pearls.

What Saraphina had imagined as a means of restoring her friend now depended upon her own children.

She waited until they were old enough to travel, nurturing the belief that they might succeed where everyone else had failed.

Around **825 THK**, she returned to Silverwood with Victor and their children, now approximately nine or ten years old.

Her intention was to take them to the founders' crypt and have them use the pearls to bring Lysander back.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0023"></div>

Saraphina knew there were dangers. She did not understand their full extent. The pearls could raise the dead, but not restore true life. Their magic returned the dead as undead and consumed the wielder's vitality. The longer someone had been dead, the greater the cost.

Bringing back Lysander after so many years could kill the child attempting it.

Whether Saraphina would have stopped had she understood that price is a question her actions never answered.

When the family reached the crypt, they discovered that Lysander's body was missing.

Saraphina confronted Vartius, demanding to know what had become of her friend. He mocked her bargain and what she had done to her own family. In secret, he altered one of the pearls, turning it into a trap tied to the founders' tomb.

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0024"></div>

Humiliated and horrified, Saraphina left without the answers she had sought.

The following year, she confessed the bargain to Brolin. He condemned what she had done, and they parted in anger.

The friendship that had founded Silverwood was breaking apart.

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-LONG-DECEPTION"></div>

## The Long Deception

Unknown to the others, Vartius had spent the years since Lysander's death pursuing a terrible ambition.

Within the tower, he discovered magic capable of transferring souls between bodies. His experiments were often disastrous, but he continued.

He even found Lysander's soul and brought it into a golem, only to discover that he could not truly restore her to life.

In **829 THK**, Vartius showed his son Eamon the soul-transfer machine he had created.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 7}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

Horrified by his father's experiments, Eamon tried to destroy it.

The machine activated, exchanging their souls.

Vartius found himself inside Eamon's body. His son was trapped inside Vartius's ageing one, which was failing.

Eamon died in his father's arms, looking up at him through his father's own face.

Vartius tried to save him, but failed.

To the people of Silverwood, the great wizard had died and his son had inherited the title of Lord Xanthos.

Only Vartius knew the truth.

Brolin buried the body of his old friend, never realising whose soul it contained.

In the years that followed, Vartius became increasingly distant. Saraphina remained estranged from Brolin, though they shared one final, difficult conversation following the supposed death of Vartius.

As Brolin approached the end of his life, he finally forgave her. Around **839 THK**, he sent a letter inviting her to return for one last supper.

Saraphina arrived two days after his death.

She never learned that he had forgiven her.

She buried him, placed two letters within his diary and left the founders' remaining equipment for those who might one day discover the truth.

She never returned to Silverwood.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 8}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Secret History of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-SEVEN-HUNDRED"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0026"></div>

## Seven Hundred Years

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0025"></div>

The generations that followed knew Lord Xanthos as the descendant of the great wizard who had founded Silverwood.

In reality, Vartius continued to live through the bodies of others.

His experiments taught him that blood relatives made better vessels for his magic. Over the centuries, he manipulated his descendants, pursuing increasingly disturbing means of preserving and transferring his soul.

Yet he was not merely a figure of cruelty.

The Lords Xanthos who defeated dragons, fought terrible enemies and protected the region were all Vartius. His heroism was real, even as his secret crimes grew more dreadful.

Every generation believed a new member of the family had inherited the title.

There had only ever been one.

And every transfer cost Vartius something he could not recover: fragments of his own memory.

The man who had once been desperate to save his wife slowly forgot the ordinary details of the life they had shared.

Eventually, he even forgot that Lysander's soul remained imprisoned within his tower.

By **1494 THK**, more than seven centuries after the founders first entered the mountains, Silverwood was prospering again.

The discovery of mythril had brought a new mining boom, fresh wealth and new dangers.

And beneath the peace of the mountain town, the consequences of its founding tragedy remained waiting to be uncovered.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-SOUL-TRANSFER"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c01b0027"></div>

## Soul-transfer rules

**Living-body exchange.** A soul can be exchanged only with another living body at the same moment. An empty body deteriorates quickly. A soul trapped in Vartius's dying former body dies with it.

**Golems are one-way.** Vartius cannot use his methods to return a soul placed in a golem to a living body.

**Bloodline compatibility.** Transfers into blood relatives work more efficiently than transfers into unrelated people. Vartius's later experimentation and breeding programme exploit that discovery.

**Memory loss.** Each transfer gradually erodes Vartius's memories.

**The heroism is genuine.** The deeds attributed to generations of Lords Xanthos were performed by Vartius himself.

}}

# The gods of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-PANTHEON"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0001"></div>


## The eight principal gods

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0002"></div>


### How Divinity Works

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0003"></div>


Four great forces shape all existence: **Order, Chaos, Creation and Entropy**. They overlap, and none of them is good or evil.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0004"></div>


The forces came first. Gods came after, born from **mortal belief**. As people live, wonder and pray, their faith gathers in the overlap between forces that fits it, and a god takes shape there.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0005"></div>


- **Worship shapes gods.** The more a god is believed in, the stronger it grows, and what its followers believe changes what it becomes.
- **Different peoples may call the same god by different names.** If their beliefs drift far enough apart, a god can change, or even split in two.
- **Forgotten gods fade.**
- **Balance** lies at the centre of all four forces. The **Void** lies outside them all.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0006"></div>


The eight gods below are the **principal gods known in Silverwood**. They aren't the only gods, and none of them is simply good or evil.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0007"></div>


## Orathen

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0008"></div>


### Keeper of the Turning Scales

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0009"></div>


**Sphere:** Balance, and the rightful cycle of life and death.
**Place:** The exact centre of all four forces. The only god who holds them all equally.
**Symbol:** Balanced scales growing from a single stem. One pan bears green leaves, the other bare roots.
**Nature:** Watchful, calm and firm. Compassionate in grief, unyielding about the natural order. *"I grieve with you, but grief does not justify breaking the order of the world."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0010"></div>


**Teaching:** *What begins must end; what ends allows another beginning.* Life and death are both sacred, and there is no life without death.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0011"></div>


**Followers' rules:**
1. Do not kill without just cause.
2. Do not interrupt the rightful passage of the dead.
3. Destroy undeath and the corruption of souls.
4. Protect life from those who prey upon it.
5. Love does not excuse defying the natural order.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0012"></div>


**Followers:** Gravekeepers, midwives and those who sit with the dying, comforters of mourners and hunters of the undead. The strictest may refuse to cure a natural illness, but will fight a plague someone unleashed on purpose. Healers often disagree with them, sharply.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0013"></div>


**In Silverwood:** **Brother Brolin's god**, and the founder of the Hallowed Halls. When Lysander died, Orathen told Brolin: *"There is no evil doing here. This is the natural flow of her existence."*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 14}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The gods of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-PANTHEON"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0014"></div>


## Aurelune

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0015"></div>


### The First Lantern

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0016"></div>


**Sphere:** Light, warmth, hope, healing and shelter from despair.
**Place:** Order and Creation, with a little Chaos.
**Symbol:** A lantern whose flame forms a rising sun.
**Nature:** Patient, reassuring and protective.
**Teaching:** *Where suffering can be eased, carry the light there.* Darkness isn't evil; the duty is to give people warmth and a way forward.
**Followers:** Healers, midwives, keepers of refuges and temple helpers. They tend the sick and keep lamps burning for anyone seeking safety.
**In Silverwood:** **Brother Alexander's god.**

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0017"></div>


## Veyrath

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0018"></div>


### Witness of the Given Word

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0019"></div>


**Sphere:** Oaths, loyalty, honour, law, contracts and order.
**Place:** Strongly Order, with some Creation.
**Symbol:** Two hands clasped over an unbroken ring.
**Nature:** Formal and exacting, but not blind to nuance.
**Teaching:** *Speak carefully; once you give your word, keep it.* A follower may bend how an oath is read, but never simply break it, even an unjust one. Veyrath is the god of promises, **not** of justice.
**Followers:** Magistrates, guards, sworn companions, guild officers and merchants. Some find merciful loopholes; others become rigid.
**In Silverwood:** Close to **Saraphina's** belief in order and keeping things running.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0020"></div>


## Sylvara

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0021"></div>


### The Green Mother

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0022"></div>


**Sphere:** Nature, fertility, growth, crops and abundance.
**Place:** Creation and Chaos, with some Entropy (rot, compost, the hunt, the turn of the seasons).
**Symbol:** An open seedpod holding a young tree.
**Nature:** Generous, fertile and untamed. She loves thorns and hunger as much as flowers and harvest.
**Teaching:** *Give life room to grow. Take what you need; return what you can.*
**Followers:** Druids, farmers, herbalists and foresters.
**In Silverwood:** Close to **Lysander's** love of life and abundance, and her revival of the dead woods.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 15}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The gods of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-PANTHEON"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0023"></div>


## Kharrek

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0024"></div>


### Lord of the Living Chase

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0025"></div>


**Sphere:** The hunt, courage, risk, contest, adventure and the thrill of the fight.
**Place:** Chaos and Creation, touched by Entropy at the moment of the kill.
**Symbol:** A running stag's antlers crossed by a spear.
**Nature:** Bold, laughing and dangerous. He respects a worthy contest more than an easy win.
**Teaching:** *Test yourself fiercely, and know the difference between a worthy challenge and needless slaughter.*
**Followers:** Hunters, rangers, explorers and fighters. Before a hunt, they tell the story of their last narrow escape.
**In Silverwood:** Close to **Elara's** love of the hunt, the fight and adventure.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0026"></div>


## Isthara

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0027"></div>


### The Spellwright

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0028"></div>


**Sphere:** Magic, discovery and shaping what's possible.
**Place:** Order and Creation, reaching into Chaos wherever invention breaks the rules.
**Symbol:** An open hand holding a star inside a geometric circle.
**Nature:** Curious, inventive and exacting. Prizes learning over obedience.
**Teaching:** *Discover, understand, then create.* Magic is older than Isthara; worshipping her isn't needed to use it.
**Followers:** Scholars, spellcasters, artificers and researchers.
**In Silverwood:** **Vartius** never worshipped her. He believed only in the power of his own hands.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0029"></div>


## Meravelle

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0030"></div>


### The Open Cup

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0031"></div>


**Sphere:** Love, friendship, hearth and home, music, feasting and celebration.
**Place:** Creation and Chaos, with a little Order where love builds families and friendships.
**Symbol:** Two cups raised together before a small hearth-flame.
**Nature:** Warm, exuberant and sometimes impulsive.
**Teaching:** *Life is not merely to be endured. Share it.*
**Followers:** Families, innkeepers, brewers, performers, lovers and friends. Their prayers are meals, songs, weddings and a cup poured for absent friends.
**In Silverwood:** The everyday god of the **Shaft and Pickaxe** and ordinary townsfolk.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 16}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The gods of Silverwood

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-PANTHEON"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0032"></div>


## Nhal

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0033"></div>


### The Unspoken

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0034"></div>


**Sphere:** The Void: absence, silence and the empty space in which everything else exists.
**Place:** Outside all four forces. Not Entropy, and not evil.
**Symbol:** A plain dark ring with an empty centre.
**Nature:** Quiet, remote, neither kind nor cruel.
**Teaching:** *Not everything needs to be filled.* The Void isn't hungry. It's simply what isn't there.
**Followers:** Contemplatives, philosophers, wanderers and some stargazers. Their shrines are bare spaces, and their prayers can be a moment of silence.

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0035"></div>


## Brolin's Prayer

<div class="swanchor" id="c02b0037"></div>


> *O Orathen, Keeper of the Scales,*
> *Watcher of the first breath and the last,*
> *Guide our souls through life's long trial,*
> *And lead us gently into rest.*
>
> *In your wisdom, life is cherished.*
> *In your judgement, death is not to be feared.*
> *Teach us to honour each rightful season,*
> *And to walk the path set before us without corruption or despair.*
>
> *Protect us from those who steal life before its hour.*
> *Shield us from those who would chain the soul beyond its proper end.*
> *Grant us strength to keep the balance,*
> *Mercy to comfort the grieving,*
> *And courage to stand against all that profanes the natural order.*
>
> *Orathen, Keeper of the Turning Scales,*
> *Guide us in life,*
> *Receive us in death,*
> *And keep the great cycle unbroken.*

}}

# Silverwood and its lands

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-OVERVIEW"></div>



<figure class="swpilot-scene"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/silverwood-l1.webp" alt="Silverwood — accepted ground level"/></figure>

Full town plans: [L0 · p. 47](#silverwood-atlas-l0) · [L1 · p. 48](#silverwood-atlas-l1) · [L2 · p. 49](#silverwood-atlas-l2) · [L3 · p. 50](#silverwood-atlas-l3) · [L4 · p. 51](#silverwood-atlas-l4).


<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0001"></div>


## Where the players can go, and when

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0002"></div>


*Silverwood is an open world. From the Copper Chapter onwards, players can go almost anywhere. The world doesn't scale to them: some places are safe, some are deadly, and some are locked until the story opens them.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0003"></div>


### Silverwood: the Story So Far

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0004"></div>


The small mining town of Silverwood was once a bustling hub of activity, but as the silver veins that sustained it ran dry, the population dwindled and the town fell into disrepair. For years it was little more than a forgotten relic of a bygone era, until a recent discovery sparked new life into the area.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0005"></div>


Deep within one of the caves that had long been mined for silver, a group of miners stumbled on a massive deposit of **mythril**. News spread like wildfire, drawing people from all across the realm to Silverwood in hopes of striking it rich.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0006"></div>


As the population swelled, the Lord of the area, **Lord Xanthos**, a descendant of the man who founded the town, enforced a law set down by his ancestor: a **10% fee** on all mythril taken from the mines. Despite the tax, the miners kept coming, and soon the once-sleepy town was bustling again.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0007"></div>


But with so many people in one place, the area's resources were stretched thin. The local forests were enchanted, making hunting difficult and dangerous, and there was no land nearby fit for farming. The sudden influx caused a **food crisis** that threatened to end the boom before it had really begun. So Lord Xanthos turned to the arcane arts. He created a new kind of fast-growing food in his own arcane-powered greenhouse, **Greenhart**, and shared it with the people of Silverwood.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 18}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Silverwood and its lands

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-OVERVIEW"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0008"></div>


The dangers weren't limited to the enchanted forests. There are rumours of other creatures lurking in the shadows, preying on miners and travellers. And then there's the **goblin town** to the east, always a thorn in the side of the local authorities. With so much wealth at stake, tensions are running high, and the potential for conflict is ever-present.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0009"></div>


{{note
##### Warn, don't block
When players head somewhere too dangerous, let the town warn them: Faldor's grunt, Sariel's raised eyebrow, a miner's story, the Guild pass rank on a job. Later, as they grow stronger, let the same people say: *"You're getting stronger. You could probably hold your own out there now."*
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0010"></div>


### The Calendar

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0011"></div>


Long before Silverwood was founded, the warring regions were joined under one crown by **the High King**, and the years have been counted from that day ever since. Dates are written **THK**, *"in the reign of the High King's peace"*. The campaign begins in **1494 THK**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0012"></div>


The year has twelve months:

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0016"></div>


## The Town of Silverwood

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0017"></div>


Silverwood is built **up**, not out. The town climbs the rock in **four tiers**, joined by stairs, walkways and bridges that cross over and under each other. The big buildings (the **Guild**, the **Hallowed Halls** and **Tormund's**) are several tiers tall. Everything else is stacked: shops on top of homes, homes on top of shops.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0018"></div>


**The walls.** Gatehouses stand at the **north**, **south** and **west**. The **east** side has no gate: it's a sheer wall of rock.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 20}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Silverwood and its lands

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-OVERVIEW"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0019"></div>


| Tier | What's here |
|:--|:--|
| **The Undercity** | The **sewers**, the old **cisterns** and water channels, and the **fighting pits** beneath Tormund's. Big rats, worse things, and people who don't want to be found. |
| **Lower tier** | The three **gatehouses**, storage, the **Miners' Mart**, the guard **barracks**, the **Shaft and Pickaxe's kitchens**, the **gambling and meeting halls**, the first floor of **Tormund's Bizarre Bazaar** and of the **Hallowed Halls**. Stairs and walkways everywhere. |
| **Middle tier** | Bridges and walkways criss-cross this level. The **Shaft and Pickaxe** itself, where the bard plays, the Guild meets and the **job board** hangs. **Mirella's Mystic Emporium**, **Ampersand's Apothecary**, the second floor of **Tormund's** and of the **Hallowed Halls**. |
| **Upper tier** | Houses and more houses. The **Guild's lodgings** and meeting rooms, the private rooms at the top of the Guild, the upper floors of **Mirella's** and **Ampersand's**, and the top of the **Hallowed Halls**. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0020"></div>


{{note
##### The sewers
The Undercity is where low-rank parties earn their first coin killing **giant rats** and **swarms of rats**, and where they find the fighting pits. The cisterns and water channels run under the whole town, and anyone who knows them can get from one tier to another unseen.
}}

| **The Labyrinth** | Beyond the Feywoods | Platinum | — | An endless dungeon (optional) |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0023"></div>


## Always Available

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0024"></div>


From the Copper Chapter onwards, alongside the main story:

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0025"></div>


- **Herb gathering** for **Mirella** and **Ampersand**.
- **Material gathering** for **Ironclad's** and **Tormund**.
- **Creature capture** for **Tormund**.
- **Dice games** in the gambling hall below the Shaft and Pickaxe.
- **The fighting pits** beneath Tormund's Bizarre Bazaar (Elara's old pits), once Tormund trusts them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0026"></div>


These are where groups using *Arcane Forge*, *Genesis Eggs* and *Ampersand's Apothecaria* will spend much of their downtime. **Each of those books uses its own difficulty rules;** use them as written, alongside the Guild's ranks. Remember: side jobs pay in **coin, contacts and gear, but little experience** (see the Copper Chapter).

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 23}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Silverwood and its lands

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-OVERVIEW"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0027"></div>


## The Lands Around Silverwood

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0028"></div>


Silverwood sits where very different lands meet. That makes it a good base for anyone gathering herbs and materials.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0029"></div>


| Land | Where | What grows there |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Silverwood forest** | Around the town and village | Common herbs, **mandrake root** at the forest edge, **healing fungi** on the forest floor |
| **Rivers and swamps** | Running down from the north; around the village | **Water** plants |
| **Swamps and wild forest** | Around the village and the Obelisk | **Poison** plants |
| **The mines and caves** | South, under the mountain | **Earth** plants |
| **The crypt and graveyards** | North and north-west | **Necrotic** plants |
| **The Feywoods** | West and south-west | **Psychic** plants, including **Silver Dream Thistle** |
| **The High Shelves** | The plateaus below the peaks | **Lightning**, **air** and **radiant** plants |
| **The Frostfang Peaks** | North, between the crypt and the tower | **Cold** plants |
| **The Cinderreach** | South-south-west | **Fire** plants, and **infernal** plants by the deepest vents |
| **Where Azazel fell** | The battlefield *(after the Gold Chapter)* | **Infernal** plants |
| **Around the Xanthos Tower** | The mountain | **Arcane** plants |
| **The Labyrinth and the deep tunnels** | Beyond the Feywoods; below the mines | **Eldritch** plants |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0030"></div>


The plant types are those of *Ampersand's Apothecaria*. The Guild's gathering jobs in each chapter name the plants they want.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 24}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# Guild rules and services

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-GUILD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0019"></div>


### Joining the Guild

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0020"></div>


New arrivals register with Sariel and receive a **Guild Pass**: a small metal badge in the metal of their rank (Copper, Silver, Gold, Platinum, Mithril). The face is stamped with the holder's **name**, **race**, **skilled class**, **rank** and **abilities**, and it's signed by **Guildmaster Faldor Ironhewer** and the **Administrative Clerk**. Each new pass is cast in the next metal, and the old one goes back to Sariel.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0021"></div>


The terms of the pass are read out at registration (see *Guild Pass Terms* in the Handout Pack).

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0022"></div>


Every pass carries two enchantments, explained to new members as a safety measure:

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0023"></div>


- **It can be traced.** The Guild can locate any pass, and therefore its owner.
- **It knows if its owner dies.** If a member falls on a job, the Guild is alerted and sends a team to **recover the remains**. It's in the terms and conditions.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0024"></div>


{{note
##### Why this matters
The remains-recovery rule is the hook for the **Remains Retrieval** job in the Silver Chapter. Make sure players hear about it on day one.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0025"></div>


### Guild Ranks

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0026"></div>


| Pass | Character levels | Roughly opens up |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Copper** | 3–4 | Town jobs, goblins, the mining village |
| **Silver** | 5–6 | The mine investigation, the crypt, kobolds, the banshee |
| **Gold** | 7–8 | The blockade, the greenhouse, the war |
| **Platinum** | 9–10 | The tower, the Feywoods, the founders' trail |
| **Mithril** | 11+ | The final confrontation |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 26}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Guild rules and services

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-GUILD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0027"></div>


### Levelling Up

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0028"></div>


The **main story** sets the pace. Characters level up at these moments, whatever else they've been doing:

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0029"></div>


| Level | Reached when |
|:--:|:--|
| 3 | The party registers at the Guild (Copper introduction) |
| 4 | The third Scratch and Sniff job is done, or the trouble in the mines reaches *quiet rescues* (Copper) |
| 5 | The Mining Council votes (end of Copper) |
| 6 | The Penhallow clerk names Selene, or Lilith's story is resolved, whichever comes first (Silver) |
| 7 | Andras strikes in the tavern (end of Silver) |
| 8 | The greenhouse is destroyed (Gold) |
| 9 | The Battle of Silverwood is won (end of Gold) |
| 10 | Both Feywoods obelisks are healed or destroyed (Platinum) |
| 11 | The party leaves the tower after finding Lysander (end of Platinum) |

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0030"></div>


**If your table prefers experience points:** award main-story fights in full and wandering monsters at half. Side jobs pay coin, contacts and gear, not experience. At each moment above, top the party up to the level listed, so nobody falls behind for taking the scenic route.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0031"></div>


**Ranks warn, they don't block.** A Copper adventurer can walk into the Feywoods if they insist. They just won't get the Guild's backing, its pay or its help recovering their body.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0032"></div>


**Ranking up.** When a member reaches the levels for the next pass, Faldor doesn't simply hand it over. He sets them one task first, usually a job of their current rank with three or more stars. *"You're not strong enough yet. Prove me wrong."*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 27}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Guild rules and services

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-GUILD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0033"></div>


### The Job Board

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0034"></div>


Every job is posted with a **pass rank** and a **star rating** for danger, for example *Silver Pass, ★★★*.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0035"></div>


**Standard pay,** per character, before the Guild's 10% cut:

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0036"></div>


| Pass | ★ | ★★ | ★★★ |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| Copper | 10 gp | 25 gp | 50 gp |
| Silver | 50 gp | 100 gp | 200 gp |
| Gold | 200 gp | 400 gp | 750 gp |
| Platinum | 500 gp | 1,000 gp | 2,000 gp |
| Mithril | 1,500 gp | 3,000 gp | 5,000 gp |

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0037"></div>


Bounties (goblin ears, wanted posters) are paid on top. **Scratch and Sniff cover jobs** pay the standard rate for their rank and stars.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0038"></div>


Types of job: hunting, gathering, missing persons, wanted posters, remains retrieval, exorcisms, urgent deliveries, monster harvesting and household odd jobs.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0039"></div>


Not every job leads to the main story. A party can spend several sessions doing local work and that's fine. Two posters, though, matter more than they look: the **wanted notices** that tie into the Blackwood and Valtair stories.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 28}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Guild rules and services

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-GUILD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0040"></div>


### Guild Services

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0041"></div>


| Service | Cost |
|:--|:--|
| Guild cut of job rewards | 10% |
| Training (a new tool, language or weapon) | 25 gp per week |
| Storage | 1 sp per chest per day |
| Gear rental | A tenth of the item's price per day |
| Kethra's library | 1 gp per day; secrets cost more |
| Information from the Guild | 5 gp, or a favour |
| Identifying an item | 20 gp |
| Lodging (members) | Squalor: free · Modest: 2 sp a night · Comfortable: 5 sp a night · A friend staying over: 5 gp |

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0042"></div>


*For healing, see the Hallowed Halls.*

{{swpilot-note
##### PERMANENT REFERENCE
Registration is in Copper, once. Pass ranks warn about challenge; they do not make physical barriers. Keep story advancement and credential requirements distinct.
}}

# Gathering, hunting and knowledge

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-REFERENCE"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0031"></div>


### Gathering Jobs

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0032"></div>


**With *Ampersand's Apothecaria*,** use its discovery and harvesting rules. The plant's rarity sets how hard it is to find.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0033"></div>


**Without it,** a gathering job is a normal quest. Each hour of searching the right land, one character makes a **Wisdom (Survival)** or **Intelligence (Nature)** check:

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0034"></div>


| Rarity | DC to find | Pay per handful (good / excellent) |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| Common | 10 | 5 gp / 10 gp |
| Uncommon | 13 | 40 gp / 60 gp |
| Rare | 15 | 150 gp / 250 gp |
| Ultra Rare | 18 | 500 gp / 800 gp |
| Legendary | Story only | Not for sale |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0035"></div>


Beat the DC by 5 or more, and the handful is **excellent**. Fail by 5 or more, and the plant fights back: the harvester takes **1d6 damage** of the plant's type (fire, cold, poison and so on). Mirella's **herb books** give advantage on these checks. Everyone who posts a gathering job also pays a little extra for anything else interesting the party brings back.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0036"></div>


**Cold and heat.** In the **Frostfang Peaks** without warm clothing, or the **Cinderreach** without water and shade, each character makes a **DC 10 Constitution saving throw** at the end of every hour. On a failure, they gain one level of **exhaustion**. Cold-weather gear or a supply of water (as appropriate) gives advantage on the save. Creatures resistant to the damage type (cold or fire) pass automatically.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 30}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Gathering, hunting and knowledge

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-REFERENCE"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0037"></div>


### Hunting and Material Jobs

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0038"></div>


The town guard posts **pest control**, Tormund posts **hunts** and **captures**, and Ironclad's posts **material** jobs. Each one pays as its poster says.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0039"></div>


**With *Arcane Forge*,** harvest parts using its rules. The creature's challenge rating sets the material tier:

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0040"></div>


| Creature CR | Material tier | Harvest DC |
|:--|:--|:--:|
| 0–2 | Common | 10 |
| 3–6 | Uncommon | 15 |
| 7–12 | Rare | 20 |
| 13–16 | Very Rare | 23 |
| 17+ | Legendary | 25 |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0041"></div>


**Without it,** the party brings back the part named on the poster. A careful kill keeps it intact. Damaged parts are worth half.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0042"></div>


**Live captures** need the creature unconscious or restrained, then caged. Tormund sells nets, cages and sedatives.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0043"></div>


## What People Know

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0044"></div>


When a character tries to recall something about the region, use the table for the skill they're using. A character learns everything at and below their result.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0045"></div>


**History**

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0046"></div>


| DC | What they know |
|:--|:--|
| 10 | Silverwood was founded over 700 years ago by **five adventurers**. The Xanthos family have ruled it ever since, and take a **10% fee** on everything mined. |
| 15 | The founders were a wizard, a druid, a priest, a ranger and a rogue. The priest, **Brother Brolin**, built the first temple. The ranger, **Elara**, founded the Guild. |
| 20 | Every Lord Xanthos in the town's history looks very much like the last one. Nobody remembers seeing one as a child. |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 31}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Gathering, hunting and knowledge

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="GM-REFERENCE"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0047"></div>


**Arcana**

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0048"></div>


| DC | What they know |
|:--|:--|
| 10 | The tower on the mountain is far older than the town. Nobody knows who built it. |
| 15 | The tower is sealed by wards nobody has ever broken. The **Greenhart** greenhouse runs on arcane power, and the food it grows is not natural. |
| 20 | Obelisks of white stone once kept the Feywoods healthy. Some of them have broken, and the forest is rotting around them. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0049"></div>


**Religion**

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0050"></div>


| DC | What they know |
|:--|:--|
| 10 | The Hallowed Halls began as a temple to **Orathen**, Keeper of the Turning Scales, who guards the passage of souls. |
| 15 | Brother Brolin kept a **diary**. It was buried with him in the founders' crypt. |
| 20 | Orathen's faithful believe that holding a soul back from its rest is the worst crime there is. Something in Silverwood makes the old priests uneasy. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0051"></div>


**Nature**

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0052"></div>


| DC | What they know |
|:--|:--|
| 10 | The Feywoods were dead before the founders came. A druid brought them back to life. |
| 15 | Fey creatures live in the Feywoods, and they **don't** like visitors. Manticores hunt the forest's edges. |
| 20 | The Feywoods are sick. The trees are rotting from the inside, and the rot is spreading. |

}}
