# Tormund’s Bizarre Bazaar

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-TORMUND"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / ONGOING PORTRAYAL
First arrival once, at any rank. Repeat visits use freeform portrayal and the current chapter’s actual changes; event speeches wait for their prerequisites.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0001"></div>


## Oddities, monster parts and what lies beneath

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0002"></div>


*Two storeys of clutter stacked against the cliff. Antlers, jars of eyes, cages that rattle, scales the size of dinner plates. A man in a stained leather apron weighs a severed claw in one hand and a coin purse in the other, and you get the feeling he's judging which is worth more.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0003"></div>


### Layout

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0004"></div>


| Floor | What's here |
|:--|:--|
| **Ground** | The workshop: carving tables, hooks, cages and the smell of fresh monster. Deliveries come in here. |
| **Middle** | The Bazaar proper: oddities, curiosities and monster parts for sale. |
| **Below** | *Hidden.* The wagering den and fight pits. See *The Pits*. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0005"></div>


### Tormund

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0006"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-013-tormund.webp" alt="Tormund"/><div><i>Human, monster carver, creature trainer and oddities merchant.</i> A muscular man with a shaved head and a bushy beard. He also runs the Guild&#x27;s armoury, sharpening swords and checking that adventurers are properly equipped. His laugh and energy draw people to him. His very presence is that of a lovable alpha male, and he has set many an imagination wondering what it would be like to be held by him.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0007"></div>


He buys almost anything that used to be alive, sells almost anything strange, and knows exactly what both are worth.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0008"></div>


**How to play him.** Friendly, loud and always doing business. He respects anyone who brings him something interesting, and he remembers who brought it.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0009"></div>


**What he wants.** Rare creatures, rare parts and a good bet.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 72}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Tormund’s Bizarre Bazaar

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-TORMUND"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0010"></div>


### What He Offers

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0011"></div>


| Service | Notes |
|:--|:--|
| **Buys monster parts** | Fair prices, and **special prices** for parts he currently has a buyer for. |
| **Sells oddities** | Curiosities, trophies, strange trinkets and harvested ingredients. |
| **Appraisal** | Tells you what your trophy is and what it's worth. |
| **Capture help** | Nets, cages, sedatives and advice for bringing creatures back **alive**. |
| **Creature training** | Basic handling and taming for ordinary beasts. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0012"></div>


| Service | Price |
|:--|:--|
| Buying monster parts | **10 gp × the creature's CR** for a good trophy; double for parts he has a buyer for |
| Appraisal | 5 gp |
| Net · cage (Medium) · sedative dose | 1 gp · 10 gp · 10 gp |
| Creature training | 10 gp per week |
| **Live capture** (he pays you) | **25 gp × the creature's CR** |
| **Pit fights** | Entry 5 gp. Purse: 50 gp at Copper rank, 100 gp at Silver, 250 gp at Gold. Bets pay even money. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0013"></div>


**His oddities,** by the party's Guild pass:

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 73}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Tormund’s Bizarre Bazaar

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-TORMUND"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0014"></div>


| Pass | Items |
|:--|:--|
| Copper | Old pocket watch (10 gp), antique spectacles (15 gp), fortune-telling cards (15 gp), strange vial of glowing liquid (20 gp), magic kaleidoscope (20 gp), climbing gear (25 gp) |
| Silver | Jar of preserved eyeballs (50 gp), vintage porcelain doll (75 gp), oddly shaped glass bottle (100 gp), showman's cloak, which flaps dramatically in no wind (150 gp), deck of illusions (300 gp), immovable rod (500 gp) |
| Gold | Taxidermy fox with two tails (250 gp), unusual music box (300 gp), crystal orb with swirling mist inside (350 gp), ring of mind shielding (2,500 gp), portable hole (4,000 gp) |
| Platinum | Mummified hand of a powerful sorcerer (1,000 gp), enchanted hourglass (1,500 gp), cursed monkey's paw (2,000 gp) |

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0015"></div>


**Monster parts he's always after:** troll blood (50 gp a vial), owlbear feathers (25 gp for ten), giant spider venom (100 gp a vial), yeti fur (200 gp a pelt), minotaur horn (500 gp), chimera hide (1,000 gp).

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0016"></div>


*With Arcane Forge, use that book's prices for harvested materials instead.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0017"></div>


{{note
##### Using Arcane Forge and Genesis Eggs
- **Arcane Forge:** Tormund is a natural buyer and seller of harvested materials. Use that book's harvesting rules and material tiers.
- **Genesis Eggs:** Tormund's ordinary taming is **not** the same as a Genesis bond. Captured wild animals are not Genesis Creatures.
- Without either book, Tormund is simply a shop, a source of hunting jobs and the way into the pits.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0018"></div>


### The Pits

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0019"></div>


Beneath the Bazaar lies a secret: a **wagering den and fight pits.** Tormund only shows it to people he trusts.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0020"></div>


**Unlocking it.** Players earn Tormund's trust by trading with him and taking his hunting or capture jobs. His jobs are posted in every chapter. Once he likes them, he takes them downstairs.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0021"></div>


**What happens there:**
- **Creature fights:** exotic beasts set against each other. Players can bet on the outcome.
- **Pit fights:** players can enter the ring themselves, for coin and reputation.
- **Live capture jobs:** Tormund pays well for new fighters, brought back alive.
- **Training:** a safe(ish) place to practise against real creatures.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 74}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Tormund’s Bizarre Bazaar

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-TORMUND"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0022"></div>


{{note
##### Running the Pits
The pits are entirely optional. Many groups will never find them, and that's fine. Don't force every party to become gamblers or creature handlers. For groups that love them, they're a whole side campaign.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0023"></div>


### The Box of Eggs

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0024"></div>


Somewhere in the clutter sits a **bag of holding** that Tormund has never got round to emptying. Inside is a carved wooden box holding what look like ordinary goose eggs.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0025"></div>


Tormund thinks they're worthless. He'll throw them in with a purchase, or sell the lot for a few coppers.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0026"></div>


They are not goose eggs.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0027"></div>


{{note
##### Genesis Eggs
This is one way into the *Genesis Eggs* book. Without the book, the eggs are simply a mystery for the GM to use or leave alone.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0028"></div>


### Jobs and Hooks

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0029"></div>


- **Hunting jobs:** specific parts from specific creatures, often for a buyer he won't name.
- **Capture jobs:** bring it back alive, unharmed and preferably not angry.
- **Appraisals** of strange finds often reveal where something came from, which can be a clue.

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0030"></div>


### Rumours About Tormund (d4)

<div class="swanchor" id="c07b0031"></div>


| d4 | Rumour |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | "He'll buy anything dead. *Anything.* Don't ask what he did with the last thing I sold him." |
| 2 | "There's cheering under his shop some nights. Under the street." |
| 3 | "He used to be an adventurer. Lost two fingers to something he then ate." |
| 4 | "If you want something dangerous brought into town alive, he's your man." |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 75}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# Ironclad’s

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-IRONCLAD"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / ONGOING PORTRAYAL
First arrival once, at any rank. Repeat visits use freeform portrayal and the current chapter’s actual changes; event speeches wait for their prerequisites.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0001"></div>


## Smithy and leatherworks

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0002"></div>


*A storefront at the front, a forge at the back. A friendly voice greets you the moment you're through the door, and behind it you can hear the pounding of red-hot metal being shaped into weapons and armour.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0003"></div>


### The People

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0004"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-014-drogan-ironclad-canon.webp" alt="Drogan Ironclad"/><div><b>Drogan Ironclad</b> <i>(dwarf, Grandmaster smith, owner).</i> A gruff, heavily muscled dwarf with a thick beard and calloused hands. He runs the forge in the back, pounding and shaping red-hot metal into weapons and armour. Legendary at the anvil. The only thing harder than the iron he works is getting him into a conversation.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0005"></div>


**Says:** *"Hear that ring? That blade will outlive you."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0006"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-015-kiera-canon.webp" alt="Kiera"/><div><b>Kiera</b> <i>(half-elf, Master armourer).</i> Long brown hair and bright blue eyes. She started as Drogan&#x27;s apprentice, working mostly with leather and other non-metal pieces, and she&#x27;s now the real brains of the business. Her comebacks to Drogan&#x27;s gruffness are as flexible as her leather. Her armour is lighter, tougher and better-fitting than anything else in the region.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0007"></div>


**Says:** *"You've been sleeping in this, haven't you."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0008"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-016-jorn-canon.webp" alt="Jorn"/><div><b>Jorn</b> <i>(human, shopkeeper).</i> Friendly and talkative, he greets customers as they come in, handles the money for both the forge and the storefront, sells finished pieces and takes orders for custom work. His eyes are as keen and sharp as the weapons he sells. He spots a fake coin, a forged receipt or a stolen blade at twenty paces, and he notices who&#x27;s been asking about what.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0009"></div>


**Says:** *"Lovely coin. Shame it's lead."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0010"></div>


{{note
##### Drogan's speciality
Choose whichever suits your party best: Drogan is either a **Grandmaster weaponsmith** or a **Grandmaster armourer**. If he's an armourer too, Kiera specialises in leather and light armour, so the two never step on each other's toes.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 76}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Ironclad’s

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-IRONCLAD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0011"></div>


The three share a home and a life. The bard has a song about them, which all three pretend to hate.

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0012"></div>


**How to play them.** A warm, noisy, close-knit household. They bicker constantly and agree on everything that matters. They respect craft and honesty, and they have no time for anyone who mistreats good equipment.

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0013"></div>


### What They Offer

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0014"></div>


| Service | Notes |
|:--|:--|
| **Weapons and armour** | Fine quality, fair prices. |
| **Repairs** | Fast and good. Faster if you're polite to Kiera. |
| **Commissions** | Custom work, given time, money and the right materials. |
| **Buying materials** | Ore, hides, scales and anything else worth working, including mythril. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0015"></div>


| Service | Price |
|:--|:--|
| Repairs | 10–25% of the item's price, depending on the damage |
| Customisation (engravings, spikes, a new finish) | 50–100% of the item's price; up to 200% for complex work |
| Appraisal | Free, or a few gold |
| Rental | 10–25% of the item's price per week |
| Custom creation | At least double the item's price, and weeks of work |
| Mythril armour | 800 gp plus the armour's price; half the extra if the party brings the mythril |
| Raw mythril | They pay 25 gp per pound |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 77}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Ironclad’s

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-IRONCLAD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0016"></div>


**Stock, by the party's Guild pass:**

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0017"></div>


| Pass | Items |
|:--|:--|
| Copper | Basic weapons (shortswords, daggers, handaxes; 10–20 gp), padded armour (5 gp), leather armour (10 gp), shields (10 gp), arrows and bolts (1 gp), studded leather (45 gp), scale mail (50 gp), breastplate (400 gp), masterwork longswords, greatswords and battleaxes (150–350 gp) |
| Silver | Chain mail (75 gp), half plate (750 gp), adamantine weapons (500 gp), +1 weapons, armour and shields (1,000–2,000 gp) |
| Gold | Full plate (1,500 gp), +2 weapons and armour (4,000–5,000 gp) |
| Platinum and Mithril | +3 weapons and armour (25,000–30,000 gp), dragon scale mail (10,000 gp) |

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0018"></div>


{{note
##### Using Arcane Forge
Ironclad's is the natural home of the *Arcane Forge* book. Drogan and Kiera can be the party's crafting mentors: setting rank trials, teaching blueprints and asking for rare materials. Use that book's rules for harvesting, crafting and rank progression.

Without the book, Ironclad's is simply a smithy and a source of material-hunting jobs.
}}

}}

# Ironclad’s

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-IRONCLAD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0019"></div>


### Jobs and Hooks

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0020"></div>


- **Material hunts:** a particular hide, a particular scale, from a particular creature.
- **Ore runs to the mines,** which put the party in the mining village early.
- **Commissions** that need rare materials, sending the party somewhere dangerous.
- **Repairs after the war** (Gold Chapter): the town will need arms, armour and barricades.

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0021"></div>


### The Old Blacksmith's Daughter

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0022"></div>


Long before Drogan, Silverwood had another legendary smith. His daughter's story still haunts the region and leads to the **banshee at Dragon's Obelisk** (Silver Chapter). Ask Drogan about her and he'll shrug: *"Before my time. Long before. But the rings her father made? I'd give my good hammer to see one."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0023"></div>


*Note: this is not Drogan's daughter. The story is generations old.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0024"></div>


### Rumours About Ironclad's (d4)

<div class="swanchor" id="c08b0025"></div>


| d4 | Rumour |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | "Drogan once forged a sword so sharp it cut the anvil." |
| 2 | "Kiera's armour stopped a troll's claw. The troll's claw did not stop." |
| 3 | "They'll pay double for good scales right now. Something big's being commissioned." |
| 4 | "Never call Drogan's work 'decent'. Just don't." |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

# The Hallowed Halls

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-HALLOWED"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / ONGOING PORTRAYAL
First arrival once, at any rank. Repeat visits use freeform portrayal and the current chapter’s actual changes; event speeches wait for their prerequisites.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0001"></div>


## Silverwood's temple

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0002"></div>


*A grand and imposing building, with towering pillars and vaulted ceilings. The walls are lined with mosaics and carvings, each one showing a different god. The air is thick with incense and the sound of chanting, and you can feel the weight of centuries of faith pressing down on you.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0003"></div>


*And yet it's warm here. Acolytes of different gods move about the temple, sweeping the floors and tending the altars, and the halls are filled with a gentle, comforting light. Whatever you came for, guidance, quiet or just a break from the world's troubles, you'll find a listening ear and a kind heart.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0004"></div>


### History

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0005"></div>


The Halls began as a small temple to **Orathen, Keeper of the Turning Scales**, built by **Brother Brolin Ironhammer**, one of Silverwood's five founders. Brolin was a dwarf priest who believed in the balance of life and death: every life deserves its full course, and every soul its rest.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0006"></div>


Over the centuries, as the town grew, the temple opened its doors to every faith. Today it welcomes worshippers of any god, and its upper pavilion has room for them all.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0007"></div>


{{note
##### The gods
See *The Gods of Silverwood* for the eight principal gods and how divinity works.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0008"></div>


### Layout

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0009"></div>


| Floor | What's here |
|:--|:--|
| **Lower** | Brolin's original temple to Orathen: the oldest shrine, the crypt-keeping records and the founders' memorial plaques. |
| **Middle** | Healing rooms, the priests' quarters and the library of temple records. |
| **Upper** | The open-air **pavilion of the Pantheon**, with its great prayer floor and shrines to a dozen gods. Open to all. |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 80}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Hallowed Halls

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-HALLOWED"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0010"></div>


### The Floor of the Four Forces

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0011"></div>


Inlaid in the pavilion's flagstones are **four enormous overlapping circles**: **Order, Chaos, Creation and Entropy**. No inscription calls any of them good or evil.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0012"></div>


Each god has **one symbol stone**, set where that god's nature falls among the circles:
- **Orathen's** balanced scales lie at the **exact centre**, where all four forces meet.
- **Nhal's** empty ring lies **outside every circle**, in the Void.
- The others lie in the overlaps that suit them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0013"></div>


There are no pews and no assigned places. Worshippers walk to the stone of their god and pray there. Two people praying to different gods still share one sacred floor. People with no patron stand wherever their own belief leads them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0014"></div>


{{note
##### A home for every player's god
The pavilion has a dozen shrines: eight for the principal gods, and the rest for local or lesser gods. Add a stone or shrine for **any god a player character follows**. It makes the temple a natural place for backstories, prayers and quiet character moments.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0015"></div>


### The People

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0016"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-017-brother-alexander.webp" alt="Brother Alexander"/><div><b>Brother Alexander</b> <i>(half-elf, temple keeper).</i> A kind and welcoming figure, and the head of the Hallowed Halls. He follows <b>Aurelune</b>, and uses that connection to offer the temple&#x27;s services to anyone who seeks them. A tireless worker, always ready to lend a helping hand or a listening ear.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0017"></div>


**Says:** *"Whoever you pray to, you're welcome here."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0018"></div>


**The temple staff:** Elowen *(elf)*, Jarel *(dwarf)*, Maren *(human)*, Tovar *(halfling)*, Vesper *(tiefling)*, Meira *(gnome)* and Borak *(half-orc)*. Priests and helpers of many faiths.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 81}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Hallowed Halls

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-HALLOWED"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0019"></div>


### Services

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0020"></div>


| Service | Notes |
|:--|:--|
| **Healing** | Wounds, poison and disease. |
| **Restoration** | Lifts curses and lasting magical harm. **Needed to cure Feywoods maximum hit point loss and later-stage Greenhart infection.** |
| **Remove curse** | As it says. |
| **Resurrection** | When the gods permit it, and at a price. |
| **Exorcism** | Hauntings, possessions and restless spirits. |
| **Blessings** | Before a dangerous job. |
| **Atonement** | For those who have strayed. |
| **Divination** | Guidance from the gods. Never a straight answer. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0021"></div>


| Service | Donation expected |
|:--|:--|
| Healing a wound (*cure wounds*) | 10 gp |
| *Lesser restoration* (early Greenhart stages, disease, poison) | 40 gp |
| *Remove curse* (later Greenhart stages) | 90 gp |
| *Greater restoration* (Feywoods rot, lost Strength) | 450 gp |
| Raising the dead, when the gods permit | 1,250 gp |
| Exorcism | 50 gp, or more for a strong spirit |
| A blessing before a job | 10 gp |
| A question to the gods (*augury*) | 30 gp |
| Atonement | No fee. Usually a task. |
| Holy water, holy symbols and other protections | 25 gp a flask; symbols 5 gp |

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0022"></div>


*Brother Alexander never turns away the poor. He'll take a promise to pay later, or a day's work in the healing rooms.*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 82}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Hallowed Halls

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-HALLOWED"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0023"></div>


### Jobs and Hooks

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0024"></div>


- **Escort and collection jobs:** bringing the sick to the temple, or relics back from the road.
- **Cleansing jobs:** hauntings and cursed places around the region.
- **Exorcism:** the **banshee** at Dragon's Obelisk (Silver Chapter) can be raised here as well as on the Guild board.
- **The Greenhart crisis** (Gold Chapter): the temple becomes the town's hospital as the infection spreads.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0025"></div>


- **Brolin's legacy:** the lower temple holds his oldest records and the founders' memorial plaques. Players digging into Silverwood's history will end up here, and it's one route onto the **founders' trail**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0026"></div>


### Rumours About the Halls (d4)

<div class="swanchor" id="c09b0027"></div>


| d4 | Rumour |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | "The lower shrine's older than the town walls. Some say older than the town." |
| 2 | "Brother Alexander's never turned anyone away. Not even a Blackwood." |
| 3 | "There's a founder's name scratched off one of the old plaques downstairs." |
| 4 | "Brother Alexander sits up with the dying himself. Every one of them. Doesn't sleep, that man." |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 83}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# The Miner’s Mart

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MART"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / ONGOING PORTRAYAL
First arrival once, at any rank. Repeat visits use freeform portrayal and the current chapter’s actual changes; event speeches wait for their prerequisites. Miriam is withdrawn until her hairpin is actually returned.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0001"></div>


## General goods and mining supplies

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0002"></div>


*A cramped, cheerful shop on the lower streets, crammed floor to ceiling with rope, lanterns, picks, rations, boots and everything else a miner or adventurer might need. A bell rings when you open the door. Two voices answer it at once, and one of them wins.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0003"></div>


### The People

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0004"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-018-gideon.webp" alt="Gideon"/><div><b>Gideon</b> <i>(human, co-owner).</i> Portly and friendly, with a thick moustache. He might wear the trousers made of cloth, but his wife wears the trousers of authority. Kind and a little timid. He starts every sentence and finishes about half of them, because his wife usually finishes them for him. He doesn&#x27;t mind. He adores her.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0005"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-019-miriam.webp" alt="Miriam"/><div><b>Miriam</b> <i>(halfling, co-owner).</i> Petite, with curly brown hair and a warm smile, which is the last thing a thief sees. Small, sharp and fierce. She keeps the stock, and the shelves are always full. She runs the shop, haggles like a dragon and <b>cannot stand thieves</b>. Anyone caught pocketing so much as a candle gets chased down the street, and then reported to the guard.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0006"></div>


**Says:** *"Put it back. I saw that."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0007"></div>


**How to play them.** A comic double act with a lot of love underneath. Gideon is soft-spoken and apologetic, while Miriam does the talking, the bargaining and the shouting.

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0008"></div>


{{note
##### When the party first meets them
Miriam is **not herself**. Her family heirloom has been stolen, and she's quiet and withdrawn, so Gideon is doing all the talking for once. Once the hairpin is returned, she comes roaring back to life, and the players see who really runs the shop.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 84}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Miner’s Mart

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MART"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0009"></div>


### What They Sell

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0010"></div>


Everything practical: rope, lanterns, oil, torches, rations, tools, climbing gear, tents, bedrolls, mining picks and helmets, and anything else in the basic equipment lists.

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0011"></div>


Prices are as in the free rules' equipment lists. No magic, and no discounts. Miriam doesn't do discounts.

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0012"></div>


### Job: Miriam's Hairpin

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0013"></div>


*Copper Pass, ★*

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0014"></div>


**The problem.** Miriam's **hairpin**, about a finger long, silver with a cut stone set in the end, has been snatched by a crow. It's only worth a silver piece, but it's been passed down her family for generations, and she's heartbroken. **Gideon** posts the job.

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0015"></div>


**Where it is.** Crows are drawn to shiny things, and this one carried it back to the **Rook's Nest**, a ruined outpost north of town, within sight of the founders' tomb (see *The Silverwood Region*).

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0016"></div>


**Finding it:**
- Asking around the market: someone saw a crow at the window that morning.
- Mirella's smoke cats may have seen it too, if the party asks her.
- Following crows out of town leads to the Rook's Nest.

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0017"></div>


**The Rook's Nest.** Crows roost in the ruins by the hundred. It's sheltered and defensible, and makes a good, safe **campsite**. The crows are noisy and annoying, but not dangerous. Inside the nests: the hairpin, plenty of shiny junk and **a strange stone tile** with markings on it.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 85}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The Miner’s Mart

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MART"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0018"></div>


{{note
##### The crypt puzzle
The **twelve stone tiles** in the nest are the **Raven Job batch for the shield-wall puzzle** (Platinum Chapter). Players won't know what they are yet. Let them keep them.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0019"></div>


**Reward.** 10 gp each (Copper ★), Miriam's eternal gratitude and a shop where they're always welcome. **No discount.**

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0020"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Miriam, with Gideon hovering)*
- **Done:** *"My hairpin! Gideon, look! You lovely, lovely people."*
- **Failed:** *"Never mind, dears. Gideon will buy me a new one. Won't you, Gideon?"*

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0021"></div>


### Rumours About the Mart (d4)

<div class="swanchor" id="c10b0022"></div>


| d4 | Rumour |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | "Someone tried to steal from Miriam once. She chased him three streets in her slippers." |
| 2 | "Gideon's never finished a sentence in his life." |
| 3 | "Miriam's been ever so quiet lately. Something's wrong." |
| 4 | "Best rope in Silverwood. Don't tell Tormund I said so." |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 86}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# The town guard

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-GUARD"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / ONGOING PORTRAYAL
First arrival once, at any rank. Repeat visits use freeform portrayal and the current chapter’s actual changes; event speeches wait for their prerequisites.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0001"></div>


## Walls, gates and law

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0002"></div>


*Blue and green tabards, a tree badge on every chest, and boots that know every stair in town. The Silverwood Guard is small for a town this busy, and it knows it. Since the mythril boom, they've been stretched thinner every month.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0003"></div>


**Insignia:** a stylised tree with shield-shaped leaves, on a shield split blue and green.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0004"></div>


### The People

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0005"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-020-captain-raelyn-rivers-canon.webp" alt="Captain Raelyn Rivers"/><div><b>Captain Raelyn Rivers</b> <i>(human, 35).</i> Tall and imposing, with short blonde hair and a stern expression. A fast, precise duellist who fights with a blade in each hand. Fair, firm and respected. He believes in the law, and he has no patience for anyone who thinks a full purse puts them above it.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0006"></div>


**Says:** *"The law's the same for everyone. Including you."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0007"></div>


<div class="swbio"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/npc-021-sergeant-corwin-hill-canon.webp" alt="Sergeant Corwin Hill"/><div><b>Sergeant Corwin Hill</b> <i>(human, 30).</i> Lean and muscular, with short brown hair and piercing blue eyes. Raelyn&#x27;s right hand. Runs the rotas and the cells, and knows every guard&#x27;s excuses before they make them.</div></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0008"></div>


**Says:** *"You're not on my rota. Why are you on my wall?"*

}}

# The town guard

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-GUARD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0009"></div>


**The guards:**

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0010"></div>


| Name | Race | Age | Looks |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| Lila Stone | Half-elf | 23 | Slender and agile, long dark hair, a mischievous grin |
| Finn Oak | Elf | 26 | Lean and graceful, long blonde hair, bright green eyes |
| Asher Reed | Human | 27 | Tall and lanky, a shock of unruly brown hair, a lazy grin |
| Brynn Birch | Half-elf | 24 | Short and agile, bright blue eyes, a friendly smile |
| Jasper Pine | Dwarf | 25 | Stocky, a bushy beard and a permanent scowl |
| Tessa Moss | Human | 28 | Lean and wiry, sharp features, piercing blue eyes |

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0011"></div>


**Stat blocks:** the guards use the **guard** from the free rules, and Corwin uses the **veteran**. Raelyn has his own **ally card** in the Bestiary.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0012"></div>


### The Law

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0013"></div>


- **Theft is taken seriously.** Shopkeepers, especially Miriam at the Miner's Mart, chase thieves down and report them. Silverwood is not a lawless playground.
- **Fighting in the streets** brings the guard quickly.
- The guard has authority **inside the walls**. The mining village to the south is policed by the mining houses' own guards, and that's part of the problem.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0014"></div>


### Walls and Gates

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0015"></div>


Silverwood sits on a shelf of the mountain, walled and fortified. There are **three gates** (north, south and west). Each gatehouse has a **customs hall** where goods are checked and taxed, a guardroom, and a yard between two round towers:

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 88}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The town guard

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-GUARD"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0016"></div>


| Gate | Notes |
|:--|:--|
| **North** | The main road in. The **Eastern Path** leaves here, bending north-east, then east. |
| **South** | The road to the mining village. |
| **West** | **Shut for generations.** Its bridge once led to the food-growing woods, but the Feywoods became too dangerous to cross. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0017"></div>


**The founders' wards.** Glyphs set by the town's founders strengthen the walls and keep monsters away. Nobody living fully understands how they work.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0018"></div>


**The gate golems.** Two stone golems stand dormant at each gate, carved to look like part of the wall. Most townsfolk have forgotten they're there. Faldor has heard the old rumour that they wake to defend the town.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0019"></div>


{{note
##### The gate golems in play
- A sharp-eyed player may spot one early. Let them wonder.
- In the **Battle of Silverwood** (Gold Chapter), if the army reaches the walls, the golems **wake and fight**.
- If the party meets the army in the field instead, the golems never wake.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0020"></div>


### Jobs and Hooks

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0021"></div>


- **Wanted posters** on the Guild board often come from the guard.
- **Pest control** jobs in every chapter: wolves, ankhegs, ghouls and rot hounds.
- **The gate rota** (Copper Chapter): the guard keeps the rotas for the mining village gates too. A name on the wrong gate is one of the first clues.
- **The Mining Council:** Raelyn sits on it, alongside Faldor and the six houses. His seat gives him a voice, **not authority** over the houses' own people.
- **In the war** (Gold Chapter), the guard holds the walls while the party meets the army.

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0022"></div>


### Rumours About the Guard (d4)

<div class="swanchor" id="c11b0023"></div>


| d4 | Rumour |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | "Captain Rivers once disarmed three men without drawing his second blade." |
| 2 | "There aren't enough of them. Not since the boom." |
| 3 | "Those statues by the gate. Has anyone else noticed they face *outwards*?" |
| 4 | "The houses pay their own guards better. Half the good ones left for the mines." |

}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0058"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0059"></div>


### Carousing

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0060"></div>


A character can spend a night drinking their way around Silverwood for **5 gp**. In the morning, roll a **d20** to see where they wake up, and a **d10** to see what else happened.

# Rumours, games and a night out

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SOCIAL"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0061"></div>


| d20 | You wake up… |
|:--:|:--|
| 19 | On a makeshift horse, with a note from a group of miners thanking you for the fun night and the gold. |
| 20 | With a mask of another player glued to your face. Roll a d4 for how many hours it stays on. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0062"></div>


| d10 | …and also |
|:--:|:--|
| 1 | You're engaged. They seem very happy about it. You don't know their name. |
| 2 | You owe Grimgar 20 gp for "the damages". He won't say what damages. |
| 3 | You learned a new song. It's filthy, and you can't stop humming it. Disadvantage on Stealth until your next long rest. |
| 4 | You're 2d10 gp richer. Nobody will tell you how. |
| 5 | You lost a boot. Pippin has it. She wants 5 sp to give it back. |
| 6 | A miner swears you promised to work his shift today. |
| 7 | A goat now follows you everywhere. It answers to your name. |
| 8 | Flubs has decided it lives in your bag now. |
| 9 | You signed something. Sariel has it. She's smiling. |
| 10 | You made a bet with Tormund, and lost. He'll tell you what you owe when it's funny. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0063"></div>


### Games of Chance

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0064"></div>


The **gambling hall** on the lower tier runs three games. Play them for real at the table with real dice. Each player puts in a stake, and the winner takes the pot.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0065"></div>


**Cart, Driver and Crew.** Each player rolls **five d6**, up to three times. They need a **6** (the cart), then a **5** (the driver), then a **4** (the crew), in that order, setting each aside as they get it. Once all three are found, the other two dice are the **haul**. A player can stop and keep their haul, or reroll those two dice if they still have rolls left. The highest haul wins. No cart, driver and crew means no haul.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0066"></div>


**Cave-In.** Players take turns rolling one **d6** as many times as they like, adding up the total. They can stop at any time and **bank** it. Roll a **1**, and it's a **cave-in**: that turn's total is lost. The first player to bank **50** wins.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 92}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Rumours, games and a night out

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SOCIAL"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0067"></div>


**Miner's Lie.** Each player rolls **five d6** under an upturned mug and keeps them hidden. Taking turns, players bid on how many of one number are under **all** the mugs, such as *"four 3s"*. Each bid must be higher than the last. Any player can call **"Liar!"** instead. Lift the mugs: if the bid was wrong, the bidder loses a die; if it was right, the caller does. The last player with dice wins.

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0068"></div>


{{note
##### Cheating
A character can try to cheat with a **Dexterity (Sleight of Hand)** check against the gambling hall's watchers (passive Perception 14). In Miner's Lie, a character can read another player with **Wisdom (Insight)** against their **Charisma (Deception)**, and the GM tells them whether that player's last bid was honest. Get caught cheating, and Grimgar throws you out. Get caught twice, and he throws you off the bridge.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0069"></div>


### Omega Oliver's Songs

<div class="swanchor" id="c04b0070"></div>


Omega plays most nights. For a few coppers, he'll play a request. For a drink, he'll play something he shouldn't.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

# Early visits beyond town

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-AREAS"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### WARN / DO NOT BLOCK
Physical access is distinct from challenge. These are valid early-visit responses, including the tomb’s wards, Obelisk day/night danger, greenhouse barns and sealed tower. The later chapter supplies the event when it actually occurs.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0053"></div>


## The Rook's Nest

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0054"></div>


A ruined outpost on the high ground north of town, built in the same style as Silverwood's oldest buildings. Crows roost in it by the hundred. It's sheltered and defensible, a **safe place to camp**, and the founders' tomb is in plain view from its walls.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0055"></div>


- **Miriam's hairpin** is here, in a crows' nest (Copper).
- So are **twelve stone tiles** for the shield-wall puzzle (the Raven Job batch), in the same nest. They can't be missed, but players won't know what they are yet.
- Players who come here for the hairpin, or while gathering, **find the route to the Xanthos Crypt**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0056"></div>


## The Xanthos Crypt

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0057"></div>


The resting place of the founders and of Silverwood's most important dead. It has two parts: the **Tomb of the Founders** above ground, and the **Catacombs** beside it.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0058"></div>


### The Tomb of the Founders

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0059"></div>


A building of **solid stone**: walls, roof and even the doors. Grand, but plain. No carvings, no windows, no mirrors.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0060"></div>


**The doors cannot be opened from outside**, and the building defends itself:

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0061"></div>


> **Warded Stone.** When a creature attacks the tomb's walls or doors with any attack (weapon, spell or arrow), compare its attack roll to **its own AC**. If the roll **equals or beats its own AC**, the attack rebounds and **hits the attacker** instead. If the roll is lower, the attack bounces off harmlessly and misses everyone. The tomb is never damaged.
>
> A spell that calls for a saving throw rebounds on the caster, who makes the save against their own spell.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0062"></div>


**The only way in is from below,** through the catacombs (see *The Crypt Puzzle*, Platinum Chapter). Inside are the founders' statues, their tombs, Brolin's diary and Saraphina's letters.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 95}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Early visits beyond town

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-AREAS"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0063"></div>


### The Catacombs

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0064"></div>


Beside the tomb, a **double-helix staircase** spirals down into the rock: two stairways winding around each other. Everything below is as ornate as the tomb above is plain.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0065"></div>


**The layout echoes the Floor of the Four Forces** in the Hallowed Halls: **four great overlapping rings**, but here the rings are **walkways**, lined on both sides with the burial niches of Silverwood's most important people since its founding. The walkways are **narrow: one person wide**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0066"></div>


Where each ring reaches its outer edge is a **wider chamber**, four in all. Each has a **mosaic** on its far wall.
- **Three of the mosaics are identical:** the founders, painted as they were in life.
- **The western chamber** is different. Its celestial mural holds **two round bronze holy seals**, each with a **5 × 5 grid** of square recesses. This is the **shield wall**. Solving it opens the passage to the **Crypt of the Founders**. Release **14 tiles** during the catacomb search; this is one batch mixing pieces from both seals.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0067"></div>


{{note
##### Running the catacombs early
Players can explore the catacombs from the Copper Chapter onwards, but they **can't solve the puzzle** until they've found the tiles. If they come here early, describe the west-catacomb wall, release the catacomb-search batch once, and let them try their pieces. The wall stays sealed until all 50 tiles are in the correct seals and squares. The full puzzle is in the **Platinum Chapter**.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0068"></div>


## Dragon's Obelisk

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0069"></div>


A tall, lonely spire of rock on the cliff edge, north-west of town, past the Xanthos Crypt. Even by day, it feels charged with a strange power.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0070"></div>


- **By day:** a landmark, with a view across half the region.
- **By night:** the **banshee** haunts it, and her wail can kill. **Only at full moon** can she be talked to (see the Silver Chapter).
- **The miners' graveyard,** on the road out to the Obelisk, holds her lover's grave.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0071"></div>


- **Gold offered here summons a dragon** (see *Wider Horizons*, Platinum Chapter).
- **Beneath the Obelisk,** hidden in the cliff face, are the caves of the **ancient dragon riders**. Nobody has found them in seven hundred years. *(A sequel hook. Not in this campaign.)*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 96}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Early visits beyond town

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-AREAS"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0072"></div>


## The Goblin Hills

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0073"></div>


East of Silverwood, along the middle road from the caravan junction. The hills are riddled with goblin warrens, like rabbits in a field: destroy one nest and there's always another.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0074"></div>


There are far too many goblins and far too little food. They eat everything, which leaves almost nothing for anyone else to hunt. **Culling them genuinely helps** the region, and the Guild pays a bounty per pair of ears.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0075"></div>


## Greenhart Grove

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0076"></div>


Lord Xanthos's great magical greenhouse, near the base of his tower.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0077"></div>


- **From Copper:** players can visit the **barns**, where produce is collected for the town (Thalion's job). **The greenhouse itself is closed.**
- **Gold Chapter:** the greenhouse crisis.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0078"></div>


## Xanthos Tower

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0079"></div>


Lord Xanthos's tower, high on the mountain. Players can walk right up to it, but **they can't get in**. The doors won't move, and the wards strike back at anyone who tries. It would take power far beyond anything in this campaign to break in.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0080"></div>


- **Platinum Chapter:** the invitation to dinner.
- **Mithril Chapter:** the way in from below.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 97}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Early visits beyond town

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-AREAS"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0081"></div>


## Swamps and Wild Forest

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0082"></div>


The marshes and woods around the mining village and towards the Obelisk. Good hunting for herbs, materials and creatures to capture, but the creatures here are **tougher than the party at Copper rank**. Warn them, and let them choose.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0083"></div>


## The Feywoods

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0084"></div>


West and south-west, beyond the **west gate**, shut for generations. Once the founders' food forest, now wild, rotten in places and full of fey.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0085"></div>


- **Very dangerous at low levels:** manticores, chimeras and worse.
- **Lirien:** players who have heard the bard's rhyme about her may meet her here at any level. Her shadow fight scales to the party.
- **Locked until Platinum:** the **Willow Mother's clearing** and the **obelisks** stay hidden until the story opens them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0086"></div>


## The Eastern Path

<div class="swanchor" id="c03b0087"></div>


The caravan road, leaving through the north gate, bending north-east and then east. Food comes in this way once a week. A little way out, it crosses the **river** that feeds Silverwood, at the **only crossing point** for miles. In the **Gold Chapter**, the Blackwood army blockades it.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 98}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# The Copper job desk

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-JOBS"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0012"></div>


### Hunt: Goblins

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [Sariel / Guild · p. 56](#p56). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0013"></div>


*Copper Pass*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0014"></div>


> *"Goblin horde control required. 25 cp for each pair of ears collected."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0015"></div>


The goblins that hit the caravan came from somewhere. The Guild wants the nearest goblin camp, east along the middle road, broken up.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0016"></div>


**The camp.** A warren dug into a hillside a few hours east along the middle road. Three parts, which the party can take one at a time:

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0017"></div>


| Area | Who's there | Difficulty (level 3) |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **The lookout:** a rock above the entrance | **4 goblins** on watch, half asleep | Easy |
| **The pens:** a fenced hollow beside the warren | **2 worgs**, chained but able to slip free | Easy |
| **The den:** the main cave, around a fire pit | **A bugbear chief** and **4 goblins** | Medium |

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0018"></div>


**The alarm.** If a lookout gets away or anyone shouts, the camp wakes. The worgs are loosed and the den empties towards the noise. Everything fighting at once is **deadly**, so let the players hear it coming: a horn, then howling, then running feet.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0019"></div>


**What's in the den:** the **sacks of food** stolen from the caravan in the Prologue, still marked with the Farman stamp, and the camp's few coins. **Bram Tallow** pays **2 gp a sack** to get them back.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0020"></div>


{{note
##### Running the camp
- **Goblins flee** once half of them are down. The chief doesn't; he's cornered in his own den.
- **Sneaking works.** A party that takes the lookout quietly can deal with each part on its own.
- **It's not the only camp.** Destroy it, and there's always another in the hills. That's what the bounty is for.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0021"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Sariel)*
- **Done:** *"Ears in the bag, please, not on the counter. Thank you. I'll count them later. Much later."*
- **Failed:** *"Back already, and no ears? Faldor's going to grunt at you. I'd go out the side door."*

}}

# The Copper job desk

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-JOBS"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0022"></div>


### Missing Person

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [Edda’s Guild notice · p. 59](#p59). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0023"></div>


*Copper Pass, ★*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0024"></div>


**Edda Morrow** is worried sick. Her husband **Jory**, an independent miner, hasn't come home in a week. He'd been struggling: rising rent, late payments and debts to his suppliers.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0025"></div>


**The truth:** Jory is safe. Out of work and ashamed, he took a Blackwood offer of a job, a bed and hot meals in their workers' lodgings, and couldn't face telling Edda. He's grateful to the Blackwoods and won't hear a word against them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0026"></div>


- **Finding him:** asking around the mining village, the boarding houses, or the Blackwood soup kitchen.
- **Why it matters:** it's a small, human story that shows players how the Blackwoods win people over: with real kindness at exactly the right moment.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0027"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Edda Morrow)*
- **Done:** *"He's alive? He's eating? The fool. The stupid, wonderful fool. Thank you."*
- **Failed:** *"Nobody's seen him? Then I'll keep looking myself."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0028"></div>


### Hunt: Kobolds in the Deep

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [The miners’ foreman · p. 108](#p108). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0029"></div>


*Copper Pass*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0030"></div>


> *"Kobolds are attacking the miners as they enter the deep from the old silver caves. We ask the Adventurers' Guild to come and dispatch them before someone is seriously hurt. The group has 4 kobolds, who have been known to set a net trap and then attack whoever's in it. 10 gp per kobold tail collected."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0031"></div>


**4 kobolds** in the tunnels between the old silver caves and the mythril deep. Their **net trap** is a DC 13 Wisdom (Perception) check to spot; a creature caught in it is restrained (escape DC 12, or cut free). *Easy at level 3.* These are the first of the kobolds; in the Silver Chapter, more arrive (see *The Kobold Caves*).

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0032"></div>


**Handing it in** *(The miners' foreman)*
- **Done:** *"That's the deep clear, then. Drinks are on the night shift."*
- **Failed:** *"They're still down there? Then we're not going down."*

}}

### Gathering: Herbs

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [Ampersand, through Valor-33 · p. 68](#p68). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0034"></div>


*Copper Pass*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0035"></div>


> *"I need Balm Fungi and Mandrake Root. They grow at the edge of the enchanted forest. If you need to ask what they look like, then don't bother. I pay for quality, not rubbish. Mandrake Root: good 60 gp, excellent 90 gp. Balm Fungi: good 40 gp, excellent 60 gp."* — Ampersand

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0036"></div>


**Mirella** sells the herb books that help find them (see the herb pages in the *Handout Pack*).

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0037"></div>


- **Without *Ampersand's Apothecaria*:** a fetch job with a few skill checks and a minor hazard. The quality depends on a DC 13 Wisdom (Survival) check when harvesting: excellent on a 18 or more.
- **With the book:** use its foraging and harvesting rules.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0038"></div>


This job is a natural route from the Guild to Mirella and Ampersand.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0039"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Ampersand, through Valor-33)*
- **Done:** *"The young master says, and I quote, 'Not rubbish! Ampersand is delighted!' Your payment is in the box."*
- **Failed:** *"The young master has looked at your herbs. He has asked me to tell you they are rubbish. I have chosen kinder words. These are rubbish."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0040"></div>


### Gathering: Manticore Parts

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [Mirella · p. 64](#p64). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0041"></div>


*Copper Pass*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0042"></div>


> *"My familiar has spotted a juvenile manticore at the Dragon Obelisk, north-west past the crypt of Xanthos. Please bring me its paws, wings and tail spikes. Each tail spike will be 1 gp. 30–150 gp for each other item, depending on damage and quality of harvest."* — Mirella

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0043"></div>


**A juvenile manticore:** use the **manticore** with 45 hit points and only one tail spike volley before it runs out. *Hard at level 3.* A careful party can bring it down with little damage to the parts. Mirella's prices drop for every part that's burnt, cut through or missing.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0044"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Mirella)*
- **Done:** *"Lovely. Barely a scratch on the wings. My cat says you're braver than you look."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0048"></div>

**Solleaf** *(radiant, Common)* adds healing to a potion. **Rillstem** *(water, Common)* does the same. An easy first job, and a reason to meet Alexander.

**Riverberry** *(water, Uncommon)* heals a little when eaten. **Starberry** *(radiant, Common)* makes people charming for a while, which is why Thalion's desserts are so popular. He pays by the handful.

*Without Ampersand's Apothecaria, use Gathering Jobs (Region file).*

### Missing: Flubs

{{swpilot-small
**Giver / return contact:** [Omega Oliver · p. 90](#p90). [Encounter desk · p. 123](#p123) · [Map IDs · p. 128](#p128). Use the stated reward and the hand-in for the actual outcome.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0091"></div>


*Copper Pass*

> *"MISSING: one slime. Small. Rainbow. Answers to Flubs (sometimes). Last seen bouncing towards the lower tier. Please return him safely to Omega Oliver."* — Omega Oliver

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0093"></div>


*The poster forgets to mention a reward. Omega didn't think of it.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0094"></div>


**Where:** the gambling hall on the lower tier.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0095"></div>


**What happened.** Flubs followed the smell of spilled ale downstairs and climbed into a dice cup during a game of **Miner's Lie**. He's still under it, very drunk, glowing softly, and making little illusions of dice that change whenever anyone looks. Two miners are arguing over whether the cup is cursed.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0096"></div>


- Asking around the Shaft and Pickaxe points the party downstairs (Pippin saw something "bouncing funny").
- Lifting the cup in the middle of the game starts a small row. Buying the table a round settles it.
- Flubs won't come quietly. He hiccups, turns invisible for a moment, and reappears in someone's bag.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0097"></div>


**Handing it in** *(Omega Oliver)*
- **Done:** *"FLUBS! You absolute puddle! Thank you, friends. A reward? Oh. Oh dear. Would a song do? Or a riddle?"* The party gets a **free song or riddle**, and Omega's riddles always hide a real clue.
- **Failed:** *"He'll turn up. He always turns up. Usually in the soup."*

# The mining houses

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MINING-DOSSIER"></div>



<figure class="swpilot-scene"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/approved-banners-plate.webp" alt="Approved banners: Silverwood, Valerion, Blackwood, Darnell, Penhallow, Farman and Ursell"/><figcaption>Left to right: Silverwood · Valerion · Blackwood · Darnell · Penhallow · Farman · Ursell</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### WHO KNOWS WHAT
GM context for Scratch and Sniff. The family relationships and logistics are known to the GM; do not hand the conspiracy answer to the party. Faldor remains uncertain.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0000"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0001"></div>


## Six families and one secret

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0002"></div>


*South of Silverwood, the new mining village of **Mithrilhold** sprawls across the hillside: tents, timber halls, smoke and noise. Six great houses have staked their claims here, each with its own guards, banners and ambitions. Most of them just want to get rich. One of them wants everything.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0003"></div>


### The Mythril Boom

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0004"></div>


For generations Silverwood was dying. The silver ran out, people left and the town shrank. Then miners digging through the old silver caves found something deeper: **mythril**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0005"></div>


Now everyone has come, and the village is changing fast, like every mining boom before it.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0006"></div>


### Who Lives Here

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0007"></div>


- **Independent miners:** a shrinking number, like the dwarf twins **Hilda and Halvar**. They own their tools, rent their rooms, buy their own supplies and sell their own mythril. Every bad week pushes a few more of them out.
- **House miners:** the growing majority. Paid wages by a mining house, and often housed and fed by it too.
- **Village businesses:** boarding houses, taverns, merchants and healers, increasingly competing with the services the houses provide for their own workers.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0008"></div>


The mines themselves are a mix: an old shared network of tunnels, and newer shafts and claims owned by individual houses.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 108}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The mining houses

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MINING-DOSSIER"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0009"></div>


### How the Mythril Moves

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0010"></div>


South of the village are the **old silver caves**, and through those caves lies the **new mythril mine** and the **base camp**, where every miner hands in their mythril for weighing and taxation. It's then taken under guard to the mining village every second day.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0011"></div>


| Stage | What happens | Who runs it |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Housing and hiring** | Miners are hired, housed and paid. | The houses, and private landlords |
| **Supplies** | Food, water, timber, medicine and goods come in. | Farman, merchants and the houses |
| **Equipment** | Tools, wagon parts, lifts and mine supports. | Darnell |
| **Transport** | Wagons carry workers and supplies to the mines and back. | Farman, with shared escorts |
| **Mine safety** | Sign-in, shifts, inspections and digging. | Each mine's owner, especially Valerion |
| **Protection** | Gates, patrols, escorts and keeping monsters down. | Ursell, and hired guards |
| **Weighing and tax** | Every load is weighed at the base camp, and the Lord of Silverwood takes **10%**. | The mine owners and the Lord's tax keepers |
| **Processing** | Mythril is sorted, processed and stored. | Valerion |
| **Contracts** | Prices, buyers and agreements. | Penhallow, and each house |
| **Export** | Mythril goes out through Silverwood; the same wagons bring supplies back. | Farman |

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0012"></div>


### The Mining Council

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0013"></div>


The town's guard has no authority in the mining village. Shared matters are decided by an **eight-member council**:

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0014"></div>


- **One representative from each of the six houses**
- **Faldor**, for the Guild
- **Captain Raelyn Rivers**, for the town guard

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 109}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The mining houses

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MINING-DOSSIER"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0015"></div>


Decisions are made by **majority vote**. The council hands out shared jobs (escorts, gate duty, repairs) to particular houses.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0016"></div>


**The key rule:** a house given a job can **swap it, share it or hire someone else to do it.** But **the house that was given the job still answers for it** if it goes wrong. Much of this swapping is agreed privately, so the council often doesn't know who is really doing what.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0017"></div>


### The Six Houses

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0018"></div>


| House | Trade | Insignia |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Valerion** | The oldest mining family. Experts in extraction and processing, and hired by others to oversee their mines. | Crossed pickaxe and hammer on a diagonal silver and black shield |
| **Blackwood** | Newly risen. Found a rich new vein and are expanding fast. Generous, protective and *very* helpful. | A tree with deep roots and spreading branches |
| **Darnell** | Makers of high-quality mining tools and equipment. | Crossed pickaxe and shovel on a red and grey shield |
| **Penhallow** | Brokers and dealmakers with contacts everywhere. | Interlinked rings |
| **Farman** | Food, transport and supplies. Runs the caravans. | A horse-drawn cart on a blue and green shield |
| **Ursell** | Professional security. Their fighters guard mines, caravans and anyone who pays. | A bear's head on a silver and black shield |

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0019"></div>


{{note
##### Silverwood and Blackwood
Both the town and the Blackwoods use a tree on their badges. That's deliberate. The Blackwoods present themselves as Silverwood's protectors, and they look the part. The difference is in the roots.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 110}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The mining houses

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MINING-DOSSIER"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0020"></div>


### The Blackwoods' Public Face

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0021"></div>


To everyone in the mining village, the Blackwoods are **the good guys**. When accidents happen, they're first on the scene. When workers are hurt, they offer shelter and care. When caravans are attacked, they offer more guards. When rivals struggle, they offer help. They even suffer thefts and setbacks of their own, and they're always sympathetic about everyone else's.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0022"></div>


And much of their help is **real**: injured miners really are cared for, hungry workers really are fed, broken wagons really are fixed. Many Blackwood workers honestly believe they're saving Silverwood.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0023"></div>


Players should **like them at first**, or at least respect them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0024"></div>


{{note
##### GM only: the truth
The Blackwoods are a crime family running a long con. They **cause some problems, make real ones worse and let the blame land on whichever house holds that job.** Then they offer help. Every rescue becomes a contract, a debt, a right or a grateful worker.

**The goal:** they don't need a majority on the council, or to own every mine. They need to run so many of the village's services that **nothing can work without them**.

**How it escalates:**
1. **Bad luck.** Equipment fails, stock goes missing, small cave-ins, the odd attack. The houses still cooperate.
2. **Quiet rescues.** The Blackwoods privately take on more and more jobs. The other houses get poorer, workers take sides, and the council starts arguing about who's to blame.
3. **Crisis.** Ursell is close to pulling out. A serious cave-in nearly kills someone. The council talks about handing vital services to "more reliable" hands, and the Blackwoods are ready and waiting.

See *The Blackwood Family* for who's who, and the **Copper Chapter** for how the players can uncover it.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 111}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# The mining houses

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-MINING-DOSSIER"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0025"></div>


### What's Going Wrong

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0026"></div>


Every house has a problem in its own area, and they're starting to blame each other.

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0027"></div>


| House | The trouble | What the Blackwoods offer |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Valerion** | Cave-ins are getting worse, some near brand-new supports. No deaths, yet. Old miners are questioning the family. | Extra inspection teams, and to oversee shared tunnels |
| **Darnell** | Broken wagons, failing tools, missing parts and a growing repair backlog. | Their own workshops, workers and equipment loans |
| **Farman** | Missing stock, unexplained surplus and late deliveries. | Extra wagons and warehouse space |
| **Ursell** | Returning wagons attacked, gates threatened, patrols failing. **Ursell is close to leaving Silverwood.** | Replacement patrols, escorts and gate guards |
| **Penhallow** | Disputed contracts and invoices. Others accuse them of profiting from the confusion. | Help settling the disputes, in return for "preferred supplier" deals |

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0028"></div>


{{note
##### Ursell is worth saving
Ursell isn't corrupt. Its guards are doing their best, being blamed for failures they can't explain and losing their friends' trust. Give the players a reason to **defend** them, not just suspect them.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c12b0029"></div>


**Other trouble brewing:** rising rents in crowded lodgings, overwhelmed healers, late or short wages, flooded workings, bad timber for supports, sick draft animals, crumbling shared roads and ramps, and disputes over who owns new mythril claims. Any of these can become another way in for the Blackwoods.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 112}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}
