# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0123"></div>


### How the Investigation Works

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0124"></div>


**Cover jobs.** Sariel arranges ordinary, paid Guild jobs in the mining village: deliveries, escorts, errands. Some are real and some she sets up on purpose, so the party has a believable reason to be in the right place.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0125"></div>


- **Sariel only describes the job.** She never says what to look for, never hands out clues and never asks the party to use false identities.
- **Every cover job is paid** like any other Guild job.
- **When the party reports back,** Sariel listens calmly and asks for proof. She won't accept "it's the Blackwoods" just because the players suspect it.
- **The party can investigate on their own** as well: talk to miners, follow leads, read the room. They should avoid open accusations and fights.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0126"></div>


**Pacing.** This is an ongoing thread through the whole Copper Chapter, **not a chain of quests**.
- Sariel brings in **one job at a time**, as she can arrange them. Sometimes she has nothing, because a house is busy or nothing suitable has come up. Sometimes an accident or attack creates an opening overnight.
- Meanwhile the party explores, takes other jobs and lives in the town. They can turn down or put off investigation jobs. **The trouble in the mines carries on regardless.**

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0127"></div>


### The Flow of Jobs

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0128"></div>


Sariel's cover jobs follow the trouble. Each one **seems to blame a different house**, but the **Blackwood thread gets stronger each time**. Most parties will work it out around job three. Jobs four and five turn suspicion into a pattern Faldor can use.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0129"></div>


Between jobs, **something happens in the village**, and that event is what lets Sariel set up the next job.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0130"></div>


#### Job 1: The Forgotten Lunch

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0131"></div>


*Day two. Take **Pell Garrow**, a Farman clerk at the depot, the lunch he "forgot".*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0132"></div>


- **Who looks guilty:** **Farman.** Sloppy, or skimming.
- **What the party can find:** the **depot dispatch book** and the **returned dockets** (Handout: *Farman Depot*). Base camp is always one meal sack short, and that really is Pell: he takes a sack every few days for his sister's children. Loads booked to the **East yard** arrive badly short, and **grease drums** nobody sent turn up with them. Every East yard load was booked by **W.H.**, the dispatcher **Wendel Hask**.
- **The Blackwood thread (faint):** the East yard is a spare store the **Blackwoods kindly lent** while base camp is re-roofed. That sounds generous, not guilty. Players who visit the village later may spot **Farman sacks at the Blackwood soup kitchen**.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 114}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0133"></div>


#### Job 2: The Replacement Parts

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0134"></div>


*Day two. Deliver parts to **Hobb Tinsley**, foreman of the Darnell repair yard.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0135"></div>


- **Who looks guilty:** **Darnell.** Shoddy parts.
- **What the party can find:** the **yard work book** (Handout: *Darnell Repair Yard*). Hobb is gruff, proud and furious. He kept the snapped axle, and it was **sawn part-way through**. Darnell really did fit cheap "seconds" pins to one cart, which muddies the water. Wagons C, F and H carry the same **scratched marks**. *(Their meaning comes out in the Silver Chapter.)*
- **The Blackwood thread (faint):** the axles were fitted by **"L. Irons + 2"**, volunteers "from the soup kitchen crowd" who helped clear the backlog for free. **Cart 12** is still working the ore run and stands overnight in the base camp cart line (see *The Village Diary*, day 4).

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0136"></div>


> **Then:** a returning wagon is attacked under **Ursell** escort. The guards swear the beasts ran straight past them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0137"></div>


#### Job 3: The Escort Duty

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0138"></div>


*Ride along on a return run, or deliver a new rota to Ursell.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0139"></div>


- **Who looks guilty:** **Ursell.** Useless, or bribed. Their rota clerk, **Corra Lusk**, really has been changing shifts without signed orders to pay off a debt.
- **What the party can find:** **bait paste** worked into the **axle grease**, and a **bait attack** on the way (wolves or giant rats). **Zoltan** at Ampersand's or **Mirella** can name the rare ingredient, **hollowhart musk**, and the one broker who imports it: **Penhallow**, on a client account. The **wagon ledger** (Handout: *Wagon Escorts*) shows a pattern.
- **The Blackwood thread (strong):** **every attacked wagon was escorted *out* by the Blackwoods**, and the bait is in grease, like the drums from Job 1. This is the first time the "helpers" line up with the trouble, and the **earliest** a sharp table should suspect them.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0140"></div>


> **Next:** use the actual third-cave-in branch in [the Village Diary · p. 120](#p120). If the party prevents the fall, use the written bait attack instead.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 115}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0141"></div>


#### Job 4: The Mine Inspection

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0142"></div>


*Deliver timber, or escort an inspector, to the Valerion workings.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0143"></div>


- **Who looks guilty:** **Valerion.** Negligent, and the foreman is covering up his mistakes out of fear for his job.
- **What the party can find,** by any route:
  - **The twins' ledger** (Handout: *Mine Attendance*): **Hilda and Halvar** always sign in together, and Hilda writes both names because Halvar can't write. But on **the evening before each cave-in**, "Halvar" signs in **alone**, late, in a different hand. Someone came in disguised as him. The ledger is kept by **Tobble Ambersand**, who'll happily show it to anyone who mentions Zoltan or Faldor.
  - **The incident reports** (Handout: *Cave-In Reports*) give the dates and places of the falls.
  - **The gate rota** (Handout: *Gate Watch Roster*): **Leif Irons** keeps turning up at posts he isn't listed for, with no signed relief chit. After the beast alarm on the 17th, he's in the **infirmary**, hurt at a gate he was never assigned to.
- **The Blackwood thread (strong):** Leif is a Blackwood guard, and his name was on the axles in Job 2.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0144"></div>


#### Job 5: The Ledger Collection

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0145"></div>


*Fetch the ore weights for the Mining Council.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0146"></div>


- **Who looks guilty:** **Penhallow.** Tax fraud. Every ore lot is sold through their account, and their clerk **Edmund Pryce** wants the Lord's tenth taken on the lower village weights.
- **What the party can find:** the **weigh house** and **receiving** books (Handout: *Ore Weighing Ledger*). Ore only goes missing when a **relief crew** took over the load on the road, and **every relief chit is countersigned "C.D."**
- **The Blackwood thread:** the relief crews are Blackwood, and **C.D.** is **Callum Dark**. That's a name to carry into Silver.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0147"></div>


{{note
##### Not every job is needed
If the party works it out at job three, jobs four and five become **corroboration**, and Sariel still wants them. If they never take a job at all, the same clues can turn up through rumours, events and their own digging.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0148"></div>


### The Escort Rota

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0149"></div>


Guards escort a wagon **out** to the mines, guard the mine for a shift, then escort **the next available wagon** back. Different houses take turns.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0150"></div>


The attacks happen on the **return** journey, so the returning guards (often Ursell, Farman or Valerion) get the blame.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0151"></div>


But compare the rota: **every attacked wagon was escorted *out* by the Blackwoods.** Not every Blackwood wagon gets attacked. It's a pattern, not proof.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 116}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0152"></div>


| Wagon | Out | Back | Result |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| A | Farman | Blackwood | Safe |
| B | Ursell | Farman | Safe |
| C | Blackwood | Ursell | **Attacked** |
| D | Blackwood | Darnell | Safe |
| E | Valerion | Blackwood | Safe |
| F | Blackwood | Farman | **Attacked** |
| G | Darnell | Ursell | Safe |
| H | Blackwood | Valerion | **Attacked** |

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0153"></div>


*Example only. This is for the players to work out, never to be told.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0154"></div>


### Other Ways In

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0155"></div>


The clues above aren't locked to their jobs. **The bait, the scratched marks, the twins, the gate guard and the drunk dwarf in the Last Lamp** can also turn up through rumours, events or the party's own digging. A lucky find, like a natural 20 at the right desk, can hand players a piece early. It won't make sense until the jobs show them the whole picture. Don't hand them all out at once.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0156"></div>


### Who's Involved

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0157"></div>


Not everyone helping the Blackwoods knows what they're part of:

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 117}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0158"></div>


| Who | How involved |
|:--|:--|
| **Wendel Hask**, Farman dispatcher | **Paid informant.** Sells wagon times and books the East yard loads. Thinks he's selling to a rival merchant. *(Notebook II)* |
| **Corra Lusk**, Ursell rota clerk | **In debt.** Makes unsigned shift swaps. Frightened. *(Notebook III)* |
| **Edmund Pryce**, Penhallow contract clerk | **Knowing collaborator.** Pays off the bribed miner and covers the weights. *(Notebook IV)* |
| A Valerion foreman | **Frightened.** Hiding mistakes to protect his job, without knowing their cause. |
| A Darnell stores clerk | **Unwitting.** Fitted the cheap pins. Thinks the Blackwoods' help is genuine. |
| **Osric Venn**, East yard hand | **Grateful.** A former independent miner. Tells everyone to trust the Blackwoods. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0159"></div>


*Behind them, and unknown to each other: **Leif Irons** (Blackwood guard, verbal orders only), **Korgoth Darkblade** (the false Halvar, Notebook VI), **Callum Dark** (Blackwood security, Notebook V) and **Darian Gray** (in Silverwood, Notebook I). Only **Vashkar** sees the whole board. See The Six Notebooks in the Silver Chapter.*

<div class="swanchor" id="NEW-COP-KORGOTH"></div>


### The Drunk in the Last Lamp

<div class="swanchor" id="NEW-COP-KORGOTH"></div>



The **Last Lamp** is Mithrilhold's shabby miners' bar, where the night shift drinks before bed. On **the night after a cave-in**, a broad, quiet dwarf drinks alone in the corner: **Korgoth Darkblade**, a Blackwood man and the false "Halvar". Other nights he keeps to himself.

- **He says nothing useful sober.** Drunk, he gets maudlin about "borrowed faces" and "brothers who can't hold a quill". That only means something to players who've seen the twins' ledger.
- **In his coat:** a small notebook (Notebook VI) and a cheap illusion trinket. Lifting it takes a party that gets him properly drunk, plus a DC 13 Sleight of Hand.
- **What it proves:** the cave-ins were deliberate, and someone else gives the orders. **It names no one.**
- **If the party lifts it,** Korgoth stops answering his notebook. Vashkar notices the silence (*The Six Notebooks*, stage 1).
- **If Korgoth is caught before the third fall, Leif Irons steps in** (see *The Third Cave-In* in the Handout Pack GM keys).

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 118}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE / CONTINUED}}

# Operation Scratch and Sniff

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-SNIFF"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0160"></div>


{{note
##### Running the investigation
- **Not every accident is the Blackwoods.** Some are real, and the Blackwoods simply take advantage.
- **No single failed check blocks the investigation.** If one route fails, another job, witness or event points the same way.
- **The players may work it out after three jobs, or sooner, or later.** Reward good thinking. Sariel keeps asking for proof either way.
- **Faldor doesn't know it's the Blackwoods.** He only knows something is wrong.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0161"></div>


### Keeping the Party on Track

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0162"></div>


The investigation is **never forced**, but the party is **gently prompted**:
- **Sariel keeps finding work.** Whenever the party comes back to the Guild, there's a chance she has a new cover job: *"We've got another one. I need you on this while the cover lasts."* It's a nudge, not an order.
- **The trouble keeps getting worse** whether they help or not (see *The Village Diary*).
- **Side jobs pay in coin, contacts and gear, but little experience.** The main thread is where the real progress is, so a party can't race ahead by ignoring it.
- **Faldor won't give out Silver Passes** until Scratch and Sniff has brought him something (see *Ranking Up*).

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0163"></div>


### Closing the Case

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0164"></div>


When the party has a convincing pattern, Sariel arranges a **private meeting with Faldor**, and the players present what they've found themselves. Faldor is delighted:

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0165"></div>


> *"Looks like Scratch and Sniff has sniffed out something rather foul!"*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0166"></div>


Sariel puts her head in her hands. Then Faldor gets serious:

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0167"></div>


> *"But this is ledgers and coincidences. The Blackwoods are the richest, best-loved house in that village. Put this in front of the council and they'll laugh us out. Get me proof. Names. Something with their hand on it."*

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0168"></div>


**What the party has is a pattern, not proof.** Finding the proof is the **Silver Chapter**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0169"></div>


## Bait Attacks

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0170"></div>


The Blackwoods' bait draws monsters onto marked carts. In this chapter, it attracts **small creatures** only: **wolves, giant rats** or **goblins**.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0171"></div>


Use a bait attack whenever the party is escorting a cart, visiting the village or things have gone quiet. As the campaign goes on, the bait gets stronger and the creatures get bigger.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 119}}

\page {sw-reorder}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GM GUIDE}}

# The Village Diary

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-DIARY"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0172"></div>


## The Village Diary

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0173"></div>


The trouble runs on a **fixed diary**, counted from the party's arrival (day 1 = **20 Verdantis 1494**). Each event happens **whether the party is there or not**. If they're away, they hear about it in the street, at the Guild or on the notice board. The paperwork in the handouts covers **2–18 Verdantis**, the fortnight before they arrived. The only live event with its own paperwork is the **third cave-in**, because it can go three ways.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0174"></div>


| Day | Date | What happens | Opens |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 1 | 20 V. | The party arrives. The **Council vote** is already posted for 1 Borealis: escort duty to go to the Blackwoods. | Scratch and Sniff |
| 2 | 21 V. | A quiet day. | Jobs 1 and 2 |
| 3 | 22 V. | A returning wagon is attacked under **Ursell**. The guards swear the beasts ran straight past them. | Job 3 |
| 4 | 23 V. | **Cart 12 overturns** on the mine road, unless the party checked it. Darnell is blamed again, and the Blackwoods are first on the scene. | — |
| 5 | 24 V. | Ursell and the Blackwoods row over who pays for guard hours. Ursell guards go unassigned, and the Blackwoods cover. That evening, "Halvar" signs in alone, unless the party stops it. | — |
| 6 | 25 V. | **Third cave-in.** Tressa Marl is dug out alive. *If the party caught the impostor at the gate, there's no fall; a bait attack hits a Valerion crew instead.* | Job 4 |
| 8 | 27 V. | **Ursell threatens to leave.** The Council wants the ore weights before the vote. | Job 5 |
| 10 | 29 V. | A bait attack at the village approach, still small creatures. The Blackwoods are the heroes of the hour. | — |
| 12 | 1 Borealis | **The Council vote.** | End of Copper |

}}

# The Village Diary

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-DIARY"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="NEW-COP-THIRD-FALL"></div>


### The Third Cave-In

<div class="swanchor" id="NEW-COP-THIRD-FALL"></div>


- **A — no interference:** Korgoth signs as “Halvar” at 18:52 on 24 Verdantis, after the twins leave. The fall is at 09:05 on the 25th; Tressa Marl survives. Use attendance leaf XX A and Incident 03. Korgoth drinks in the Last Lamp that night.
- **B — Korgoth caught or out of action:** Leif, out of the infirmary on light duty, uses an illusion scroll. He signs “Halver” at 21:10 after the lamp tally. Tobble was locking up; Elvira Evergreen sees him pass the mine-road barrier again. The fall still happens. Use leaf XX B and Incident 03.
- **C — the party watches the wicket on the 24th:** they catch Korgoth or Leif at the gate. No fall. Use leaf XX C, no Incident 03. On day 6, a small-creature bait attack hits a Valerion crew coming off shift, and the Blackwoods help first. The vote remains day 12.
Neither Korgoth nor Leif talks. Korgoth carries Notebook VI and the illusion trinket; its silence is Vashkar’s stage 1. Leif has no notebook, and the Blackwoods disown him. Handing either to the Ursell or Council watch is a real success, but not proof against the family. Early attendance enquiries through Tobble, Zoltan or Faldor can earn Branch C; Job 4 is normally offered after the fall.

{{swpilot-note
##### BRANCH-SPECIFIC HANDOUTS
Use only the matching leaf XX. The old Mythril Weighing Ledger and the old gate player sheet are retired. The current Job 5 ledger and gate roster v2 are in [the evidence index · p. 130](#p130).
}}

}}

# Copper into Silver

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-HANDOVER"></div>



<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0175"></div>


### The Council Vote: the End of Copper

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0176"></div>


- **If the party has brought their pattern to Faldor in time,** he and Raelyn use it to **block the vote**. The Blackwoods aren't exposed, but they don't get the escorts, and Ursell has a reason to stay.
- **If not, the vote passes.** The Blackwoods go into the Silver Chapter **controlling the escorts**, and the hunt is harder.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0177"></div>


Either way, the Silver Chapter begins.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0178"></div>


## Ranking Up: Silver

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0179"></div>


When the characters reach **level 5**, Faldor still won't hand over Silver Passes until **Scratch and Sniff has brought him something**: at least one real lead from the mining village.

<div class="swanchor" id="c16b0180"></div>


> *"Not yet. Prove me wrong."*

# Copper encounter desk

{{swpilot-flow

<div class="swanchor" id="COP-ENCOUNTERS"></div>



{{swpilot-note
##### EXISTING PARTY-SIZE RULES
The original encounter rules include a six-player adjustment for these tables: add two of the weakest creature listed. Do not automatically apply this table rule to named set-piece encounters unless their own instructions say so. The Prologue has its own goblin-count rule.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0002"></div>


*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0003"></div>


{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0004"></div>


All creatures are from the free rules (SRD 5.1) unless marked **(Bestiary)**. See *Bestiary* at the back of the book.

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0005"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0006"></div>

| Deadly | None. |

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0020"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0021"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0022"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0029"></div>


| Difficulty | Encounter |

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0030"></div>

<div class="swanchor" id="c21b0032"></div>

# The Silver Chapter

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-cover"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-022-selene-blackwood.webp" alt="Selene Blackwood"/></figure>

## Levels 5–6

A GM guide to the Blackwood hunt, dangerous discoveries and Selene’s invitation.

{{swpilot-small
Full-width facing-page review | Version 01 | GM only
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 133}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Silver at the table

{{swpilot-flow

The party now suspects the Blackwoods, and Faldor needs **proof**: names, and something with the family's hand on it. This chapter is the **hunt**: direct investigation, staying alive and discovering exactly how dangerous this family is. Alongside it, the region opens up: kobold caves, a banshee, a crypt and a girl looking for her ancestors.

It ends with a dinner invitation that nobody should accept, and everyone will.

### What's Really Going On

**GM only.** The Blackwoods have noticed they're being watched. While the party gathers proof, the family is already preparing **Plan B**: cutting Silverwood off from the outside world and taking it by force.

**If the council vote passed** at the end of Copper, the Blackwoods now control the escorts. Wagons, guards and gate duty are all theirs, which makes the party's work harder and the family bolder.

**Sariel keeps finding cover jobs** in Silver, just as in Copper, to keep the party close to the trail.

{{swpilot-note
##### THE CHAPTER’S THROUGH-LINE
**Starting state:** bring forward Copper’s actual council result, discovered evidence and current Guild credentials. The vote changes escort control; it does not automatically award a pass. **The hunt:** turn the pattern into named evidence. **Parallel work:** jobs, Lilith and the crypt can unfold alongside it.
}}

{{swpilot-note
##### KEEP DISCOVERY IN PLAY
**Who knows what:** Faldor needs proof. The courier is a hired runner who knows little. The contract clerk can name Selene when confronted with the evidence. Vashkar’s identity and Plan B are GM knowledge. Use the scene triggers; chapter rank alone does not deliver a speech.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 134}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Copper into Silver

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENTRY CHECK
Record what happened rather than assuming Copper succeeded. The source below distinguishes a council outcome from Silver Pass eligibility. Bring the real leads into the hunt.
}}

When the characters reach **level 5**, Faldor still won't hand over Silver Passes until **Scratch and Sniff has brought him something**: at least one real lead from the mining village.

| Carry-forward state | What to retain |
|:--|:--|
| Council transfer | Passed, or blocked by the party’s pattern |
| Escort control | Use the actual council result |
| Evidence | Marks, bait, ledger, rota, witnesses actually found |
| Credentials | Current level and actual Guild Pass |
| Linked character | Runner, Adopted Child, or the existing Mordai fallback |
| Town contacts | First meetings already delivered; current NPC states |
| Side threads | Open jobs, recovered tiles, Lilith and Vigilance if encountered |

### The Linked Character

One player character is tied to the Blackwoods, either raised in the household or caught up in its criminal business before running away (see *Session Zero*).

- **They don't know** the family is in Silverwood.
- **The butler spots them**, and the family's attention turns to them.
- **The party assumes the family just wants them home.** Right up until Selene's knife.

If no player takes the link, use **Mordai**, a Blackwood defector the party befriends, as a last resort.

The linked-character choices remain in [Session Zero, p. 38](#p38). The Copper closing spread remains at [p. 119](#p119).
}}

# Marks and the drop point

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### INVESTIGATION / ALTERNATIVE ROUTE
**When:** the party pursues the mining pattern. The marks, loose stone and courier are the existing route, with Mirella’s observation as backup. Do not give the courier the family’s whole plan. **Before this lead is pursued:** Sariel can continue source-supported cover work; no new daily countdown is added.

Courier statistics: use the source’s SRD spy. If killed or escaped, the dropped satchel still carries the bait, trap gem, spent illusion scroll, payments and ward-mark ink; Pryce is the next lead. Removing the courier alerts the family as written. Notebook handout release and GM meanings: [notebook pages · p. 141](#p141).
}}

{{note
##### If the council vote passed
With the Blackwoods running the escorts, their guards are everywhere in the village. Watching the drop point and questioning people is riskier, and the courier is better protected.
}}

## The Blackwood Hunt

In Copper, the party found a **pattern**. Now they need **proof**: names, and something with the family's hand on it. The hunt runs in roughly this order, but players can take it at their own pace, and each step has a backup.

}}

# Marks and the drop point

{{swpilot-flow

### Step 1: The Marks

The scratched marks from Copper are a **secret code**: a thieves' sign-language marking which wagons to hit, where payments are left and which guards can be bought.

- **Kethra**, the Guild librarian, can read them, for a price. So can a character with a criminal past, like the Blackwood-linked character.
- One mark, repeated on several wagons, points to a **drop point**: a hollow under a loose stone on the edge of the mining village.

*Backup:* Mirella's smoke cats have seen people coming and going from the same loose stone.

### Step 2: The Drop Point

Watching the drop point pays off. After a night or two, a **courier** (a **spy**, from the free rules) arrives to collect a packet.

**Catching him alive is best.** The party can ambush him, follow him or trick him. He's a hired runner, not family, and he knows very little: only the drop point, his payment, and that the packets go to **Edmund Pryce**, a clerk at Penhallow.

**His satchel** holds what can't travel by notebook: **two vials** of bait twisted together, a **trap gem**, a spent **illusion scroll**, a purse of payments and a pot of **ward-mark ink** (the marks that keep Mirella's cats away). No letters. The messages travel by notebook *(see The Six Notebooks)*.

- **If he's killed or escapes,** the satchel still drops, and the name Pryce is enough to move on.
- **Taking him has a cost.** When he doesn't come back, the Blackwoods know someone is onto them. That moves the family towards Step 4.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 138}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Witnesses and recognition

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### LEADS / RECOGNITION IS NOT A REPEAT GREETING
The bribed miner has three existing routes: tavern gossip, Tobble or Jory. The clerk’s confession follows confrontation with evidence. Vashkar’s recognition is a separate event that can eventually occur even if the party does not investigate.

The wanted notice is available only after Vashkar spots the linked character and the written delay has passed. Do not display it as a generic Silver job beforehand. The Runner and Adopted Child versions retain their different truth behind the alleged theft.
}}

### Step 3: The Bribed Miner

A miner in the village tavern is spending far beyond his wages: buying rounds, new boots, a silver ring. The party can find him through tavern gossip, through **Tobble**, or through **Jory Morrow**, who's noticed his new friend's money.

- Friendly drinking, intimidation or simple patience gets the truth out of him.
- He was paid to look the other way during a cave-in, and he names the man who paid him: the same **Penhallow contract clerk**.

**Pryce** is the knowing collaborator from Copper's list. His **notebook** (Notebook IV) is in his desk. Confronted with the courier, the notebook and the miner, he cracks, and names **Selene Blackwood**.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 139}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Witnesses and recognition

{{swpilot-flow

### Step 4: Spotted

At some point during the hunt, **Vashkar spots the Blackwood-linked character**: in the mining village, around town, or watching the drop point from the other side. **If the party never investigates, he still spots them eventually.**

From now on, the family knows exactly who is hunting them.

**The poster.** A few days later, a new notice goes up on the Guild board, with a description that matches the **linked character** exactly:

> *"Wanted alive. The Blackwoods have received information that a criminal known to be dangerous has been sighted in the area of Silverwood. [Description of the linked character.] They stole something of great value to the Blackwoods, and are the only one living who knows its location, so they must not be harmed. If you spot this individual, please notify Callum Dark at the Blackwood estate in Mithrilhold village. 100 gp for information that leads to their detainment."*

*If the linked character is the Runner (Session Zero), this is what they stole. If they're the Adopted Child, the "theft" is a lie to get them home.*

### Step 5: Proof to Faldor

With **Pryce's notebook**, the miner and the clerk's confession, the party has **proof**. Any other notebooks they've found make the case stronger. See *How Silver Ends*.

}}

# The Six Notebooks — GM meanings

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### SPOILER BOUNDARY / HANDOUT CONDITIONS
**Copper / Silver handouts:** the six agent notebook pages and optional burnt scraps, earned through investigation. A captured notebook shows only its current page. **Gold handout:** the unchanged lodgings letters, found later in the empty Blackwood rooms. GM meaning tables are not automatic player revelations.
}}

### The Six Notebooks

**Vashkar** keeps six small notebooks in a chest. Each agent holds the twin of one: whatever is written in one appears in the other, within about 15 miles. Agents burn each page when it's full, so a captured notebook only shows the **page in use**. Vashkar never signs. **No agent knows the others exist.** Blackwood agents write in heavy riddles; bought outsiders use simple code words. Hand over the pages and let the players **decode them themselves**. The notebook pages and the GM key are in the Handout Pack.

**The key is in plain sight.** Each mining house is named by its **crest** (see *The Mining Houses*): the horse and cart is **Farman**, the pickaxe and hammer is **Valerion**, the bear is **Ursell**, and the **tree, roots, branches, saplings and timber** are the **Blackwoods** themselves. "Blades" and "candles" are adventurers.

**If they get stuck,** prompt them to take the pages to **Omega Oliver** in the Shaft and Pickaxe. He loves a riddle. For a drink, he'll give them **one hint** per page, never the whole answer.

The courier's notebooks replace the old satchel letters. The letters left behind in the **empty Blackwood lodgings** in the Gold Chapter are unchanged. The notebook meanings below are for the GM.

}}

**The notebooks**

| Notebook | Holder | Where it's found | What it shows |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| I. The Hearth | Darian Gray | His room, Shaft and Pickaxe | Someone reports on the party |
| II. Horse and Cart | Wendel Hask | Farman depot | Sells wagon times; "the tree takes the second" is attacked wagon F |
| III. The Bear | Corra Lusk | Ursell office | Unsigned shift swaps, for a debt |
| IV. The Ledger | Edmund Pryce | Penhallow office | The bribed miner, the Valerion blackmail plan, the weights. **The proof.** |
| V. The Watch | Callum Dark | Blackwood security post, or the lodgings (Gold) | Bait, Mirella's wards, Selene being sent |
| VI. The False Miner | Korgoth Darkblade | His coat, in the Last Lamp | The borrowed face and the cave-ins |

**Burnt scraps** of Callum's old pages can be found in the culvert below Mithrilhold's lower road. They're optional, never compulsory.

**Vashkar notices** in four stages: a missing answer, a wrong reply, several notebooks going silent, then confirmation. He learns that someone is hunting the network, **not who**. Players who write in a captured notebook must match that agent's voice.

# The mining village desk

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST VISIT / WORKING REFERENCE
Use the hillside description for first arrival only if the party has not already visited. Everything below is a GM reference; house responsibility, guards, rumours and what a witness knows are different kinds of information. Do not read the whole desk aloud.
}}

*South of Silverwood, the new mining village of **Mithrilhold** sprawls across the hillside: tents, timber halls, smoke and noise. Six great houses have staked their claims here, each with its own guards, banners and ambitions. Most of them just want to get rich. One of them wants everything.*

### Who Lives Here

- **Independent miners:** a shrinking number, like the dwarf twins **Hilda and Halvar**. They own their tools, rent their rooms, buy their own supplies and sell their own mythril. Every bad week pushes a few more of them out.
- **House miners:** the growing majority. Paid wages by a mining house, and often housed and fed by it too.
- **Village businesses:** boarding houses, taverns, merchants and healers, increasingly competing with the services the houses provide for their own workers.

The mines themselves are a mix: an old shared network of tunnels, and newer shafts and claims owned by individual houses.

### How the Mythril Moves

South of the village are the **old silver caves**, and through those caves lies the **new mythril mine** and the **base camp**, where every miner hands in their mythril for weighing and taxation. It's then taken under guard to the mining village every second day.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 145}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The mining village desk

{{swpilot-flow

| Stage | What happens | Who runs it |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Housing and hiring** | Miners are hired, housed and paid. | The houses, and private landlords |
| **Supplies** | Food, water, timber, medicine and goods come in. | Farman, merchants and the houses |
| **Equipment** | Tools, wagon parts, lifts and mine supports. | Darnell |
| **Transport** | Wagons carry workers and supplies to the mines and back. | Farman, with shared escorts |
| **Mine safety** | Sign-in, shifts, inspections and digging. | Each mine's owner, especially Valerion |
| **Protection** | Gates, patrols, escorts and keeping monsters down. | Ursell, and hired guards |
| **Weighing and tax** | Every load is weighed at the base camp, and the Lord of Silverwood takes **10%**. | The mine owners and the Lord's tax keepers |
| **Processing** | Mythril is sorted, processed and stored. | Valerion |
| **Contracts** | Prices, buyers and agreements. | Penhallow, and each house |
| **Export** | Mythril goes out through Silverwood; the same wagons bring supplies back. | Farman |

### The Mining Council

The town's guard has no authority in the mining village. Shared matters are decided by an **eight-member council**:

- **One representative from each of the six houses**
- **Faldor**, for the Guild
- **Captain Raelyn Rivers**, for the town guard

Decisions are made by **majority vote**. The council hands out shared jobs (escorts, gate duty, repairs) to particular houses.

**The key rule:** a house given a job can **swap it, share it or hire someone else to do it.** But **the house that was given the job still answers for it** if it goes wrong. Much of this swapping is agreed privately, so the council often doesn't know who is really doing what.

}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The mining village desk

{{swpilot-flow

### The Six Houses

| House | Trade | Insignia |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Valerion** | The oldest mining family. Experts in extraction and processing, and hired by others to oversee their mines. | Crossed pickaxe and hammer on a diagonal silver and black shield |
| **Blackwood** | Newly risen. Found a rich new vein and are expanding fast. Generous, protective and *very* helpful. | A tree with deep roots and spreading branches |
| **Darnell** | Makers of high-quality mining tools and equipment. | Crossed pickaxe and shovel on a red and grey shield |
| **Penhallow** | Brokers and dealmakers with contacts everywhere. | Interlinked rings |
| **Farman** | Food, transport and supplies. Runs the caravans. | A horse-drawn cart on a blue and green shield |
| **Ursell** | Professional security. Their fighters guard mines, caravans and anyone who pays. | A bear's head on a silver and black shield |

{{note
##### Silverwood and Blackwood
Both the town and the Blackwoods use a tree on their badges. That's deliberate. The Blackwoods present themselves as Silverwood's protectors, and they look the part. The difference is in the roots.
}}

}}

# The mining village desk

{{swpilot-flow

### What's Going Wrong

Every house has a problem in its own area, and they're starting to blame each other.

| House | The trouble | What the Blackwoods offer |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Valerion** | Cave-ins are getting worse, some near brand-new supports. No deaths, yet. Old miners are questioning the family. | Extra inspection teams, and to oversee shared tunnels |
| **Darnell** | Broken wagons, failing tools, missing parts and a growing repair backlog. | Their own workshops, workers and equipment loans |
| **Farman** | Missing stock, unexplained surplus and late deliveries. | Extra wagons and warehouse space |
| **Ursell** | Returning wagons attacked, gates threatened, patrols failing. **Ursell is close to leaving Silverwood.** | Replacement patrols, escorts and gate guards |
| **Penhallow** | Disputed contracts and invoices. Others accuse them of profiting from the confusion. | Help settling the disputes, in return for "preferred supplier" deals |

{{note
##### Ursell is worth saving
Ursell isn't corrupt. Its guards are doing their best, being blamed for failures they can't explain and losing their friends' trust. Give the players a reason to **defend** them, not just suspect them.
}}

**Other trouble brewing:** rising rents in crowded lodgings, overwhelmed healers, late or short wages, flooded workings, bad timber for supports, sick draft animals, crumbling shared roads and ramps, and disputes over who owns new mythril claims. Any of these can become another way in for the Blackwoods.

}}

# Hunt: the Kobold Caves

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENCOUNTER DESK
The traps and five-area sequence below are the written encounter, not a newly redrawn dungeon. Keep escaped kobolds available for the den. [Trapmaster statistics · p. 188](#p188) and [the cave map](#p194) are direct references.
}}

*Silver Pass, ★*

> *"Kobolds are now attacking the base camp in the deep. Two teams of either 3 or 4 kobolds have been spotted attacking miners and setting traps. Guards are stretched keeping the camp safe. Adventurers will need an understanding of traps. 1,000 gp for stopping the kobolds, and 1,000 gp if you find a way to stop them entering the deep."*

The kobolds from the Copper Chapter came back with friends. They've dug into old tunnels near the mythril deep and are raiding the base camp.

These kobolds don't fight fair. They fight with **traps**: snares, pits, falling rocks and **oil-soaked tunnels they set alight**. There aren't many of them, but every corridor is dangerous.

{{note
##### What this teaches
- **Search for traps.** Players who walk in face-first will learn fast.
- **Know when to run.** A burning tunnel is no place to stand and fight. Retreating is a valid choice.
}}

**The caves.** Four stretches of tunnel, then the den. Spotting a trap takes a **DC 14 Wisdom (Perception)** check; disarming one takes a **DC 14 Dexterity** check with thieves' tools.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 152}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Hunt: the Kobold Caves

{{swpilot-flow

| Area | Trap | Kobolds |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **1. The entrance** | **Snares.** DC 14 Dexterity save or be hoisted upside down and restrained (escape DC 14, or cut the rope). | 2 **kobolds** with slings, who run deeper once spotted |
| **2. The pit hall** | **Covered pits,** 20 feet deep with spikes. DC 15 Dexterity save or fall: 7 (2d6) bludgeoning plus 11 (2d10) piercing damage. | 2 **winged kobolds** dropping stones from above |
| **3. The rockfall** | **Falling rocks.** A kobold cuts a rope: everyone in a 15-foot square makes a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 22 (4d10) bludgeoning damage on a failure, or half on a success. | 2 **kobolds** on the rope |
| **4. The oil tunnel** | **Fire.** The floor is soaked in oil. When a kobold throws a torch, the tunnel burns for 1 minute: a creature that enters it or starts its turn there makes a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 16 (3d10) fire damage on a failure, or half on a success. Thick smoke fills the tunnel. | 2 **winged kobolds** with torches |
| **5. The den** | Every trap they have left. | **The kobold trapmaster** (Bestiary), **2 kobolds** and whoever escaped from earlier |

**What's in the den:** stolen mining supplies (lamp oil, rope, timber and tools from several houses), which the Guild returns to their owners for a small reward.

*Difficulty: medium for four level 5 characters on its own, hard with the traps.*

**Handing it in** *(The miners' foreman)*
- **Done:** *"No more fire in the tunnels! You've earned every coin."*
- **Failed:** *"They burned another tunnel last night. We need someone who knows traps."*

}}

# Dragon’s Obelisk: the banshee

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### MOON / ALTERNATE RESOLUTION
Check the actual moon before choosing conversation or hostility. The bard, Mirella, mining memorials and Drogan supply existing clue routes. The paired rings and curse are preserved exactly. The area table also has a full-moon exception.
}}

### Exorcism at Dragon's Obelisk

*Silver Pass, ★★*

> *"Experienced adventurers only. Some evenings, a banshee can be found around the Dragon Obelisk. Extreme caution is advised if you intend to approach. Only those brave of heart and strong of mind should take on this task. She has been known to fell the strongest with a single scream. 1,100 gp to the party who can exorcise this banshee."*

A **banshee** haunts Dragon's Obelisk, a cliff north-west of town, past the Xanthos Crypt. Her wail has killed travellers.

**The story.** Generations ago, a legendary blacksmith's daughter loved a young miner. When she heard he'd died in a cave-in, she threw herself from the Obelisk. But he had survived. When he learned what she'd done, he took his own life in grief. Each wore a ring her father made for them.

**The key: the moon.**
- **At full moon,** she can be talked to. Show her the truth, his ring or his grave, and she gives up her own ring and finally moves on.
- **At any other time,** she's a hostile banshee whose wail can kill.

**His grave** is in the old **miners' graveyard**, on the road out to the Obelisk. Its stone is carved with a broken heart.

**Clues to the story:** the bard's songs, Mirella's riddles, the memorials around the mines and Drogan's memories of the old smith.

**Reward:** the **Ethereal Union Rings**, the two rings her father made. Each wearer must attune.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 154}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Dragon’s Obelisk: the banshee

{{swpilot-flow

- **Ring of the Blacksmith's Daughter.** Silver, set with a small ruby and engraved with hammers and anvils. Once per day, as an action, the wearer summons a spectral hammer that deals force damage equal to their level to a target within 30 feet. While within 10 feet of the other ring's wearer, they gain a +2 bonus to AC.
- **Ring of the Miner's Son.** Gold, set with a small emerald and engraved with pickaxes and shovels. Once per day, as an action, the wearer summons a spectral pickaxe that deals force damage equal to their level to a target within 30 feet. While within 10 feet of the other ring's wearer, they have resistance to fire damage.
- **Shared Protection.** Both wearers have resistance to necrotic damage. When one makes a saving throw to take half damage, the other (within 30 feet) also takes only half. Within 30 feet of each other, both have advantage on saves against being charmed or frightened. Once per day, they can use an action to **swap places**, as long as they're on the same plane.
- **Curse.** If one wearer dies wearing their ring, the other makes a DC 15 Wisdom save. On a failure, they're consumed by grief: disadvantage on Wisdom and Charisma checks for 24 hours, and anyone reading their thoughts must pass a DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) check or believe they're a banshee. If the dead partner isn't brought back within 7 days, the survivor makes another DC 15 Wisdom save; on a failure, their mind breaks for good, and they become an NPC under the GM's control.

{{note
##### Content note
This story involves suicide. Handle it gently, and keep it off-screen for tables that prefer.
}}

**Handing it in** *(Alexander, for the Hallowed Halls)*
- **Done:** *"She's at rest. Orathen keeps the scales. Thank you for being gentle with her."*
- **Failed:** *"She still wails at night. Some souls need more than force."*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 155}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# The orc commander

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### APPROACH / PARTS OR WHOLE
The source allows patrol, camp and commander encounters separately. All combined is deadly. Gorgrak’s mishandling of Vigilance occurs as written; it is not the sword’s normal player power. The existing bard’s riddle is referenced in [p. 94](#p94).
}}

### Wanted: the Orc Commander

*Silver Pass, ★*

> *"An orc commander has appeared in the north-west, past the crypt of the Xanthos. Scouts report more than 10 orcs, with at least 1 able to access the arcane arts. Suitable parties with healers and defence against the arts only. 1,000 gp for the orc commander's head, and 10 sp per pair of orc tusks."*

**The war band** has made camp in the wild country beyond the crypt, on the road to the Obelisk. Like the goblin camp, it can be taken in parts:

| Part | Who's there | Difficulty (level 5) |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **The patrol** | **4 orcs** | Easy |
| **The camp** | **6 orcs** and **an orc shaman** (use the **cult fanatic**) | Medium |
| **The commander's tent** | **The orc commander** (use the **gladiator**) and **2 orcs** | Hard |

All at once, it's **deadly**: the poster means it about healers.

**The commander** is **Gorgrak Skullcrusher**, and he fights with a greatsword that hates him. It's **Vigilance**, and it won't work for him: in his hands it's just a greatsword, and **once per fight it twists in his grip**, turning one of his hits into a miss. Omega Oliver's riddle (*The Shaft and Pickaxe*) points the party here.

**Handing it in** *(Sariel)*
- **Done:** *"Gorgrak Skullcrusher. I'll write it in the ledger: closed. And... is that sword talking?"*
- **Failed:** *"Still out there? Then the north road stays shut. Faldor will want to talk to you."*

{{swpilot-small
On recovery, use [Vigilance’s attunement, memories and pass progression · p. 158](#p158). The approved Orc Swamp Camp remains a map reference at [p. 198](#p198); its geographic continuity with this encounter is flagged separately for author review.
}}

**The riddle of the sword.** Once the party has heard of the orc commander (Silver Chapter), Omega has a riddle for them:

}}

# The orc commander

{{swpilot-flow

{{descriptive
In a lair of green, where orcs do thrive,
A weapon of power, but not yet alive.
With a name that tells of its deadly aim,
It longs to be wielded, to feel the same.

The sword was once free, in the hands of a hero,
But now it is bound to a new master's ego.
The orc chief holds it, but it does not comply,
For it yearns for a warrior with a righteous eye.

In the orc commander's grasp you'll find
A blade of legend, strong and fine.
But heed my words, and take great care,
For that greatsword holds a hateful glare.

Its name is Vigilance, make no mistake.
It longs for justice, but hates to take.
In the hands of evil, it broods and seethes,
Its magic twisted, its will bereaved.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 157}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Vigilance

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-037-isolde.webp" alt="Isolde"/><figcaption>Isolde<br/>Reference edition p. 157</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### ITEM / ACTUAL TRIGGERS
**First attunement:** the first dream. **Later memories:** new passes and protective actions, as written. Later-rank powers remain gated by the table; they are not granted early.
}}

*Weapon (greatsword), sentient, requires attunement.* Vigilance **grows with its wielder**: it gets stronger as they rank up, so a player who loves their sword never has to swap it. *(Groups using* Arcane Forge *can upgrade it further with that book's rules.)*

**Her story.** In the distant realm of Eldoria, a group of valiant adventurers known as **the Order of the Crimson Light** dedicated their lives to defending the innocent. Among them were a courageous paladin named **Isolde** and her beloved husband, **Roderick**, both sworn to **Veyrath**.

The Order was summoned to face a fearsome **red dragon** terrorising the town of **Emberholme**. The battle was fierce, and the Order was gradually overwhelmed. As it reached its climax, a devastating blow struck Isolde. Knowing her time was short, she begged Veyrath to spare her husband. Veyrath offered her a choice: give up her mortal form and become an instrument of protection. She accepted, and her body became a magnificent greatsword. With it, Roderick slew the dragon.

Roderick stayed in Emberholme and raised a **monument** to the fallen, with Vigilance encased in its stone. Centuries passed, and the monument fell into ruin. A treasure hunter, ignorant of what it was, pulled the sword free and sold it to an orc commander, Gorgrak Skullcrusher.

**Personality.** Vigilance is Isolde: warm, fierce and fiercely protective. Her purpose is to **protect loved ones**. She has Intelligence 12, Wisdom 14 and Charisma 16, and sees and hears out to 60 feet.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 158}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Vigilance

{{swpilot-flow

**Growing with the wielder.**

| Wielder's pass | Vigilance |
|:--|:--|
| **Silver** *(on attunement)* | **+1 greatsword.** She shares **feelings** with her wielder: warmth, worry, anger. |
| **Gold** | **+2 greatsword.** She **speaks** telepathically. **Guardian:** when a creature you can see attacks an ally within 5 feet of you, you can use your reaction to give the attack disadvantage. |
| **Platinum** | Her strikes deal an extra **1d8 radiant damage** against fiends and undead. |
| **Mithril** | **+3 greatsword.** **Isolde's Choice** (once per long rest): when an ally within 30 feet would drop to 0 hit points, use your reaction to leave them at 1 hit point. You take the damage instead. |

**Memories.** When the wielder first attunes, they dream. After that, Vigilance reveals one more memory **each time they gain a new pass**, and whenever they risk themselves to protect someone they love.

}}

# Vigilance

{{swpilot-flow

| # | Memory |
|:--:|:--|
| 1 | Isolde and Roderick swearing their oaths to Veyrath and joining the Order of the Crimson Light. |
| 2 | The brave charge of the Order, clashing against the dragon's fiery breath. |
| 3 | Isolde standing resolute, shielding her husband from the dragon's onslaught. |
| 4 | Isolde's desperate prayer, accepting Veyrath's offer, her body becoming Vigilance. |
| 5 | Roderick's grief and determination as he raises the monument in Emberholme. |
| 6 | The passing of time, the monument crumbling, Vigilance trapped in the stone. |
| 7 | The treasure hunter, and the orc commander Gorgrak Skullcrusher. |

**When they clash.** If the wielder abandons their friends, puts loved ones in danger for gain, or harms the innocent, Vigilance resists. The wielder makes a **DC 14 Charisma saving throw**. On a failure, their attacks with her have **disadvantage** until they make it right, or until she forgives them after a long rest.

{{note
##### A one-shot for later
Vigilance ends where it began. In a spin-off one-shot, the party carries her back to the ruined monument in **Emberholme**, and chooses whether to lay Isolde's soul to rest. Losing the sword is the price of setting her free.
}}

}}

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note

Kill the rider and the ogre goes wild; kill the hexer and bane ends. Keep the named encounter counts. Statistics: [goblin rider · p. 186](#p186) and [goblin hexer · p. 187](#p187).

*Silver Pass, ★*

> *"One of our adventurers has been killed in the field. His Guild pass notified us that its detect life had failed, and sent us his location. A scout has confirmed the death, but was unable to retrieve the remains due to a high beast active in the area. Please return our guildmate.*
>
> *Name: Eadric Stonefist. Description: a tall, muscular human with weathered skin and a thick beard. He wears worn leather armour and carries a longbow and a quiver of arrows on his back, along with a pair of sturdy hunting knives at his belt. Please find his items, so we can return everything to his grieving family."*

**What happened.** He was crushed by an **ogre**, steered by a **goblin rider** using hooks through its nose and arms. The goblins are still there.

**The goblins fight smart:** blowguns, short bows, clubs and blades, plus a small goblin spellcaster casting ***bane*** on the party.

# Eadric Stonefist: remains retrieval

{{swpilot-flow

{{note
##### What this teaches
Goblins with tactics are far more dangerous than goblins in a line. Kill the **hexer**, and the *bane* ends. Kill the **rider**, and the ogre goes wild, attacking whatever's nearest, goblins included.
}}

**The fight.** A rocky hollow where the Guild member fell, his body still under the rubble.

| Who | Where |
|:--|:--|
| **An ogre** with **a goblin rider** (Bestiary) | In the open, guarding the body |
| **A goblin hexer** (Bestiary) | Behind cover at the back, casting *bane* on the party |
| **8 goblins** | Spread around the rocks, with shortbows and blowguns |

*Difficulty: hard for four level 5 characters, but not deadly. The goblins flee once half of them are down.*

**The remains.** The Guild member's pass is still around his neck. Bring both home, and Sariel pays the reward.

**Handing it in** *(Sariel)*
- **Done:** *"You brought him home. His family will thank you. I'll thank you now."*
- **Failed:** *"Then he stays out there a little longer. We'll send someone else."*

# Silver gathering work

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### CHOICE / LOCATION AND HAND-IN CONDITIONS
Offer only the work currently on the board. Keep the Feywoods danger warning, bare-hand Dreamroot effect, night-opening flowers, storm timing and paired poison/cure requirement. These are separate jobs, not a mandatory route.

### Silver Gathering Jobs

### Gathering: Dreams for Mirella

*Silver Pass, ★★*

> *"Mindleaf and Dreamroot, from the edge of the Feywoods. Wear gloves for the root, unless you'd like a nap in the forest. Don't go further in than you have to. I mean it."* — Mirella

**Where:** The edge of the Feywoods.

**Mindleaf** *(psychic, Uncommon)* protects against charm and fear. **Dreamroot** *(psychic, Rare)* brings prophetic dreams, and puts anyone who touches it bare-handed to sleep for 1d4 hours. The Feywoods are still dangerous at Silver.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Mirella)*
- **Done:** *"You didn't fall asleep in the woods. I'm impressed. My cat isn't, but she never is."*
- **Failed:** *"Dreamroot, bare-handed? You slept for how long? Oh, sweetheart."*

### Gathering: Grave Flowers

*Silver Pass, ★*

> *"Gravepetal and Corpsebloom are growing in the miners' graveyard again. They feed the restless dead. Bring us every one you find, so we can burn them on the altar."* — Alexander

**Where:** The miners' graveyard, on the road to the Obelisk. Best at night, when they open.

**Gravepetal** *(necrotic, Uncommon)* frightens. **Corpsebloom** *(necrotic, Rare)* heals the undead and harms the living, and stinks. The Halls pay the full rate even though they burn them. Ampersand would pay more. Whether the party sells to him instead is up to them.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Alexander)*
- **Done:** *"Every one? Then the dead will sleep easier. Come, watch them burn."*
- **Failed:** *"Some are still out there. We'll pray, and post it again."*

}}

# Silver gathering work

{{swpilot-flow

### Gathering: Storm Harvest

*Silver Pass, ★★*

> *"Ampersand needs Boltberries, Thunderblooms and Arcvine from the High Shelves, yes yes! Do not squeeze the berries. Do not let the vine touch you. Ampersand pays double for the vine if you still have both eyebrows."* — Ampersand

**Where:** The High Shelves, during or just after a storm.

**Boltberry** and **Thunderbloom** *(lightning, Uncommon)* explode if handled badly. **Arcvine** *(lightning, Rare)* chains lightning between targets, and lashes the harvester. Use the **High Shelves** encounter table.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Ampersand)*
- **Done:** *"Both eyebrows! BOTH! Ampersand pays double, as promised, yes yes!"*
- **Failed:** *"Ampersand sees no eyebrows and no Arcvine. Ampersand sees disappointment."*

### Gathering: Poison and Cure

*Silver Pass, ★*

> *"Needlethorn for blade coatings, Nightfang Root for the cure. Same swamp, same day. Bring both or don't bother."* — Kiera, for Ironclad's

**Where:** The swamps and wild forest.

**Needlethorn** *(poison, Rare)* adds poison to a weapon. **Nightfang Root** *(poison, Uncommon)* is brewed into antivenom. Ironclad's sells coated blades with an antidote included, which Drogan considers good manners.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Kiera)*
- **Done:** *"Both. Good. Drogan will be pleased, and he's never pleased."*
- **Failed:** *"One without the other is no use to me. Bring both."*

> *"One griffon egg. Unbroken. 600 gp. The mother will object. That's why it's 600 gp."* — Tormund

**Where:** A griffon nest on the High Shelves.

A **griffon** nest with **2 griffons** nearby, and a storm rolling in. Stealth, distraction or a fight all work. A hatched griffon can be trained, which Tormund knows very well.

**Where:** The swamps, or the roads at night.

# Lilith Valtair and the Crypt

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-027-lilith-valtair.webp" alt="Lilith Valtair"/><figcaption>Lilith Valtair<br/>Reference edition p. 152</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST CONTACT / GM TRUTH SEPARATE
**First tavern arrival, once:** use her visible manner and odd questions; reveal the pearl only at the stated passive Perception threshold. Her ancestry and plan are GM knowledge until learned. Later visits use freeform portrayal.
}}

### The Shifty Stranger

**Lilith Valtair** *(tiefling, about twenty: barely an adult by tiefling standards).* Her arrival in the tavern early in the chapter is noticeable; how noticeable is up to the GM. She's jittery, doesn't like to talk, and nurses a badly healed cut on her forearm. She asks odd questions about the old crypt and the town's founders. A character with a **passive Perception of 20 or more** spots a **black pearl** on a silver chain at her neck.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Lilith Valtair and the Crypt

{{swpilot-flow

She often **pays the bard** for old tales, and the whole tavern hears them. One of her favourites:

> *In a tomb of five, where warriors rest,*
> *Paths of danger, all put to the test.*
> *The walls are stone, but not all is as it seems;*
> *The way ahead leads only to deathly dreams.*
>
> *To find the treasure, seek the path unseen,*
> *Through walls that whisper, with secrets keen.*
> *The warriors five, their watchful eyes will guide,*
> *But do not be fooled: their power has not died.*

{{note
##### Why she matters
Lilith teaches players that **the bard knows things**. Her requests steer his songs towards the founders and the crypt, and players listening in learn too.
}}

**The truth.** Lilith is a descendant of **Saraphina**, one of Silverwood's founders. She has taken one of her family's black pearls and come to find her ancestor's resting place, and what her family left behind in the founders' tomb. **In the hands of Valtair blood, the pearl can animate the dead with no time limit.** Her plan is to raise the crypt's dead and ask them how to get through the tomb's door.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 168}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Lilith’s disappearance

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-028-tarquin-valtair.webp" alt="Tarquin Valtair"/><figcaption>Tarquin Valtair<br/>Reference edition p. 153</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### LATER EVENT / TWO WRITTEN OUTCOMES
The wanted notice follows her disappearance later in the chapter. Tarquin’s agenda is not the same as a neutral rescue request. The prompt rescue and ignored-hook routes have different outcomes; do not award the living-rescue ending automatically.
}}

### Her Brother

**Tarquin Valtair** *(tiefling).* Rich, snooty, weak and surrounded by bodyguards. He thinks money means people do what he says. He has posted a **wanted notice** for his sister on the Guild board: *wanted alive, for questioning about a theft from the Valtair family, 1,000 gp for her capture.* He doesn't say the theft is a black pearl.

He wants her back, but what he *really* wants is the **pearl**.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 169}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Lilith’s disappearance

{{swpilot-flow

### Missing: Lilith Valtair

Later in the chapter, Lilith stops coming to the tavern. Her brother's notice goes up. She went to the **catacombs of the Xanthos Crypt** at night (see *The Silverwood Region*) and used the pearl. But it was **Vartius's sabotaged pearl**: near the founders' tomb, it doesn't raise one obedient corpse. It wakes **the catacombs' dead**, and they turn on her.

**Players who did the hairpin or gathering jobs already know the way.** Others can learn it from the Guild, the bard or Lilith's brother.

**If the party goes after her (the best route):**
- They fight through the crypt's restless dead and find Lilith alive but hurt.
- She tells them her story: her ancestor Saraphina, the founders and why she came.
- She carries **some of Saraphina's belongings**, including a pouch of **14 stone tiles** for the **crypt puzzle**.
- Her brother pays the **1,000 gp** reward. He tries to haggle it down because she's "damaged". He is a terrible person.

**If the party ignores it (the backup route):**
- Her brother goes himself. He comes back hurt, alone, his bodyguards gone, and begs the adventurers to help.
- The **abomination** is still there. When the party reaches her, **Lilith is dead.**
- Once the abomination falls, her brother grabs the **pearl** from her body and runs. That's all he ever wanted.
- The party finds **Saraphina's belongings** on her body, including the pouch of **14 stone tiles**.

Either way, the party ends up with what they need for the **founders' trail**.

{{note
##### The crypt
The catacombs can be explored now. The sealed **Tomb of the Founders** cannot be entered until the party solves the **crypt puzzle** (Platinum Chapter).
}}

{{swpilot-note
##### AFTERMATH / PLATINUM TRAIL
Record Lilith’s fate, who holds the pearl and the recovered 14 tiles. The catacombs can be explored; this is not an early opening of the sealed founders’ tomb. [The abomination encounter · p. 171](#p171) follows.
}}

}}

# The catacomb abomination

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENCOUNTER / ENTRY AND AFTERMATH
The narrow walkways and wide chamber are tactical constraints, not a new battle map. Run the two written lesser-undead groups on approach, then the abomination. Its death ends the pearl-raised undead as stated. [Creature statistics · p. 189](#p189) are included; [the approved catacomb map](#p196) remains a separate GM reference.
}}

### The Abomination

The sabotaged pearl didn't raise one corpse. It **fused dozens of the catacombs' dead into one creature**: a heaving mass of bodies, limbs and skulls.

**It's too big for the walkways.** The catacomb walkways are only one person wide, so the abomination is stuck in one of the **wide chambers**. Lilith fled into a narrow walkway, where it can't follow, but it can reach. It claws and grabs at her through the opening, and she's running out of places to back into.

**To save her, the party has to get past it,** which means fighting their way **single file** down a narrow walkway, then into the chamber to finish it.

- **Lesser undead** wander the walkways on the way in: first **4 skeletons** and **2 zombies**, then **2 ghouls** and **4 zombies**. Each group is easy on its own, but it wears the party down.
- The abomination can only reach one or two people in the walkways, but **in the chamber, everyone is in reach**.
- When it dies, the pearl's magic breaks and the remaining dead fall still.

{{note
##### The narrow walkways
The walkways cut both ways: the abomination can't get in, but the party can't all fight at once either. They'll have to decide who goes first, and how to get into the chamber.

**Stat block:** the **catacomb abomination** (Bestiary). It's a hard fight for four level 5 characters.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 171}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY}}

# Proof, convoy and invitation

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### STORY TRIGGERS / NOT RANK ALONE
**Intended Silver finale:** Selene’s dinner. Faldor’s line requires proof brought to him. The late weekly food convoy is Plan B, not an ordinary delay. The invitation follows the source sequence, with Vashkar having recognised the linked character. Do not use Selene’s dinner speech during an unrelated visit.
}}

## How Silver Ends

### 1. "Leave it with me"

When the party brings their proof to **Faldor**:

> *"Good work. Now stay out of it. You poke the Blackwoods, they bite. Leave it with me."*

### 2. A late convoy

The **weekly food convoy is late**. It looks like a normal delay. It isn't. The Blackwoods have quietly started **Plan B**.

### 3. The family moves

**Vashkar** has already spotted the Blackwood-linked character (Step 4 of the hunt). He appears to be an ordinary, elderly human servant. He is not. With the party closing in, he and Selene decide to deal with them.

### 4. The invitation

A beautifully written invitation arrives: **Selene Blackwood** would be honoured to host the party for dinner, in gratitude for their service to the mining village.

It's a trap. It always was.

{{swpilot-small
Dinner cast and encounter: [Selene’s dinner · p. 175](#p175). Aftermath: [council, pillow note and tavern attack · p. 178](#p178).
}}

}}

# The hosts: GM portrayal

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-023-vashkar.webp" alt="Vashkar"/><figcaption>Vashkar<br/>Reference edition p. 107</figcaption></figure>

### Who Runs the Conspiracy

**Selene and Vashkar organise it together.**

- **Selene** is in charge on the ground, on Belial's orders: *secure Silverwood.* She is the public face: generous, gracious and always first to help.
- **Vashkar** is the planner at her side. He decides which problems to create, which to make worse and which to simply take advantage of, and he keeps the whole thing from falling apart. As Hell's handler, he is also steering it towards Lord Xanthos.

The rest of the family carry out their parts:
- **Andras:** the money and the contracts.
- **Azazel and Malphas:** the muscle: the attacks, the bait and the threats.

### Selene

Elegant, gracious and impeccably mannered. You'd trust her to host a princess. She is also the most dangerous person in the family, because she enjoys it.

**Says:** *"Oh, darling. Don't look so worried. It's only dinner."*

**Her orders, as she sees them.** Belial told her to secure Silverwood. When she spotted the linked character, he told her to deliver a message: *come home, the family has work for you.* She delivered it. But he never actually said **not to kill them**, so she decided she could.

**In a fight,** she lashes out with a **whip of crackling magic**: part warlock, part street fighter.

{{note
##### Her stat block
Selene's stat block is in the Bestiary: part warlock, part street fighter. Every Blackwood is there: **Azazel**, **Selene**, **Andras**, **Malphas** and **Vashkar**.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 173}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The hosts: GM portrayal

{{swpilot-flow

### Vashkar

An elderly, impeccably polite human butler, who has served the Blackwoods for longer than anyone can remember. He is really a **rakshasa**, Hell's handler inside the family.

- He watches Selene's madness calmly, because he's seen it all before.
- He only steps in to **protect an asset**: Selene at the dinner, and Selene again in the battle.
- Ordinary magic barely touches him. It takes **Mirella's prism** to bring him down.
- Killed outside the Hells, he simply **returns there** in time.

**Says:** *"Will that be all, my lady?"*

{{swpilot-note
##### KNOWLEDGE BOUNDARY
**Player-facing appearance:** the elderly, impeccably polite human butler. **GM-only truth:** rakshasa and infernal handler. His dinner behaviour follows its event instructions; his identity is revealed later in Gold. Do not announce that identity merely because this reference names it.
}}

# Selene’s dinner

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/art-4b9394d63d-selene-dinner.webp" alt="Selene dinner"/><figcaption>Selene dinner<br/>Reference edition p. 158</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### ARRIVAL → DESSERT → AMBUSH → ESCAPE
**When:** the party actually arrives for the accepted invitation. Use the arrival passage once. **Over dessert:** the written speech addresses the linked character; the ambush follows it. **When Selene starts losing:** run the butler’s written fireball and escape sequence. The source’s scripted escape and survival safeguard remain unchanged.
}}

*For the guests' arrival at Selene's dinner, before her speech:*

{{descriptive
Candlelight runs gold along polished timber. The table is laid for you: roast meats, fresh bread and silver cutlery that gleams. A servant stands behind every chair, eyes lowered, and two more wait by the doors. Selene Blackwood rises to greet you, every inch the perfect host. "Our heroes," she says warmly, and takes your hands one by one. In the corner, an elderly butler inclines his head, and doesn't blink.
}}

**Selene Blackwood** *(tiefling).* The perfect host: elegant, gracious, with impeccable manners. You'd trust her to host a princess. She is also completely unhinged.

**The setting.** A grand **wooden hall** in the mining village. One servant stands behind each guest's chair, plus two more by the doors. The butler waits in the corner.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 175}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Selene’s dinner

{{swpilot-flow

### The Speech

Over dessert, Selene rises and circles the **Blackwood-linked character**, stroking their shoulders. Her speech starts sweet and turns. Read or paraphrase, using that character's name:

> *"Oh, [name], my dear. How surprising to see you here in Silverwood after all this time. I must admit, I underestimated you. I believed you were hiding behind that caring heart of yours, nothing more than a coward who ran from the family. But now, seeing you here, I can see I was wrong. You followed your heart, didn't you? How beautiful. How very admirable.*
>
> *You've always had a caring heart, and that's what I loved about you. But that heart won't protect you from its own lifeblood soaking into the very floor you're sitting on.*
>
> *So I offer you a mercy. Surrender, and I'll let you watch your own heart stop in my loving hand. Oh, how delightful it will be, to feel that last beat under my fingers. Do this, and maybe, just maybe, your friends can leave with their hearts still in their chests."*

### The Ambush

As she finishes, **the servants grab the guests**, grappling them in their chairs. Selene **stabs** the Blackwood-linked character. Roll initiative.

**Selene** uses her stat block in the Bestiary. Her first attack is the stab, with **advantage** (*The Perfect Host*).

**The servants** (**Blackwood servants**, Bestiary) are terrified, not hostile. They had their tongues removed and obey only out of fear. **One hit** and they cower in a corner or drop. They grapple, but never try to kill.

**The butler** stands in the corner and watches, unbothered, as if he's seen this many times before.

### The Fireball

When Selene starts losing, the butler moves:

1. He casts a **delayed fireball** into the middle of the hall.
2. He crosses to Selene and **teleports them both out** with *dimension door*.
3. The fireball **detonates in d4 rounds**. Roll secretly.

A character who makes a **History or Arcana check** recognises the spell, and knows they need to get out.

**The choice.** The terrified servants are still in the hall. Do the players save them, or save themselves?

**The blast** destroys the wooden hall and blows the roof off.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 176}}

\page {sw-silver-bridge}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / SILVER / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Selene’s dinner

{{swpilot-flow

{{note
##### Running the fireball
- **The butler's escape is scripted.** He doesn't fight, and the party can't stop him.
- **The damage:** when it goes off, each creature in the hall makes a **DC 15 Dexterity** save, taking **7d6 fire damage plus 1d6 for every round it waited**, or half as much on a success.
- **It can't wipe the party.** A character it would drop to 0 is left at **1 hit point**, thrown clear through the wall.
- **The servants have 1 hit point.** Anyone left inside dies. That's the choice.
- The butler is a **rakshasa** in disguise, revealed in the Battle of Silverwood (Gold Chapter).
}}

Keep [Selene · p. 190](#p190) and [servant · p. 177](#p177) statistics available. The fireball timer, DC, damage growth, 1-hit-point safeguard and servants’ fate are all in the running-fireball box.
}}
