# After the dinner

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### TIMED AFTERMATH / SEPARATE EVENTS
The council responds to the exposed violence. The pillow note is the night after dinner; Andras strikes a few days later in the crowded Shaft and Pickaxe. Neither is a generic return-visit greeting. The attack’s fixed damage, minimum remaining hit point and no-chase escape remain as written.
}}

**The Mining Council.** With the Blackwoods exposed and violence spreading, the **Mining Council** (see *The Mining Houses*) meets in emergency session. The Blackwood seat sits empty. The remaining members must decide who takes over the many jobs the Blackwoods had quietly taken on, and quickly.

### The Note on the Pillow

The night after the dinner, the Blackwood-linked character finds a note on their pillow, in Selene's elegant hand:

> *"[Name], we have a job for you. Come home and all will be forgiven. Refuse, and know that Malphas and Azazel are already on your trail."*

Nobody saw who left it.

### The Uncle Strikes

A few days later, in the crowded Shaft and Pickaxe, **Andras Blackwood**, the family's uncle and its knife in the dark, strikes the **Blackwood-linked character** from nowhere.

- **20 piercing damage, no attack roll.** It should terrify, not kill: if it would drop the character to 0, they drop to 1 instead.
- Then he speaks, quietly, for that character alone:

> *"If you won't stand with the family, the family won't stand with you. Watch your back. Enjoy your meals this week. Try not to go hungry."*

- **He vanishes,** stepping into a cloud of **magical darkness** and teleporting away. **There is no chase.** The party can search the whole town, and he's simply gone.

The taunt is the clue: **the late convoy was no accident.**

The Silver Chapter ends here. The **Gold Chapter** begins with the blockade.

}}

# Andras: return-visit reminder

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-025-andras-blackwood.webp" alt="Andras Blackwood"/><figcaption>Andras Blackwood<br/>Reference edition p. 106</figcaption></figure>

### Andras

On paper, Andras is the family's legitimate face: a respectable merchant who keeps the books clean. In practice, he is **the knife in the night**.

- He **teleports** in and out of fights.
- He wraps himself in **magical darkness** and sees perfectly through it.
- He strikes, and he's gone before anyone can react.

He gets away at the end of the battle with Selene. He's the family's biggest loose end, and a good villain for a sequel.

**Says:** *"Business is business. Nothing personal."*

# Silver encounter reference

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### EXISTING RULES / STATE BEFORE ROLLING
The general encounter tables are balanced for four; the source gives a six-player table adjustment. This is not a blanket recalculation of every named fight. The catacomb state, full moon and council result each change specific written rows or tables.
}}

*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

All creatures are from the free rules (SRD 5.1) unless marked **(Bestiary)**. See *Bestiary* at the back of the book.

## The Silver Pass

### Levels 5–6

Balanced for four **level 5** characters. At level 6, every row is about one step easier.

| Hard | **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** in Blackwood colours, "inspecting" travellers *(if the council vote passed, use this as a medium encounter instead)* |
| Deadly | **A troll** and **2 ogres** |

| Deadly | **2 chimeras** |

*If the party has heard the bard's rhyme, Lirien may turn up instead of any roll.*

### Greenhart Grove

Still no wandering monsters.

## Bait Attacks

The Blackwoods' bait draws creatures onto marked wagons (see the Copper Chapter). The bait grows stronger as the campaign goes on.

| Chapter | Creatures | Difficulty |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Copper** (early) | **4 wolves**, or **2 swarms of rats** and **4 giant rats** | Easy |
| **Copper** (late) | **2 worgs** and **3 wolves** | Medium |
| **Silver** | **6 dire wolves** | Medium at level 5 |
| **Gold** | **A chimera** (see the Gold Chapter) | Medium at level 7 |

*Bait attacks give full experience. They're part of the main story.*

}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM ONLY}}

# Gold at the table

{{swpilot-flow

The Blackwoods have stopped pretending. The road is cut, the food has stopped, and an army is gathering on the Eastern Path. Silverwood must survive a siege, a poisoned harvest and finally a battle for its life.

### What's Really Going On

**GM only.**
- **Azazel Blackwood**, the family's brutal enforcer, leads the blockade. His cousin **Malphas** marches with him, and their uncle **Andras** moves unseen around the edges.
- **Behind the family, something in Hell is pulling the strings** (see *The Blackwood Family*). The Blackwoods want the mythril; their infernal patron wants Lord Xanthos gone.
- **The town's only cheap food** now comes from Lord Xanthos's magical greenhouse, and that food is changing the people who eat it.
- **Lord Xanthos is absent** for this whole chapter. Publicly, he's old and reclusive. In truth, he is **moving into a new body**. He sends what help he can (see *The Battle*), but he cannot come himself.

{{swpilot-note
##### THE CHAPTER’S THROUGH-LINE
**Carry forward:** the actual council result, Ursell’s position, Selene’s escape, the late convoy and the party’s Guild Pass. **The blockade:** food supply fails. **The greenhouse:** track actual Greenhart-food days after the blockade begins. **The battle:** the council meets the army at the single river crossing. These are different triggers, not automatic repeat-visit speeches.
}}

{{swpilot-note
##### KEEP REVELATIONS IN PLAY
The matron’s trapped soul, Xanthos changing bodies, the covered rigs’ true purpose and Vashkar’s identity are GM knowledge until discovered. Present observable symptoms and the written scenes, rather than explaining the answers.
}}

}}

# Silver into Gold

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENTRY CHECK
Record what happened at the dinner and afterwards. Gold begins with the convoy failing after the uncle’s warning. Do not replace the actual Copper council decision or Ursell outcome with a presumed result.
}}

## Ranking Up: Gold

When the characters reach **level 7**, Faldor gives them their Gold Passes. By now, the town needs every capable sword it has.

> *"Don't make me regret it."*

### Where Things Stand

The party's choices in earlier chapters change how Gold begins.

**Ursell (from Copper):**
- **If Ursell stayed,** their guards stand with the town, guarding Silverwood and the mining village.
- **If Ursell left,** they're back within days, with fewer men. On the road out they ran straight into the Blackwood army and fled back to Silverwood. Their survivors are the first to say how big it is.

**The council vote (from Copper):**
- **If it passed,** the Blackwoods were already running the escorts. Cutting the road was easy: the convoys simply stopped.

**The Blackwoods' people:**
- **The loyal ones fled** with Selene. The Blackwood lodgings, workshops and soup kitchen stand empty. Search them, and the party finds the **rest of the coded letters** (Silver Chapter).
- **Those they'd bought or bribed** are keeping their heads down and pretending nothing happened. Some are still in the village and in town, and still useful to the family.
- **The people the Blackwoods housed and fed,** like Jory Morrow, now have no work, no bed and no meals, in a town that's about to run out of food.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM REFERENCE}}

# The blockade

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ROAD CLOSURE / CHOICE
**When:** the weekly wagons stop after the uncle’s warning. Mirella’s crow reports the road, and Faldor warns against an early assault. **Player choice:** keep the source’s warned-not-blocked approach. No new deadline or compulsory sortie is added.
}}

After the uncle's warning, **no more wagons arrive.**

**Mirella's crow.** Mirella sends a crow made of smoke down the **Eastern Path** (out of the north gate, north-east, then east). It comes back with bad news: the road is blocked by armed men, and there are more every day.

**Nobody will go.** The blockade is already too large to attack. The townsfolk refuse. Faldor is blunt:

> *"You'll be on your own. We can't leave the walls, and you won't survive that. Not yet."*

{{note
##### Warned, not blocked
If the party insists on attacking the blockade anyway, let them. It's an army. They will very likely die or be driven back. That's their choice.
}}

### The Food Runs Out

Supplies come up the road once a week. Without them, the **regular menu** at the tavern (see *The Shaft and Pickaxe*) runs short, and its prices **double**. Almost the whole town turns to the cheap **Greenhart menu**, which has been on offer since the party's first day.

To feed everyone, the greenhouse is pushed harder than ever, and **that's when the food turns.**

{{swpilot-small
Blocked-road patrols: [Gold Eastern Path table · p. 230](#p230). Greenhart exposure: [food clock · p. 205](#p205). The regular menu’s doubled prices apply here; [the menu desk · p. 207](#p207) retains the original base prices.
}}

}}

# The Greenhart clock

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### CONDITION TRACKER
**Start:** the blockade, not the characters’ first tavern meal. **Count:** days on which each character eats any Greenhart food. Track each eater separately; regular-menu eaters stay safe. Retain the stated stage thresholds and cures.
}}

## The Greenhart Crisis

### The Taint

Before the blockade, Greenhart food is completely safe. **Once the blockade begins,** it isn't. From then on, count the **days** on which each character eats any Greenhart food.

| Days | Stage | What happens | Cure |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| 2 | **Cravings** | They want more Greenhart food. | Lesser Restoration |
| 4 | **Green veins** | That food's side effect kicks in (see below). | Lesser Restoration |
| 6 | **Bark** | Patches of bark on the skin, and a craving for blood. | Temple / Remove Curse |
| 8 | **Thrall** | The greenhouse can charm them when they're near it. | Temple / Remove Curse |
| 10 | **Bloomfallen** | They become a plant creature. | Only after the greenhouse is destroyed |

*A character who eats only Greenhart food turns in ten days. That's the clock on the greenhouse.*

**Side effects.** Each food brings its own change, sometimes useful and sometimes not:

}}

# The Greenhart clock

{{swpilot-flow

| Food | Side effect |
|:--|:--|
| Spiky kiwi | Hair and nails grow faster and harden like thorns. They turn aggressive and territorial, like a plant protecting its space. |
| Squash blossom | Skin turns oily and slick, like leaves. They can understand plants and talk to them, and crave squash above all. |
| Cactus fruit | Skin toughens like a cactus's, but they turn stoic and emotionless, enduring everything without complaint. |
| Carob pods | A sense of smell keen enough to track creatures from far away, and a feel for changes in the weather and the movement of animals. They crave carob. |
| Gourd | Skin thickens like a rind. They become grounded and stubborn, anchored like a plant, and crave gourds. |
| Mossy apples | Skin turns green and starts to scale over. |

**The town.** The same thing is happening to everyone eating Greenhart food. The **Hallowed Halls** become the town's hospital.

{{note
##### Players who pay for real food
Characters who pay for the regular menu stay safe. That's the point of the two menus. Penny-pinchers pay a different price.
}}

}}

# Two menus under siege

{{swpilot-flow

### The Two Menus

Silverwood can't grow enough food, and supplies arrive by caravan only once a week. So from the party's first day, the tavern offers **two menus**:

| The Regular Menu | Price |
|:--|:--|
| **Hunter's Stew:** venison or other game, potatoes and root vegetables | 10 gp |
| **Trout and Wild Greens:** freshly caught trout with wild greens and herbs | 8 gp |
| **Mushroom and Barley Soup:** wild mushrooms, barley and root vegetables, with bread | 6 gp |
| **Roasted Wild Boar:** with root vegetables and a savoury sauce | 20 gp |
| **Foraged Fruit Tart:** foraged berries and fruit, with whipped cream | 7 gp |
| **Grilled Acorn Squash:** filled with wild rice, mushrooms and herbs | 12 gp |

}}

{{swpilot-flow

| **Blossoming Squash Soup:** creamy, with root vegetables and herbs, and bread | 4 sp |

The regular menu is expensive because everything on it comes up the road by caravan. That's deliberate: it makes the cheap menu tempting.

The Greenhart food comes from **Lord Xanthos's magical greenhouse**, a gift to the town. It's cheap, plentiful and delicious, and most miners eat nothing else.

# Greenhart Grove

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### FALLBACK LEAD / FIRST ENTRY
**If the party misses the food connection:** the written attack behind the Guild kitchen supplies the trail. **First entry:** use the greenhouse passage once when they enter. The full roof-off plan stays a separate GM map; no automatic player handout.
}}

### Forcing the Issue

If the players don't connect the food to the symptoms, the food does it for them. **Plants start growing from the scraps** behind the Guild kitchen, and they attack: grabbing, stabbing and dragging (**a bloodvine shambler** and **6 Greenhart drones**, Bestiary; easy at level 7). The trail leads to the greenhouse.

### The Greenhouse

**Greenhart Grove** stands near the base of Lord Xanthos's tower on the mountain. It's a vast, magically powered glasshouse, worked by small golems that plant, tend and harvest.

*When the party enters the Greenhart greenhouse:*

{{descriptive
The heat hits first, wet and thick as breath. Glass arches overhead, fogged and dripping. Vines as thick as your arm climb the iron frames, and fruit hangs everywhere: kiwis split with ripeness, squash flowers as big as shields. Deeper in, small golems click and whirr as they tend the rows. Then, a few paces ahead, a vine turns slowly to face you.
}}

**The matron.** At its heart stands the **monitoring golem**: a mythril construct with no arms or legs, fixed in place, directing every other golem in the building. Her one instruction: *sustain the town; grow resilient life.*

**The plants.** Years of growth magic have created plants that grow, think and **hunt** on their own:

- **Bloodvine Shamblers, Tendrils and Terrors**
- **Bloodthorn Vines** and **Lurking Vines**
- **Psychedelic Shrooms**
- **Explosive cactus**, **shrinking plants** and **pitcher plants** big enough to swallow a person

All of them are in the Bestiary: the small ones are **Greenhart drones** (roll for the variety), and the big ones are **bloodvine shamblers**, **bloodvine terrors** and **pitcher plants**.

{{swpilot-small
Operational map: [Greenhouse roof off](#p256); exterior: [Greenhouse exterior](#p255). Fallback attackers: [bloodvine shambler · p. 236](#p236) and [Greenhart drones · p. 235](#p235). Main set-up: [the greenhouse fight · p. 210](#p210).
}}

}}

# The greenhouse fight

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENCOUNTER CONTROL
**Set-up:** two mothers 20 feet apart, two starting drones each, two pitchers on the approach. **Each mother’s turn:** apply her Brood and six-living-drone limit. Keep track of which mother spawned each drone. **Mother dies:** only her drones become docile.
}}

### The Fight

Two huge **mother plants** keep **spawning** smaller drone plants. Reinforcements keep coming for as long as the mothers live.

- **Kill a mother, and her drones go docile.** They stop fighting back entirely. You could beat them with a stick.
- **The pitcher plants** make it harder: they **grab** anyone who gets close and start to **digest** them.
- The party has to **focus down the mothers** without getting overrun by drones on the way.

**The set-up:** **2 mother plants** (Bestiary) at the far end of the greenhouse, **20 feet apart**, each with **2 drones** already beside her. **2 pitcher plants** grow on the paths between the doors and the mothers. Each mother spawns two more drones every turn, up to six. *Difficulty: hard for four level 7 characters.*

{{note
##### What this teaches
- **Kill the summoners first,** and don't get swamped. This is practice for the battle to come.
- **Fire works well** against almost everything here.
- **Companions are not safe.** Pets and creatures can be grabbed, swallowed and killed like anyone else.
}}

| Drone varieties | [Greenhart Drone · p. 235](#p235) |
| Swallow / acid / escape | [Pitcher Plant · p. 238](#p238) |
| Fallback attackers / roaming plants | [Shambler · p. 236](#p236) / [Terror · p. 237](#p237) |
| After the mothers fall | [The matron · p. 211](#p211) |

}}

# The matron

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### EVENT SCRIPT / REVELATION
**After the mothers die:** she speaks proudly; use the approach description and quoted lines at their written moments. **If struck:** her plea. **At death:** her final words. Her history is GM-only; leave the clue for players to notice. Preserve the written inability to persuade her and the loss of cheap food.
}}

**She does not fight.** Once the mothers are dead, she speaks, proudly. Read or paraphrase:

*When the party approaches the matron after the fighting ends:*

{{descriptive
In the middle of the wreckage stands a figure of polished mythril, set into the floor like a fountain: a head and shoulders, no arms, no legs. Her face is smooth and kind. She turns it towards you, and her voice is warm, motherly and delighted, as if you've come to admire the garden.
}}

> *"I made those. Aren't they self-sufficient? Aren't they beautiful? Next time I'll make them hardier. Much hardier."*

She was told to make the town **self-sustaining food**. She understood it as **self-sufficient plants**, and she's very proud of them.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 211}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-gold}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c18b0049"></span><span id="c18b0050"></span><span id="c18b0051"></span><span id="c18b0052"></span><span id="c18b0053"></span><span id="c18b0054"></span><span id="c18b0055"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The matron

{{swpilot-flow

**She can't be talked out of it.** If the party questions her, she insists she will make them again, bigger and better. It's her job; it's her **whole existence**. Nothing they say will change it, so the only way to end the crisis is to **destroy her**.

If she's struck:

> *"Please stop. You're damaging me."*

Her last words:

> *"Oh, no. I don't want to die again."*

{{note
##### Why this matters
*"Die again."* The matron was once a living person: one of the women who bore Vartius a son. Her soul is trapped in the golem (see research note 18). She isn't evil; she's doing the only thing she knows. This is **one of the biggest clues** to what Lord Xanthos really does. Don't play her as a villain, and don't point it out. At the original table, one player caught it and the rest let it pass.
}}

**Aftermath.** Destroying the greenhouse ends the taint, but it also **ends the town's cheap food**. Silverwood is now truly starving, and it can't last much longer.

{{swpilot-small
Her unchanged defensive card is at [The Matron · p. 240](#p240).
}}

}}

# The war council

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### COUNCIL SCENE / KNOWLEDGE BOUNDARY
**When the war council convenes:** use the established participants and Mirella’s arrival. A routine visit to her shop does not trigger the smoke map. Covered wagons initially look like cannon; the rigs’ true purpose is learned on the field.
}}

The Guild's top room. **Faldor** leads it, with Silverwood's representatives around the table: Sariel, Captain Raelyn, Brother Alexander, the remaining members of the Mining Council and the party. Anyone else the GM thinks the town would call in can be there too, such as Drogan, Tormund or Ampersand.

Mirella arrives last. She draws on her long pipe and **blows thick purple smoke across the table.** It settles into the **Eastern Path in miniature**: the hills, the road, the **river** and the army marching on it, coming closer.

{{note
##### Using the smoke map
This is the GM's moment to show the players **exactly how big the army is**. Describe the units, the camp and the strange machines. Let them plan around it.
}}

}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The war council

{{swpilot-flow

### The Army Moves

The army begins its march up the Eastern Path towards the **north gate**. Mirella's smoke shows something that terrifies the council: **large, covered wagons** among the troops, hiding some kind of **cannon-like devices**. Everyone at the table can see what they're for: **breaching Silverwood's walls.**

*(The truth, which the party learns on the field: they're **torture rigs**, iron, trumpet-like contraptions with restraints built in, and a **battering ram** the size of a house.)*

**The plan: the river crossing.** The river that feeds Silverwood runs across the Eastern Path, and there is **only one crossing point**. If the town meets the army there, it can **bottleneck** them and choke the advance before those wagons get anywhere near the walls.

**The town finds its nerve.** After weeks of saying no, Silverwood decides to **meet the army at the river.**

{{note
##### Content note
The torture rigs are grim: prisoners forced to channel magic through pain. For tables that prefer, the mages can simply be shackled captives.
}}

{{swpilot-small
The approved crossing is a stone bridge. The manuscript’s later battle passage still calls it a ford; that discrepancy is recorded separately without changing either source or artwork. Operational references: [Eastern Crossing](#p257) and [Eastern Pass](#p258).
}}

}}

# Battle control desk

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ARRIVAL / WAVES / SEPARATE THREATS
**First arrival at the crossing:** use the written defenders’ passage once. **Wave 1:** three units and two rigs. **Wave 2:** the leaders, two units, two more rigs and the tower’s two golems. **Wave 3:** Selene, then Vashkar. Andras acts separately from wave 1, every other round. Between waves is a breather, not a rest.
}}

## The Battle of Silverwood

A large battle over one or more sessions, fought at the **river crossing** on the Eastern Path. Silverwood holds the near bank; the army has to force its way across the single crossing.

*When the defenders take their places at the river crossing:*

{{descriptive
The river runs fast and cold over the stones of the ford. On the far bank, the Blackwood army fills the road as far as you can see: banners of the spreading tree, ranks of spears and four covered wagons creaking forward. Behind you, the people of Silverwood grip borrowed swords and pitchforks. Across the water, a horn sounds once. The covers come off the wagons.
}}

### Running the Battle

- **Hired soldiers** are **Blackwood soldiers** (Bestiary): minions that drop to one hit, fighting in units of five. This keeps a big battle moving.
- **Allies from town:** each player may also control **one friendly NPC** alongside their own character, using the **ally cards** in the Bestiary: Raelyn, Grimgar, Drogan or **Zoltan with his alchemical cannon**. *Optional.* One player controls **Mirella** (see *Mirella's Prism*).
- **Run it in three waves.** Between waves, the army regroups across the river. The party gets a breather (a minute to drink a potion and swap places), but not a rest.

| Wave | What comes across | When it ends |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **1. The crossing** | **3 units of soldiers** wade the ford. **2 mage rigs** fire from the far bank. | The soldiers break, or 5 rounds pass |
| **2. The charge** | **Azazel** on his nightmare and **Malphas** lead **2 units** across. **2 more rigs** roll up. The **tower's golems** arrive at the start of this wave. | Azazel falls, or Malphas pulls back |
| **3. Revenge** | **Selene** arrives and goes for the linked character. When she falls, **Vashkar** steps in, and Mirella's prism must land. | Vashkar falls, and Andras escapes with Selene |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM REFERENCE}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-gold}

# Battle control desk

{{swpilot-flow

**Andras** isn't part of a wave. From wave 1 onwards, every other round he appears behind someone, attacks once, and Shadow Steps away before anyone can answer.

{{note
##### Malphas survives
When Malphas drops below half his hit points, or when Azazel dies, he pulls back across the river with whatever soldiers are left. If the players kill him anyway, nothing breaks: the attack on the tower in the Platinum Chapter is simply led by what's left of the army, and the party finds their bodies instead of his.
}}

# The battle’s threats

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### TARGETS / REVELATIONS / CONTINGENCY
The mages are victims: destroying a rig or opening its restraints frees its prisoner alive. Selene seeks the linked character. Vashkar’s true form is revealed when he intervenes for her, rather than at the start of Gold. Malphas has a written survival contingency if players kill him anyway.
}}

### The Threats

**The Tormented Mages.** Four prisoners strapped into the rigs, forced to blast magic at the town: chaotic bolts and bursts of agony that hit random targets in a cone.

- They're **victims, not soldiers.**
- Players can **break the rigs** to free them, instead of killing them.
- Any mages freed **stay in Silverwood** afterwards.

**Stat block:** the **tormented mage rig** (Bestiary). Break a rig to 0 hit points or pick its restraints, and the mage inside comes out **alive**.

**Azazel Blackwood**, on a vicious horse that kicks and bites.
- **His sweeping attack drains life.** The more enemies around him, the more he heals. **Crowding him is a mistake.**
- **Kill the horse** first, and he loses his speed and reach.
- Minions screen him while the party deals with the mages.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 218}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-gold}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c18b0080"></span><span id="c18b0081"></span><span id="c18b0082"></span><span id="c18b0083"></span><span id="c18b0084"></span><span id="c18b0085"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The battle’s threats

{{swpilot-flow

**Malphas Blackwood**, the cousin. A brute: big, armoured and hard-hitting, fighting at Azazel's side. He survives the battle. *(He returns in the Platinum Chapter.)*

**Selene Blackwood.** She comes back for revenge, lashing out with a **whip of crackling magic**, and she goes straight for the **Blackwood-linked character**. She hasn't forgiven the humiliation of the dinner.

{{note
##### Selene gets reckless
At the original table, Selene attacked while the linked character was surrounded by friends, and **the whole party turned on her at once**, dropping her in a single round. Let that happen. It's a great moment, and it's what draws Vashkar out.
}}

**Andras Blackwood**, the uncle. Throughout the battle he **blinks in and out**: appearing behind someone, striking, then vanishing into **magical darkness** he can see through perfectly. He never stays long enough to be pinned down.

**Vashkar, the butler.** When Selene falls, he **steps in to save her**, and the party sees his **true form: a rakshasa.** (Sharp players may have worked it out already.)

*All of the Blackwoods' stat blocks are in the Bestiary: **Azazel**, **Malphas**, **Selene**, **Andras** and **Vashkar**.*

{{swpilot-small
Use [the wave table · p. 216](#p216) for timing and [the prism · p. 220](#p220) for Vashkar’s resolution. The portraits and private family history are GM reference, not automatic player disclosures.
}}

}}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM ONLY}}

# Mirella’s prism

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-010-mirella.webp" alt="Mirella"/><figcaption>Mirella<br/>Reference pp. 65</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### PLAYER-CONTROLLED ALLY / RITUAL
**Battle use:** a player controls Mirella. Apply the written three actions, damage setbacks, line of sight and range. Record ritual progress and prism layers separately from wave number. A successful Vashkar save halves layer damage; it does not prevent capture.
}}

Vashkar is far too dangerous for a level 7–8 party to fight head-on, so he isn't meant to be. **Mirella** has come to the battle to deal with him.

- **One player controls Mirella** during the battle.
- She begins a **ritual** that takes **three rounds**: she uses her action on each of her next three turns, and it completes at the start of her fourth. If she takes damage, she makes a **DC 15 Constitution** save or loses one round of progress. When it completes, she needs **line of sight** to her target, within 120 feet.
- **The party's job:** keep her alive and uninterrupted, and draw Vashkar into the open. Selene's attack, and Vashkar coming to save her, is the perfect moment.
- When the ritual completes, Vashkar is trapped in a **sphere of layered, many-coloured smoke** (based on *prismatic wall*). He makes a **DC 22 Wisdom** save, so it *feels* like a roll, but he's meant to go in: on a success, he's still trapped, but takes **half damage** from each layer.
- Inside the sphere, he can't act. It **lifts him 10 feet higher** at the start of each of his turns, as one layer breaks and blasts him: **red** 21 (6d6) fire, then **blue** 21 (6d6) cold, then **yellow** 21 (6d6) lightning, then **violet** 21 (6d6) force.
- When the last layer goes, he **falls** in a final burst: 14 (4d6) force damage, and he lands **prone**. Until the end of the next round, he **loses his immunities**, so ordinary weapons and spells can finish him. It takes very little.

{{note
##### Rules note
Rakshasas ignore most spells. Mirella's prism is a **9th-level effect**, so it gets past that. Say so plainly at the table if a player asks.
}}

**When Vashkar dies,** his body crumbles to ash. Rakshasas killed outside their home plane **always return** to the Hells in time. He's gone for now, but not for ever.

{{swpilot-small
Mirella’s ally card: [battle allies · p. 251](#p251). Vashkar’s base reference and custom changes: [Vashkar · p. 245](#p245).
}}

}}

# Battle consequences

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ESCAPES / WALL FALLBACK / OPTIONAL THREAT
Keep the written outcomes distinct: Andras takes Selene away; Azazel’s gear vanishes with him; the two sent golems are destroyed. If the crossing fails, the rigs and ram attack the walls and gate golems wake. The chimera is the source’s optional extra threat, not another mandatory wave.
}}

### The Escape

By the end of the battle, **Andras** reaches Selene in the chaos and **teleports her away**. Both escape. Whatever the party does, they shouldn't be able to stop it.

### Help from the Tower

Two **stone golems** (**Xanthos golems**, Bestiary) march down from Lord Xanthos's tower to join the fight. They fight well, and they're destroyed. The Lord himself never appears.

### Azazel's End

When Azazel dies, he **implodes in brimstone and flame**, dragged back to whatever he made a deal with. His weapons and armour go with him.

{{note
##### Foreshadow it
Before the battle, let players hear rumours that Azazel made a **bargain with something infernal**, so his gear vanishing feels earned, not unfair.
}}

### Backup: If the Army Reaches the Walls

If the town doesn't hold the river crossing, or the army breaks through:
- The rigs and the ram attack the walls.
- **The gate golems wake** and fight (**Xanthos golems**, Bestiary): two at each gate.
- The founders' wards fight back against the cannon fire.

### The Chimera

The Blackwoods' bait has grown stronger. If the battle needs one more threat, or the party is out on the road before it, a **chimera** answers the bait.

{{swpilot-small
Azazel’s death burst is on [his stat card · p. 241](#p241). The tower and gate constructs use [the same golem card · p. 248](#p248).
}}

}}

# After the battle

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### AFTERMATH / HANDOVER
The town’s survival does not restore its food. The tower invitation follows the battle; Platinum Passes are granted on reaching level 9, as written. Keep the invitation, current character level and actual Pass issuance as separate state.
}}

Silverwood has survived. The Blackwoods are broken. The town celebrates, but it's still starving.

Then an invitation arrives from the tower. **Lord Xanthos** wishes to thank the heroes in person.

The **Platinum Chapter** begins.

## Ranking Up: Platinum

When the characters reach **level 9**, Faldor gives them their Platinum Passes in front of the whole Guild. For once, he almost smiles.

> *"Hmph. Don't let it go to your heads."*

| Carry into Platinum | Record actual outcomes |
|:--|:--|
| Crossing / walls | Held, breakthrough, damage and survivors |
| Blackwoods | Azazel / Malphas / Selene / Andras / Vashkar |
| Freed mages | Rescued and staying in Silverwood |
| Greenhouse / food | Matron, taint and lost cheap supply |
| Allies / golems | Who fought, who survived, constructs destroyed |
| Invitations and Passes | Tower invitation received; level; Platinum Pass issued |
| Jobs | Cannon shells delivered; wounded; infernal plants; dead |

}}

# Fire for the Cannon

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### PRE-BATTLE PREPARATION
**Before battle:** record each handful actually delivered. Each grants the stated special shell; Cryosap produces the stated frost shell. Keep that ammunition record beside [Zoltan’s existing cannon card · p. 251](#p251). No new number of free shots or extra payout is supplied.
}}

*Gold Pass, ★★*

> *"War! Ampersand's cannon is hungry! Sparkspores, Charpods and Scorchvine from the Cinderreach, as many as you can carry without exploding. And if you find Cryosap in the Frostfang Peaks on the way back, Ampersand pays DOUBLE."* — Ampersand

**Where:** The Cinderreach. Cryosap in the Frostfang Peaks.

**Sparkspore** and **Charpod** *(fire, Uncommon)* explode. **Scorchvine** *(fire, Rare)* binds and burns. **Cryosap** *(cold, Ultra Rare)* freezes everything nearby. **Every handful delivered before the battle** gives Zoltan's cannon one **special shell** for the battle: that shot deals an **extra 2d8 fire damage**. A handful of **Cryosap** makes a **frost shell** instead: creatures that fail the save are also **restrained** until the end of their next turn. Use the **Cinderreach** encounter table, and the cold and heat rules.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Ampersand)*
- **Done:** *"BOOM! Ampersand will make such a BOOM! Valor, load the shells!"*
- **Failed:** *"No fire? Then Ampersand's cannon will just have to be very loud and slightly disappointing."*

| Ingredient delivered | Handfuls / shells | Expended in battle |
|:--|:--|:--|
| Sparkspore | __________ | __________ |
| Charpod | __________ | __________ |
| Scorchvine | __________ | __________ |
| Cryosap | __________ | __________ |

# Post-battle gathering

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### AFTERMATH JOBS / EVENT-SPECIFIC DIALOGUE
**For the Wounded:** after the battle, collect the stated Snowmoss and Glacient Root quantities. **Where Azazel Fell:** after his fall, gather the infernal growth and burn the remainder. His plants are not available before his death. Mirella’s dialogue belongs to that job’s actual hand-in.
}}

### Gathering: For the Wounded

*Gold Pass, ★*

> *"The Halls are full of the wounded and the burned. We need Snowmoss, and lots of it, and Glacient Root to cool the burns. Please hurry."* — Alexander

**Where:** The Frostfang Peaks.

**Snowmoss** *(cold, Common)* heals minor wounds. **Glacient Root** *(cold, Uncommon)* gives cold resistance and soothes burns. Twenty handfuls of Snowmoss and five of Glacient Root, and the Halls can save everyone they still have.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Alexander)*
- **Done:** *"Enough for everyone. Orathen be thanked, and you with him."*
- **Failed:** *"We'll do what we can with what we have. Some won't see the spring."*

### Gathering: Where Azazel Fell

*Gold Pass, ★★★*

> *"Something is growing where Azazel was dragged under. Bring me samples of everything, and then burn the rest. Don't touch the berries. Don't listen to the petals."* — Mirella

**Where:** The battlefield, where Azazel fell.

Infernal plants have sprouted in the scorched ground: **Bloodberry** *(infernal, Uncommon)*, **Dreadpetal** and **Infernal Bulb** *(infernal, Rare)*. Burning the patch takes an hour. Leave it, and it spreads, and the town's rumours turn dark.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Mirella)*
- **Done:** *"You burned it all? Good. Don't tell me what the petals said to you."*
- **Failed:** *"It's still spreading. I can smell it from here."*

}}

### Hunt: Hell Hound Teeth

*Gold Pass, ★★*

> *"Hell hounds have been seen in the Cinderreach. Teeth, 50 gp each. A whole hide, 500 gp. A live pup, name your price."* — Tormund

**Where:** The Cinderreach.

A pack of **hell hounds** (use the **Cinderreach** table). Tormund's prices drop for every burned or broken part.

*Gold Pass, ★★*

**4 ghouls** and **a ghast**, feeding on the fallen. If Mirella's job hasn't been done, the infernal plants where Azazel fell are spreading towards them.

**Handing it in** *(Sergeant Corwin Hill)*
- **Done:** *"Our dead stay buried now. Thank you."*
- **Failed:** *"Something's still out there. The diggers won't go out after dark."*

# Hunts and field work

{{swpilot-flow

### Capture: a Winter Wolf

*Gold Pass, ★★★*

> *"ALIVE. One winter wolf for the pits. 300 gp. Sedatives at cost. It breathes ice, so don't stand in front of it."* — Tormund

**Where:** The Frostfang Peaks.

**2 winter wolves**: one to capture, one that won't let you. Capturing takes knocking it out (melee damage that drops it to 0 can leave it unconscious) or a net, a cage and a sedative.

**Handing it in** *(Tormund)*

- **Failed:** *"Dead? Well, I'll take the pelt. It's not the same."*

### Materials: Frost Giant's Bones

*Gold Pass, ★★★*

> *"There's a frost giant in the peaks. Its bones are harder than iron and colder than the north wind. I want them. 2,000 gp."* — Drogan

**Where:** The Frostfang Peaks.

**A frost giant** and **2 winter wolves**. Deadly for four level 7 characters: plan it. Use the cold rules. *With* Arcane Forge*, harvest the parts using that book's rules (see* Hunting and Material Jobs*, Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Drogan)*
- **Done:** *"Harder than iron. Colder than the north wind. I'm going to make something wonderful."*
- **Failed:** *"Then the giant's still up there, and I'm still waiting."*

# Regional rules desk

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### COMPANION BOOKS / FALLBACKS
Use the companion books’ agreed systems when those books are in play. The fallback gathering checks below are the existing campaign rules, not replacements for Ampersand or Arcane Forge. Cold, heat, damaged parts and live transport remain as written.
}}

These are where groups using *Arcane Forge*, *Genesis Eggs* and *Ampersand's Apothecaria* will spend much of their downtime. **Each of those books uses its own difficulty rules;** use them as written, alongside the Guild's ranks. Remember: side jobs pay in **coin, contacts and gear, but little experience** (see the Copper Chapter).

### Gathering Jobs

**With *Ampersand's Apothecaria*,** use its discovery and harvesting rules. The plant's rarity sets how hard it is to find.

**Without it,** a gathering job is a normal quest. Each hour of searching the right land, one character makes a **Wisdom (Survival)** or **Intelligence (Nature)** check:

| Rarity | DC to find | Pay per handful (good / excellent) |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| Common | 10 | 5 gp / 10 gp |
| Uncommon | 13 | 40 gp / 60 gp |
| Rare | 15 | 150 gp / 250 gp |
| Ultra Rare | 18 | 500 gp / 800 gp |
| Legendary | Story only | Not for sale |

Beat the DC by 5 or more, and the handful is **excellent**. Fail by 5 or more, and the plant fights back: the harvester takes **1d6 damage** of the plant's type (fire, cold, poison and so on). Mirella's **herb books** give advantage on these checks. Everyone who posts a gathering job also pays a little extra for anything else interesting the party brings back.

**Cold and heat.** In the **Frostfang Peaks** without warm clothing, or the **Cinderreach** without water and shade, each character makes a **DC 10 Constitution saving throw** at the end of every hour. On a failure, they gain one level of **exhaustion**. Cold-weather gear or a supply of water (as appropriate) gives advantage on the save. Creatures resistant to the damage type (cold or fire) pass automatically.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / GOLD / GM REFERENCE}}

# Regional rules desk

{{swpilot-flow

### Hunting and Material Jobs

The town guard posts **pest control**, Tormund posts **hunts** and **captures**, and Ironclad's posts **material** jobs. Each one pays as its poster says.

**With *Arcane Forge*,** harvest parts using its rules. The creature's challenge rating sets the material tier:

| Creature CR | Material tier | Harvest DC |
|:--|:--|:--:|
| 0–2 | Common | 10 |
| 3–6 | Uncommon | 15 |
| 7–12 | Rare | 20 |
| 13–16 | Very Rare | 23 |
| 17+ | Legendary | 25 |

**Without it,** the party brings back the part named on the poster. A careful kill keeps it intact. Damaged parts are worth half.

**Live captures** need the creature unconscious or restrained, then caged. Tormund sells nets, cages and sedatives.

}}

# Gold encounter tables

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### STATE BEFORE ROLLING
These are wandering-table rules. The stated five/six/three-character adjustments do not automatically rewrite the greenhouse set piece or the mass battle. The blockade replaces Eastern Path encounters with patrols. Greenhart wandering plants stop after its destruction.
}}

*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

All creatures are from the free rules (SRD 5.1) unless marked **(Bestiary)**. See *Bestiary* at the back of the book.

## The Gold Pass

| Easy | **A veteran** and **4 thugs** |
| Medium | **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** |
| Hard | **A knight**, **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** |
| Deadly | **A mage**, **2 veterans** and **2 thugs** |

}}

| Medium | **2 hell hounds** and **an azer** |
| Hard | **A fire elemental** and **2 magma mephits** |
| Deadly | **A young red dragon** and **2 magma mephits** |

*If the party carries **Vigilance**, she knows a red dragon when she sees one. Expect her to have opinions.*

{{swpilot-small
Weather and harvesting: [regional rules desk · p. 228](#p228). These optional destinations support Gold work; their listed monsters do not scale down because the party chooses to go there early.
}}

}}

Custom cards below retain all source abilities, statistics and wording. The nightmare, rakshasa base, chimera and common field creatures remain the source’s SRD references; no replacement statistics are invented. Goblin Rider and Goblin Hexer references are included for the Gold hills table.
}}

### The Greenhart Plants

All Greenhart plants share these traits:
- **Damage Vulnerabilities:** fire.
- **Condition Immunities:** blinded, deafened, exhaustion.
- **Senses:** blindsight 30 ft. (blind beyond), passive Perception 10.
- **Languages:** none.

{{swpilot-note
##### SHARED TRAITS / EXCEPTION
Apply the shared vulnerability, immunities, senses and languages to the Greenhart plants. Drone docility depends on its own mother; roaming Gold-table drones have no mother nearby and never become docile. The matron is a construct and has her own card.
}}

{{swpilot-small
Cards: [Drone · p. 235](#p235), [Shambler · p. 236](#p236), [Terror · p. 237](#p237), [Pitcher · p. 238](#p238) and [Mother · p. 239](#p239).
}}

# Greenhart Drone

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Greenhart Drone
*Small plant, unaligned*
___
**Armor Class** :: 12
**Hit Points** :: 22 (5d6 + 5)
**Speed** :: 20 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|12 (+1)|14 (+2)|12 (+1)|2 (−4)|10 (+0)|3 (−4)|
___
**Challenge** :: 1/2 (100 XP)
___
***Docile.*** If the mother plant that spawned it dies, the drone stops fighting entirely. It won't even defend itself.

***Variety.*** Roll a d6 or choose. The drone gains that trait:

**1. Bloodvine Tendril.** A creature hit by its Lash is grappled (escape DC 12).

**2. Bloodthorn Vine.** Its Lash deals an extra 3 (1d6) piercing damage.

**3. Lurking Vine.** While it stays still, it looks like an ordinary plant. It has advantage on its first attack.

**4. Psychedelic Shroom.** Instead of a Lash, it puffs spores: each creature within 10 feet makes a DC 12 Wisdom save or has disadvantage on attacks until the end of its next turn, as the world swims.

**5. Explosive Cactus.** When it dies, each creature within 10 feet makes a DC 12 Dexterity save, taking 7 (2d6) piercing damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.

**6. Shrinking Plant.** A creature hit by its Lash makes a DC 12 Constitution save or shrinks to half size until the end of its next turn (as the *reduce* effect of *enlarge/reduce*).
### Actions
***Lash.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +4 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 6 (1d8 + 2) piercing damage.
}}

}}

***Sweet Scent.*** A creature that starts its turn within 30 feet of the plant makes a DC 13 Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, it must use its movement this turn to move towards the plant.
### Actions
***Swallow.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 10 ft., one Medium or smaller creature. *Hit:* The target is swallowed. A swallowed creature is blinded and restrained, has total cover and takes 10 (3d6) acid damage at the start of each of its turns. The plant can hold one creature at a time.

A swallowed creature can escape with an action and a DC 14 Strength check. It's also freed if the plant takes 20 damage or more in one turn, or dies.

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Azazel Blackwood
*Medium humanoid (tiefling), lawful evil*
___
**Armor Class** :: 18 (infernal plate)
**Hit Points** :: 152 (16d8 + 80)
**Speed** :: 30 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|20 (+5)|10 (+0)|20 (+5)|10 (+0)|12 (+1)|14 (+2)|
___
**Saving Throws** :: Str +9, Con +9
**Skills** :: Athletics +9, Intimidation +6
**Damage Resistances** :: fire
**Senses** :: darkvision 60 ft., passive Perception 11
**Languages** :: Common, Infernal
**Challenge** :: 8 (3,900 XP)
___
***Mounted Warrior.*** While mounted, Azazel has advantage on melee attacks against unmounted creatures smaller than his mount. His warhorse is a **nightmare** (SRD) that kicks and bites.

***Infernal Bargain.*** When Azazel dies, he implodes in brimstone and flame and is dragged to the Hells with his weapons and armour. Each creature within 10 feet makes a DC 16 Dexterity saving throw, taking 14 (4d6) fire damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.
### Actions
***Multiattack.*** Azazel makes three weapon attacks.

***Hellforged Lance (mounted only).*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +9 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 11 (1d12 + 5) piercing damage plus 7 (2d6) fire damage.

***Greatsword.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +9 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 12 (2d6 + 5) slashing damage plus 7 (2d6) fire damage.

***Soul Reap (Recharge 5–6).*** Azazel sweeps his blade around him. Each creature within 10 feet makes a DC 16 Constitution saving throw, taking 22 (5d8) necrotic damage on a failure, or half as much on a success. Azazel regains hit points equal to half the total necrotic damage dealt. **The more creatures crowd him, the more he heals.**
}}

***The Perfect Host.*** If Selene starts a fight from a friendly conversation, she has advantage on her first attack.

### Reactions

# Vashkar

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Vashkar
*Medium fiend (rakshasa, in the shape of an elderly human butler), lawful evil*
___
Use the **rakshasa** from the free rules, with these changes for the Gold Chapter:

***Toned-Down Spellcasting.*** Replace his spell list with: at will: *detect thoughts*, *disguise self*, *mage hand*, *minor illusion*; 3/day each: *charm person*, *dimension door*, *invisibility*; 1/day: *delayed blast fireball* (used at the dinner; see the Silver Chapter).

***Not Meant to Be Fought.*** Ordinary spells of 6th level or lower can't touch him (his Limited Magic Immunity). Only **Mirella's prism** (Gold Chapter) can bring him down. If players attack him head-on, let them see their spells fizzle, and point them at Mirella.
}}

}}

# Tormented Mage Rig

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Tormented Mage Rig
*Large construct (with a living prisoner), unaligned*
___
**Armor Class** :: 15
**Hit Points** :: 60 (the rig)
**Speed** :: 0 ft. (wheeled; pushed 20 ft. per round by its crew)
___
**Damage Immunities** :: poison, psychic
**Condition Immunities** :: all, except restrained
**Challenge** :: 5 (1,800 XP)
___
***The Prisoner.*** The mage inside is strapped in and can't be targeted while the rig stands. When the rig drops to 0 hit points, it breaks open, and the mage falls out unconscious **but alive**.

***Free Them.*** A creature next to the rig can use an action to try to open the restraints: a DC 15 Strength (Athletics) check or a DC 15 check with thieves' tools. On a success, the mage is freed and the rig stops working.

***Crew.*** Each rig is pushed by a unit of **Blackwood soldiers**.
### Actions
***Agony.*** At the start of the rig's turn, roll a d6. The rig doesn't choose its targets; it can hit anyone, including the army.

**1–2. Bolt.** A random creature within 120 feet takes 22 (4d10) force damage (+7 to hit).

**3–4. Cone.** A 30-foot cone towards the nearest group. Each creature there makes a DC 15 Constitution save, taking 27 (6d8) psychic damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.

**5. Overload.** Each creature within 20 feet of the rig makes a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 18 (4d8) lightning damage on a failure, or half as much on a success. Its own crew usually dies.

**6. Misfire.** Nothing happens but a scream.
}}

}}

*Small humanoid (goblinoid), neutral evil*
___
**Armor Class** :: 13 (hide armour)
**Hit Points** :: 27 (6d6 + 6)
**Speed** :: 30 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|8 (−1)|14 (+2)|12 (+1)|10 (+0)|10 (+0)|14 (+2)|
___
**Skills** :: Stealth +6
**Senses** :: darkvision 60 ft., passive Perception 10
**Languages** :: Common, Goblin
**Challenge** :: 1 (200 XP)
___
***Nimble Escape.*** The goblin can take the Disengage or Hide action as a bonus action.

***Spellcasting.*** The hexer's spellcasting ability is Charisma (spell save DC 12, +4 to hit with spell attacks). It knows:

Cantrips (at will): *chill touch*, *minor illusion*
1st level (3 slots): ***bane***, *thunderwave*
2nd level (1 slot): *darkness*
### Actions
***Bone Knife.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +4 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 4 (1d4 + 2) piercing damage.

# Platinum at the table

{{swpilot-flow

Silverwood has survived the Blackwoods, but it's starving. Its greatest hero, Lord Xanthos, wants to thank the party in person, and to ask one more favour: bring the old woods back to life.

This chapter takes the party into the tower, into the Feywoods and finally into the founders' tomb. Every step brings them closer to the truth about the man who invited them.

### What's Really Going On

**GM only.** Lord Xanthos is **Vartius**, one of Silverwood's five founders. For six centuries he has lived on by **stealing bodies**, swapping his soul into his own descendants. He has just swapped again, which is why he missed the battle.

He is a genuine hero who has saved the realm many times. He is also a monster. He doesn't believe he is evil.

He has spent those centuries foiling the plans of Hell, which is exactly why something there sent the Blackwoods to bring him down.

{{swpilot-note
##### THE CHAPTER’S THROUGH-LINE
**Invitation:** the new young lord thanks the town. **The woods:** restore the obelisks, or destroy and rebuild them. **The truth:** the house supplies the final tiles, the tomb supplies the founders’ evidence, and the second tower visit reveals the machine and Lysander. Carry actual outcomes forward, especially whether the party freed her and tidied up.
}}

{{swpilot-note
##### DISCOVERY / FIRST CONTACT / EVENT
The lord’s identity, the soul machine and Lysander’s fate are GM secrets until learned. First arrivals are once, regardless of visit order. Dinner speeches, the alarm, Lirien’s guidance and Sariel’s discoveries each require their own written events.
}}

}}

# The first tower invitation

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### PUBLIC ADDRESS / ACCEPT OR REFUSE
**After the battle:** deliver the town address once. The young lord’s explanation and name answer belong to this encounter. **If refused:** the source sends play to [the Feywoods request · p. 268](#p268), including its west-wall attack.
}}

## The First Tower Visit

### The Invitation

After the battle, Lord Xanthos addresses the town. Read or paraphrase:

> *"Good people of this fair town, brave souls who stood unwavering in the face of adversity, I, Lord Xanthos, stand before you with the deepest admiration for your valour. Today, you have demonstrated the indomitable spirit that courses through the veins of this community.*
>
> *As the sun sets on this field of triumph, let it be known that your deeds have not gone unnoticed. I, the newly anointed Lord Xanthos, offer you a rare and extraordinary opportunity. Tomorrow, at the break of dawn, I extend to our heroes an invitation, one that shall forever change the course of their lives.*
>
> *Within the ancient walls of the Xanthos Tower lie the treasures of knowledge, the wonders of magic and the mysteries of ages long past. You need not fear, for your safety shall be paramount in my thoughts. It is a privilege few have been granted.*
>
> *Until then, rest well, my friends, for a new chapter in your lives is about to begin."*

**He is young.** The Lord Xanthos the town remembers was an old man. He explains that his **grandfather** has passed, and the title with him. If anyone asks the grandfather's name, he smiles:

> *"To common folk like yourself? Lord Xanthos."*

*If the party refuses the invitation, see* The Feywoods Request *below.*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 263}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c19b0015"></span><span id="c19b0016"></span><span id="c19b0017"></span><span id="c19b0018"></span><span id="c19b0019"></span><span id="c19b0020"></span></div>

# The first tower tour

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST VISIT / RECORD REGISTRATION
**On the accepted first visit:** he meets the guests in the foyer and asks them to touch the brass plates. Record who actually does so: later recognition depends on this visit. Keep first-visit access separate from the later investigation.
}}

### The Tour

He meets them in the foyer:

> *"Ah, my honoured guests, welcome to the illustrious Xanthos Tower! I trust your journey here was pleasant, and I must express my gratitude once more for your valour on the battlefield. You have earned this day of wonder and discovery.*
>
> *Before we begin, there is a small but necessary ritual. Each of you, please step forward and place your hand upon these plates. They shall recognise you."*

**The brass plates** record each guest. *(This matters later.)*

> *"Excellent! Here, in the portal room, you shall witness glimpses into other realms: the elemental planes, the enchanting Feywild, the shadowy domain and more. Look to the portraits, and you shall see these portals come to life.*
>
> *Allow me to show you our chamber of astrology, where the movements of the celestial bodies are mirrored in a delicate, mesmerising dance. Behold the central diamond, the heart of this apparatus, as it orchestrates the movements of the metal planets.*
>
> *Ah, you may have noticed my other guests, my loyal servants. They are golems, brought to life by the finest craftsmanship, moving as if they were made of flesh and bone, yet silent in their service.*
>
> *Now, let us retire to the dining hall, where you shall be served a feast fit for heroes."*

The **shadow portal** sits behind a crackling **force shield**. A character who saw the greenhouse matron may feel uneasy about the golems.

{{swpilot-small
The astrological-apparatus sentence is retained at the author’s request for this review. Its conflict with the earlier private-soul-machine decision remains in the editorial notes; no replacement apparatus or decoy is invented.
}}

}}

# Dinner with Lord Xanthos

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### EVENT SCRIPT / OPTIONAL CLUES
**During the accepted tower dinner:** use the speech and select lineage comments for the actual guests. The founder-name slip and early talking-golem clue are optional nudges, not a compulsory reveal or repeat greeting. His reason for asking remains GM-only.
}}

### The Dinner

> *"Ladies and gentlemen, as we partake in this sumptuous feast, let us reflect upon the legacy of the Xanthos bloodline. The previous lords of this great tower have safeguarded our realm from the encroaching shadows time and time again. Demons, devils, dragons and interplanar creatures have all tested the might of our lineage.*
>
> *I, Lord Xanthos, now stand as the guardian of our noble family's traditions, and I pledge to follow in their illustrious footsteps.*
>
> *Now, my esteemed guests, I must admit I am deeply curious about your unique talents and lineages. Tell me, those of you who possess magical gifts or extraordinary skills, what can you do? And what remarkable heritage has granted you such abilities?"*

{{note
##### The author's original slip
At the original table, he said *"I, **Vartius** Xanthos, now stand as the guardian…"* here. If your players need a nudge, let him say it.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 265}}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c19b0024"></span><span id="c19b0025"></span><span id="c19b0026"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Dinner with Lord Xanthos

{{swpilot-flow

**His comments about lineage** are odd, and a little inappropriate:
1. *With a sly smile:* *"Ah, lineage is a fascinating subject, wouldn't you agree? When it comes to humans and their bloodlines, it's rather remarkable how their traits tend to dominate in the end. The old saying 'like father, like son' often rings true."*
2. *Turning to a tiefling:* *"Now, my dear tiefling friend, you are quite the exception, aren't you? It's said that tieflings are resistant to such dominant lineage effects. Quite intriguing, wouldn't you say?"*
3. *A chuckle, eyeing a character of mixed heritage:* *"Ah, the mysteries of lineage can be ever so complex. When different races mix, one often wonders which traits will emerge. It's like a captivating dance, don't you think?"*
4. *In a joking tone:* *"They say blood runs thicker than water, but in some cases it seems to run a bit… wilder. After all, there's a bit of magic in every family tree, wouldn't you say?"*

{{note
##### Why he's asking
At first, this just seems eccentric. Later, the players will realise he was **shopping**: judging whose bloodline might suit his breeding programme.
}}

{{note
##### Optional extra clue: the talking golem
The talking golem's main moment is in the **second tower visit**. If the players **aren't suspecting him at all**, you can also use it here. One golem servant, older than the rest, can speak. While serving a character, it says something simple, like:

> *"Master Vartius Xanthos hopes the wine is to your liking."*

If anyone reacts, Lord Xanthos smooths it over with a smile: *"An old construct. It still uses my ancestor's name. Family habit."* It's a believable excuse, but it's a slip. Vartius is the name of the **founder**, who died six hundred years ago.
}}

{{swpilot-small
Dining/state plan: [L6 — first dinner · p. 358](#xanthos-l6-first-dinner). The talking golem’s main later moment is at [the second tower visit · p. 279](#p279).
}}

}}

# The first visit: the alarm

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### BREACH / DEPARTURE / LIMITED EXPLORATION
**Mid-meal:** the breach interrupts dinner. He leaves, fights offstage and returns by projection. The allowed exploration is the dining hall and its corridor on L6, with other doors and upper stairs sealed on this visit. The aftermath in the portal room is evidence, not a new party combat.
}}

### The Alarm

Mid-meal, his voice rings out:

*In the measured, authoritative voice of Lord Xanthos:* *"Ladies and gentlemen, esteemed guests, I regret to inform you that a security breach has occurred within the portal room. The containment protocols have been compromised, and our vigilant stone golems have been activated to address this threat."*

*With urgency:* *"I must attend to this matter personally. Please remain here in the dining area. I assure you that all necessary precautions are being taken to ensure your safety."*

He leaves. **Behind the scenes:** the last of the Blackwoods' infernal allies have broken through the shadow portal. In his new body, Lord Xanthos destroys them single-handed.

Later, his **astral projection** materialises before the party, battered and wounded. He offers a courteous nod, his voice echoing in the empty room:

> *"My sincere apologies for my abrupt departure. Urgent matters require my attention. I trust you can find your way out? Simply command one of the golems, and it will guide you safely. Until our paths cross again."*

His image flickers and disappears.

**Exploring.** The party can wander the dinner hall and the corridor outside it, on Level 6. Every other door on that floor, and every stair above it, is sealed on this visit. On their way out, they notice **a trail of blood** leading towards the portal room. Inside: **two smashed stone golems** and **three lifeless demons** (**vrocks**, from the free rules) strewn across the floor. A shimmering force shield surrounds the shadow portal, crackling with energy. It's clear a fierce battle took place here.

{{note
##### What the players should take away
Lord Xanthos is **far more powerful** than he lets on. He killed all of that alone, while they ate dessert.
}}

{{swpilot-small
Portal-gallery plan: [L4](#p355). No secret route through the sealed floors is invented.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

# The Feywoods request

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ACCEPTED VISIT / EXISTING REFUSAL ROUTE
**If attending:** he raises the food problem at dinner or afterwards, directly or wistfully according to the conversation. **If refusing:** the rot behemoth attacks the west wall and Faldor sends the investigation. Keep the written choice and both routes; no compulsory dinner is added.
}}

### The Feywoods Request

Either at dinner or afterwards, he raises the town's real problem: **food.**

- **If the party asks good questions,** he asks them directly to restore the old woods.
- **If not,** he simply mentions it, wistfully.

Either way:

> *"If the old woods were what they used to be, back in my day… well. The town could forage and farm there again. But the obelisks that kept them healthy are broken."*

**The slip.** *"Back in my day."* He's young. A character who passes an **Insight check** notices him catch himself, a flicker of annoyance and then the smile is back.

**If the party refused the dinner:** a huge, rotting creature (a **rot behemoth**, Bestiary) crawls out of the Feywoods and attacks the west wall. The town guard and the gate golems help hold it. **Faldor** sends the party to find out why. The woods need fixing either way.

## The Feywoods

West and south-west of Silverwood, beyond the gate that has been shut for generations. The founders once made these woods a garden that fed the town. Now they're wild, dangerous and in places **rotten**.

**The Feywoods don't wait for Platinum.** Players can enter at any time, and lower-level parties will meet **manticores, chimeras** and worse. Rumours and warnings should make that clear long before now.

{{swpilot-small
West-wall attacker: [Rot Behemoth · p. 301](#p301). The source’s Insight check has no fixed DC; none is assigned here. The woods are accessible before Platinum, with their original danger warnings.
}}

}}

# Lirien

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-art"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/npc-029-lirien.webp" alt="Lirien"/><figcaption>Lirien<br/>Reference pp. 305</figcaption></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### EARLIER CONTACT / TRIAL / HEALING GUIDANCE
The bard’s rhyme may already have been heard. Her trials can occur at any level. Track whether they were completed and the bell’s remaining rings. Once the party is working to heal the woods, her guiding encounter can occur whether or not she was met earlier. Do not replay the first introduction on every visit.
}}

The bard's rhyme about her, which players may have heard in the tavern:

> *Amidst the trees of whispered dreams,*
> *Where the vines and roots doth seam,*
> *There lies a glade of sylvan green,*
> *And in its heart, a faerie queen.*
>
> *Beware, dear travellers, of her tricks,*
> *For her mirth is oft paired with a prick.*
> *But if your hearts are true and bold,*
> *Lirien's secrets she may unfold.*
>
> *But if you solve her challenge fair,*
> *And prove your wit beyond compare,*
> *Then Lirien, the faerie queen,*
> *Shall grant you aid, and be serene.*

A **wind sprite**, the strongest of the sprites who once lived in the great willow at the heart of the woods. She calls herself the **Fae Queen**. She isn't one. She's cheeky, vain and dangerous when bored.

**Her trials:**
1. **Riddles.** For example: *"I am always in front of you, but can never be seen. What am I?"* (The future.)
2. **A memory challenge:** half the party each tell her a treasured memory. The more vivid, the better.
3. **The shadow fight:** she summons **shadow copies of the whole party**. They have the same skills, spells and tactics, but their gear is ordinary, with no magic. *(Full rules: The Shadow Copies, Bestiary.)*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 269}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Lirien

{{swpilot-flow

{{note
##### Running the shadow fight
- It **scales itself**, because the copies match the party. It can happen at **any level**.
- The GM controls the copies as a **real team**: coordinating, flanking, focusing fire. It teaches players to plan together.
- If it goes badly, Lirien gets bored and ends it.
}}

**Her reward:** a **silver bell** with **three rings**. Each ring summons her help. Roll a d4 to see what kind:

| d4 | Lirien's help |
|:--:|:--|
| 1 | **A wisp** appears and guides the party to wherever they're trying to go in the Feywoods. |
| 2 | **A fey creature** arrives with a small gift: a healing berry, a useful herb or a forgotten trinket. |
| 3 | **The forest goes quiet.** The party can take one short rest, safe from wandering creatures. |
| 4 | **A mischievous pixie** turns up. It helps, but it causes trouble too. |

**At this stage, Lirien shows them the way.** Once the party is working to heal the woods, she turns up, whether or not they've met her before:

> *"Oh, so you're the ones poking at the rot. Mother will want to meet you. Come on, keep up!"*

The sprites once lived in the Willow Mother's tree, so to Lirien, the Willow Mother is *mother*. She **guides the party to the clearing**.

{{swpilot-small
Full shadow-fight rules: [The Shadow Copies · p. 304](#p304).
}}

}}

# The Willow Mother’s clearing

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpilot-scene"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/main/Silverwood/production-2026-10-25/opn-006-willow-mother-clearing.webp" alt="Willow Mother clearing"/></figure>

{{swpilot-note
##### FIRST ARRIVAL / ALTERNATIVE SEARCH
**First arrival, by either written route:** use the clearing description once. The party can follow Lirien or find it through careful searching/persistence. This preserves the manuscript’s scene; the separate sketch’s house node remains an unresolved continuity note. No fixed magical-path destinations are invented.
}}

### The Clearing

At the centre of the woods lies a safe, beautiful clearing, untouched by the rot. Here stand:

- **The Willow Mother:** an ancient, sentient willow, kind and wise.
- **An old treant,** who remembers the founders.
- **A working obelisk** that keeps the clearing healthy. It shows what a healed obelisk should look like.
- **Lysander's house** (see below).

**How to find it:** Lirien leads them there. It's hidden, not locked, so a party that refuses her help can still find it with careful searching or simple persistence.

*When the party first reaches the Willow Mother's clearing, by either route:*

{{descriptive
One step, and the rot is behind you. Green light falls through a vast willow whose branches sweep the grass like curtains. Bees work the clover. A treant, grey with lichen, opens one eye. Beside a small wooden house, an obelisk of white stone hums softly, clean and whole. Somewhere above you, Lirien laughs. "Told you. Mother, they're here!"
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 271}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c19b0064"></span><span id="c19b0065"></span><span id="c19b0066"></span><span id="c19b0067"></span><span id="c19b0068"></span><span id="c19b0069"></span><span id="c19b0070"></span><span id="c19b0071"></span><span id="c19b0072"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY}}

# Escort the Willow Maids

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### DAWN DEPARTURE / ESCORT / REINFORCEMENTS
**Preparation:** the mother sends them at dawn after rest; root them before nightfall. Three daughters exist, two are needed, one is spare. **At each obelisk:** protect the maid, AC 11 / 22 HP; add two rotlings at the start of round three. The ritual ends when the attackers are dealt with, not after a new fixed timer.
}}

### The Broken Obelisks

Two obelisks lie **north** and **south** of the clearing, dark with rot and surrounded by its creatures. These are the **Corrupt Heartwood Obelisks**: twisted versions of the healthy one in the clearing.

The stone, once pristine, is weathered and cracked, covered in dark, oozing moss and thorny vines. The engravings that once told the forest's history are warped into nightmarish shapes. At each obelisk's core, a corrupted heart pulses with a sickly glow, and the ground around it is a mire of foul, putrid sludge. Rotworm swarms writhe through the soil, and **rot beasts**, once the forest's guardians, now roam the blight, leaving trails of decay behind them.

**The Willow Maids.** The Willow Mother has **three daughters**, young Willow Maids. Two are needed, one for each obelisk. The third is a spare.

A Willow Maid is delicate and humanlike, with smooth skin the pale, creamy green of fresh willow branches, and almond-shaped amber eyes that hold an ancient wisdom. Instead of hair, she has a crown of willow vines, lush and glowing faintly. When she casts a spell, some of the vines wither and turn reddish-brown, giving up a little of their life to fuel the magic.

- A Willow Maid must **root into a new place within one day**, or she withers. The Willow Mother sends them at dawn, after the party has rested. They must be rooted **before nightfall**.

- The party **escorts** each Willow Maid through the rot. When she reaches an obelisk, she roots into it and begins to **purify** it.
- **The party must protect her while she works.** The ritual ends once the attackers are dealt with.
- **At each obelisk:** **a rot beast**, **2 rot hounds** and **2 rotlings** attack (Bestiary). At the start of the third round, **2 more rotlings** crawl out of the rot. *Difficulty: hard for four level 9 characters.* The attackers go for the **Willow Maid** first; she has **AC 11 and 22 hit points**.

- A purified obelisk keeps its Willow Maid forever. She can't be moved.

**If too many Willow Maids die,** the obelisks can't be purified this way, and the party must take the other route.

{{swpilot-small
Combat: [Rot Beast · p. 300](#p300), [Rot Hound · p. 299](#p299), [Rotling · p. 298](#p298). Shared hazards: [rot rules · p. 273](#p273). North and south maps: [310](#p310) / [311](#p311). If maids are lost: [the other route · p. 274](#p274).
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

# Rot: the running rules

{{swpilot-flow

### Rot Creatures

Rot creatures **explode when they die.**

> **Death Burst.** When the creature dies, each creature within 5 feet makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, it takes necrotic damage, and **its hit point maximum is reduced by the same amount.**

- **A long rest does not restore it.** Only **greater restoration**, from the temple or a party cleric, does.
- **Limit:** a hit point maximum can't fall below **half** its normal value. The rot is frightening, but it can't wipe a character out.

**Rotworms** burrow into flesh. Burn them out with even a small flame, quickly, before they reach the heart.

*Stat blocks for **rotlings**, **rot hounds**, **rot beasts**, the **rot behemoth** and the full **rotworm** rules are in the Bestiary.*

{{note
##### What this teaches
Fight rot creatures up close and they'll ruin you. Smart players learn to **wear them down, step back and finish them from range.** By the second obelisk, they should have worked it out.
}}

### Rot Creatures

The rot twists the Feywoods' animals into swollen, splitting things. All rot creatures share this trait:

> ***Death Burst.*** When the creature dies, each creature within 5 feet (10 feet for the behemoth) makes a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, it takes the listed necrotic damage, and **its hit point maximum is reduced by the same amount.**
>
> A long rest doesn't restore it. Only *greater restoration* does. A hit point maximum can't fall below **half** its normal value this way.

> ***Rotworms.*** An infested creature takes 7 (2d6) necrotic damage at the start of each of its turns. Any creature can use an action to burn the worms out with even a small flame (a torch, a fire cantrip), which ends the infestation. *Lesser restoration* also ends it. If the worms aren't removed within **3 rounds**, they reach the heart: the creature's hit point maximum is reduced by 10 (as Death Burst), and the worms die.

}}

# The other obelisk route

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ALTERNATIVE / REBUILDING
The party can choose destruction, or need it after too many maids die. Smashing stops the spread but does not heal the woods. The source then supplies Sariel’s rebuilding quest: moonstone, heartwood and a dragon scale. Keep that step between destruction and restored farmland.
}}

### The Other Route: Destroying the Obelisks

If the Willow Maids are lost, or the party simply chooses to, they can **smash** the obelisks instead. The rot stops spreading, but the woods won't heal.

The obelisk fights back. Use the **Corrupt Heartwood Obelisk** (Bestiary). As it's damaged, it calls rotworm swarms and a rot beast out of the blight.

Back in Silverwood, **Sariel** researches the problem:

> *"I've found the original plans. If you can bring back what's needed to rebuild them, we can start them running again."*

That becomes a **rebuilding quest** for three rare materials, each from a place the party already knows:
- **Moonstone** from deep in the **mines**.
- **Heartwood** from the old trees at **Greenhart**.
- **A dragon scale** from **Dragon's Obelisk**.

**Either way, the woods become farmable again**, and the party reports back to the Guild. Either way, they've been to the clearing, so **Lysander's house** (and its crypt tiles) is open to them.

{{swpilot-small
Destruction uses [Corrupt Heartwood Obelisk · p. 302](#p302), including its HP-threshold reinforcements. The source does not specify a new construction DC or timed crafting system; none is added.
}}

}}
