# Lysander’s house

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### REWARD / LATER DISCOVERIES
The home is the written reward and also carries the final ten tiles and moonlight lantern. The secrets emerge over time as stated; do not give future tower evidence merely because the house is entered. Its exact placement versus the separate sketch node is flagged, not silently rearranged.
}}

In the clearing stands a small wooden house. It belonged to **Lysander**, the druid founder who first made these woods grow.

**It's bigger on the inside.** It grows to fit whoever lives there: a bedroom and bathroom for each, plus a kitchen and living space. From outside, it's always the same little house.

**This is the party's reward: a home.** It's the perfect base for downtime, including crafting (*Arcane Forge*), raising eggs (*Genesis Eggs*), brewing (*Ampersand's Apothecaria*) and training.

**It also has secrets.** Over time, the house gives up clues to its owner: a hidden drawer, a portrait, the last **10 stone tiles** for the crypt puzzle, and **Lysander's moonlight lantern**.

{{swpilot-small
Tile accounting and the lantern: [The crypt puzzle · p. 276](#p276). Companion crafting, eggs and brewing keep their own agreed rules.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 275}}

# The shield wall — west catacomb

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### WEST CATACOMB → FOUNDERS’ CRYPT
The shield wall stands in the **west catacomb**. Completing both seals opens the passage to the **Crypt of the Founders**. Release the four batches once each: **12 + 14 + 14 + 10 = 50**. Every batch mixes tiles from both seals.
}}

Two round bronze holy seals sit within a celestial mural of the **Four Cosmic Gods**. Each has a **5 × 5 grid** of square recesses: 25 squares per seal. Both completed seals show the same approved pantheon picture—four overlapping bronze circles, with **Oravaro north, Elyndra west, Avaris south and Caldris east**.

**Normal light.** Every tile has an identical twin. The ordinary picture cannot tell the players which seal a tile belongs to.

| Release stage | Tiles | Current digital password |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| The Raven Job | 12 | **BLACKFEATHER** |
| The Search of the Catacombs | 14 | **RESTLESSBONES** |
| The Rescue of Lilith | 14 | **SILVERPEARL** |
| Lysander’s Hut | 10 | **MOONWILLOW** |

The last release also unlocks the **Moonlight Lantern** button. These passwords are GM release controls; give each only when its stage is reached. Use the digital puzzle’s existing batches; do not invent fixed tile-ID assignments.

**Moonlight reveal.** The lantern acts like a black light. On the **left seal**, the Venn circles and a great outer ring through the corner tiles glow; its frame reveals a ring. On the **right seal**, the four holy symbols and a star in each corner tile glow; its frame reveals the four compass points.

**Completion.** All 50 tiles must occupy the correct seal **and** the correct square. There is no additional word search or spoken-word lock.

{{descriptive
The seals blaze with moonlight. Deep inside the wall, something heavy turns and unlocks.
}}

The passage to the Founders’ Crypt opens. Continue with [the Crypt of the Founders · p. 278](#p278).


}}

# The shield wall — play at the table

{{swpilot-flow

<figure class="swpuzzle-wall"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/shield-wall-player.webp" alt="Two bronze seals, each with a five-by-five grid, on the celestial mural"/></figure>

## Play the shield wall

<div class="swpuzzle-access"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Omegaoliverid/Public-images/67124c193e8b5fa3c01f0682bcd24c4230fa89a3/Silverwood/book-continuity-2026-10-29/shield-puzzle-qr.png" alt="QR code for the live Silverwood shield puzzle"/><div><p><strong>Scan to play:</strong><br/><a href="https://omegaoliverid.github.io/silverwood-shields/">omegaoliverid.github.io/silverwood-shields/</a></p><p>Drag a tile onto a seal, or tap a tile and then a square. Move it back to the tray to remove it.</p></div></div>

**Release tiles at the table.** Use the four passwords on the preceding page at their actual discoveries. The Raven Job at Rook’s Nest provides 12; the catacomb search 14; Lilith’s belongings 14, whichever way her story ends; Lysander’s Hut the final 10 and the lantern. Each release mixes pieces from both seals. Record each batch only once.

**Use the lantern.** After the final release, the Moonlight Lantern button switches the hidden marks on and off. The normal pictures are twins; the different moonlit marks distinguish the two seals. Players must arrange every piece correctly, rather than enter an answer word.

**Physical play.** Use the same 50 tiles and four release counts. Red decoder film reveals the hidden lines in place of the lantern. Keep tile numbers on the GM key only, never on player tiles. Prepare and check the printed decoder materials before a paper-only session; the digital link is the ready-to-play option.

**The story result.** The completed wall in the west catacomb unlocks the route to the Founders’ Crypt. It does not grant the crypt’s diary, letters or equipment until the party reaches and searches their written discovery points.

{{swpilot-small
Give players the QR code or puzzle link. Keep the release passwords and numbered GM key out of the player handout.
}}


}}

# Crypt of the Founders

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ENTRY / EVIDENCE / REWARDS
**After the puzzle opens the passage:** describe the five statues and preserve the distinction that only four founders rest here. Release the diary, letters and named gear at their written discovery points. The evidence creates suspicion of Lord Xanthos; it is not an automatic confession from him.
}}

Through the completed shield wall in the west catacomb, a passage leads up from the catacombs into the **Tomb of the Founders**. **Five statues** stand inside, one for each founder:
- **Vartius:** tall and broad-shouldered, short black hair and a trimmed beard, in richly patterned robes. The sculptor gave his eyes a sadness.
- **Lysander:** slender and graceful, long flowing hair carved with flowers woven through it, in garments patterned with leaves.
- **Brolin:** a sturdy, weathered dwarf with a short beard and old scars on his face, in plain travelling clothes.
- **Elara:** small and grinning, long hair loose, sleeves rolled up, as if she's about to laugh or throw a punch.
- **Saraphina:** poised and elegant, loose curls to her shoulders, in fine clothes and jewellery. But only four founders rest here: **Brolin, Elara, Lysander** and **Vartius**. Saraphina, the fifth, is buried elsewhere, with her family.

**What the party finds:**
- **Brolin's diary:** decades of entries about Silverwood, his friends and his growing unease about Vartius.
- **Saraphina's letters,** left with Brolin's diary: her confession, the black pearls and what Vartius did.
- **The founders' old gear,** left as payment for whoever uncovers the truth, one piece on each tomb:
  - **Brolin's tomb:** a *mace of disruption*.
  - **Elara's tomb:** a *belt of hill giant strength*. (She really did punch one.)
  - **Lysander's tomb:** a *staff of the woodlands*.
  - **Saraphina's gift,** left on Brolin's tomb with her letter: a *ring of evasion*.
  - **Vartius's tomb:** nothing. His things went to the tower, which is a clue in itself.

**Handout Release:** When the characters discover Brolin's diary and the two letters left by Saraphina, provide the corresponding documents from the Handout Pack. These are not available to the party at campaign start.

**What it all means.** Lysander's body was taken. Vartius did something terrible with souls. And "Eamon", the son who inherited the tower, was never quite the same after his father died.

The party now **suspects Lord Xanthos.**

{{swpilot-small
Approved tomb exterior and roof-off reference: [pp. 195](#p195) / [313](#p313). The shield-wall puzzle is on the western chamber’s wall; it opens the route into this crypt. The existing diary and letters remain in the handout source.
}}

}}

# The second tower visit

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### ALARM / RECOGNITION / ACCESS DEPENDENCY
**Trigger:** smoke and the alarm during the last Blackwood attack. The main talking-golem slip happens at the door. Recorded brass-plate guests are recognised while Vartius is busy. If the first visit was refused or plates were not touched, the manuscript supplies no equivalent admission here; flag that state rather than assuming registration.
}}

## The Second Tower Visit

### The Last Blackwood Attack

**Malphas Blackwood** has gathered what's left of the Blackwood army, and **breaks into the tower through the portal room.** Smoke pours from the tower's windows. The tower's alarm rings out across the valley.

The party races up the mountain to help.

**At the door,** the old golem that can speak meets them:

> *"Thank you for your concern for my lord. But Master Vartius Xanthos does not require your assistance."*

{{note
##### The talking golem
This is the **main place** for the golem's slip: *Master Vartius Xanthos.* If you already used it at the first dinner, it lands twice as hard here.
}}

From inside comes the sound of a battle: explosions, screams and the roar of magic. Lord Xanthos is destroying, **single-handed**, an army like the one it took the whole town to beat.

**Getting in.** The brass plates in the foyer **recorded the party** on their first visit. The tower still recognises them. While Vartius is busy, the doors open for them, and nobody is watching.

**Getting higher.** Climb the shaft of the levitating platform, or find the **hidden stairs** between floors.

**Afterwards.** In the portal room, the party can find what's left of **Malphas**: a burnt, broken body. Vartius killed him without breaking a sweat.

{{swpilot-small
If Malphas died in Gold, retain that chapter’s contingency: the remnant army attacks without him. Do not read “Malphas” as a resurrection. His source paragraph remains unchanged for review.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

# Level 5: The Soul Machine

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### DISCOVERY / TRAP / RESEARCH
**On discovering the machine:** touching it triggers the written random two-character soul swap. Restoring them requires reactivation with a diamond worth at least 300 gp. The research notes are found in this study; the source’s six-note shortcut is retained. No new body-swap statistics or cure procedure is supplied.
}}

**The machine:** a vast apparatus like an astronomical clock, with rune-carved panels, gears, crystals and levers.

> **Trap.** If anyone touches the machine, **two random party members swap souls.** Each controls the other's body. Swapping back requires the machine to be reactivated with a **diamond worth at least 300 gp**.

**His study:** shelves of books and scrolls on souls, planar travel and magic, and **twenty-four research notes**, centuries of them, in his own hand (see the *Handout Pack*). Between them, they show:
- How he put Lysander's soul into a golem, and took its arms and legs away.
- The accident that swapped his soul with his son **Eamon's**.
- His moves into new bodies, and the decades it took to get his power back.
- His **breeding programme**, and what he did with the mothers of his sons: they became **matron golems**.
- That the **greenhouse matron** is one of them.

*If time is short, hand over notes 2, 6, 13, 18, 22 and 23.*

{{swpilot-small
Plan: [L5 open astrological soul machine](#p356). Handout release and the selected note numbers: [evidence desk · p. 305](#p305).
}}

}}

# Level 8: Lysander

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### DISCOVERY / PLAYER DECISION / HANDOVER
**First discovery in the abandoned laboratory:** use the passage once. The decision about the core belongs to the players. Record whether Lysander was freed and whether everything was put back exactly; both matter in Mithril. Vartius’s ignorance is GM-only.
}}

His old, abandoned laboratory. Dust everywhere. In the corner, chained so it can't destroy itself, is a **golem** with its arms and legs removed.

Inside it is **Lysander's soul.**

Across the room, in a case of shimmering magic, lies **Lysander's body**, perfectly preserved, exactly as she was the day she died.

She has been trapped there for centuries, unable to sleep, rest or die. Her mind is broken. She can't speak clearly, but she **begs** the party, over and over, to end it.

*When the party discovers Lysander in the abandoned laboratory:*

{{descriptive
Dust lies thick on everything. In the corner, a golem of pale stone with **no arms and no legs** lies chained to the wall at the waist and the throat, so it can't thrash enough to break itself. Across the room, in a case of shimmering light, lies a golden-haired woman who looks as if she fell asleep a moment ago. The golem's head turns towards you. Its voice is a woman's, cracked and broken: "Please. Please. End it."
}}

If they **smash the golem's core**, her soul is finally free. She thanks them.

{{note
##### He's forgotten her
Vartius has forgotten Lysander is here. Everything he has done for six hundred years was to bring her back, and he has left her chained in an attic. **He doesn't know she's gone.**
}}

### Did They Tidy Up?

{{note
##### Ask the players
**"Did you put everything back exactly as you found it?"**
Note the answer. It decides how the Mithril Chapter begins.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 281}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

# The way in to Mithril

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### FINAL APPROACH / CREDENTIALS
After this investigation, the front entrance is sealed. Sariel’s archived blueprints supply the way beneath. Keep the player answer to “Did you tidy up?” separate from the Guild’s level-11 Pass requirement. Platinum does not grant a new level merely for finding a room.
}}

The front door of the tower is now **sealed**. Back at the Guild, **Sariel** has found the founders' **old cave blueprints**, filed away centuries ago in **Elara's archives**:

> *"There's a way in from beneath. It's marked 'extremely dangerous.' No explanation. Just that."*

## Ranking Up: Mithril

When the characters reach **level 11**, Faldor hands over the Mithril Passes himself. He looks every one of them in the eye.

> *"Come back."*

The **Mithril Chapter** begins.

The dangerous cave sequence belongs to Mithril; see [pp. 318–323](#p318) without advancing or rewriting it.

# Optional: the Obelisk’s song

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### OPTIONAL INVESTIGATION / WARNING
These threads are optional. Sariel’s discovery requires the party to have asked about Dragon’s Obelisk. Preserve the warning about lethal dragons and the future-book boundary; no dragon-rider dungeon is invented here.
}}

## Optional: Wider Horizons

*Three optional threads that open up in the Platinum Chapter. None of them is needed to finish the campaign. Each one is kept short here, and leaves the door open for future books.*

### The Song of the Obelisk

By now, the party may have heard the old rhyme about **Dragon's Obelisk**:

> *High on a cliff stands a towering stone,*
> *A beacon to those with hearts of scales, alone.*
> *It sings a song when gold is near.*
> *Beware: only a fool would dare*
> *To cross the dragons' path and interfere,*
> *Lest their fate be met with fire and fear.*

**Sariel's discovery.** If the party has asked about the Obelisk, Sariel has been digging. She has found rumours from **before Silverwood was founded**: there was once a settlement of **dragon riders** here.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 283}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c19b0143"></span><span id="c19b0144"></span><span id="c19b0145"></span><span id="c19b0146"></span><span id="c19b0147"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Optional: the Obelisk’s song

{{swpilot-flow

**The secret.** Gold offered at the Obelisk **summons a dragon**. Long ago, this is how the riders found their mounts.

- **How much gold decides how old the dragon is:**

| Gold offered | Dragon |
|:--|:--|
| Up to 500 gp | Wyrmling |
| Up to 5,000 gp | Young |
| Up to 50,000 gp | Adult |
| More | Ancient |

- **Roll a d10 for which dragon comes:** 1 black, 2 blue, 3 green, 4 red, 5 white, 6 brass, 7 bronze, 8 copper, 9 gold, 10 silver.
- **Chromatic dragons** (1–5) arrive angry, hungry and greedy for more. **Metallic dragons** (6–10) are curious, and may talk before they decide what to do.

{{note
##### Handle with care
A summoned dragon can easily wipe out a level 9–10 party. Warn the players through the rhyme, Sariel and anyone sensible. **The hidden dragon-rider caves beneath the Obelisk** are not part of this campaign; they're a hook for a future book.
}}

}}

# Optional: the Southern Ruins

{{swpilot-flow

### The Southern Ruins

If the party has been chasing old stories, **Mirella** and **Sariel** have heard rumours of an **old outpost to the south**. The bard's tales and the rumours are patchy, with far more missing than known.

Players who go looking can find the **ruins**, and in them, the remains of an **airship**.

{{note
##### A door to the next book
The airship can't be finished in this campaign. But in the **epilogue**, the party can start to **build or restore it**, and where they fly it is the next adventure.
}}

# Optional: the Labyrinth

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### OPTIONAL / NO EXPERIENCE / FLOOR ACCESS
Optional training and materials, not an experience farm. Keep pedestal unlocks to touched floors and use the source’s room generation. Neither exact floor maps nor a new loot system are invented.
}}

### The Labyrinth

Beyond the Feywoods, on the far side from Silverwood, lies the **Labyrinth**: an endless, shifting dungeon. Nobody knows who built it or where it ends.

- **It opens in Platinum,** once the party can reach the far side of the woods.
- **It's a proving ground.** Rooms, traps and monsters that test the party, as deep as they dare to go.
- **Teleport pedestals.** At the end of each floor is a pedestal. Touch it, and next time the party can **teleport straight back to that floor** from the entrance.

- **Rewards are items and materials, not experience.** It's a place to find treasure, crafting materials, rare ingredients and creatures, not to gain levels. *(This matches the rule for side jobs.)*

**Building a floor:** roll on the **room table** (see *Encounter Tables*), and use the encounter tables for the party's rank.

{{note
##### Just the basics
The Labyrinth could fill a book on its own, and one day it might. In this campaign, it's an optional training ground for groups who want one.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 286}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Optional: the Labyrinth

{{swpilot-flow

## The Labyrinth: Room Table

Each floor has **d6 + 3 rooms**. Roll a **d12** for each.

| d12 | Room |
|:--:|:--|
| 1 | **An empty corridor,** with a note or mark left by someone who came before. |
| 2 | **A trap.** DC and damage by rank: Copper DC 12, 2d10; Silver DC 13, 4d10; Gold DC 15, 6d10; Platinum DC 16, 8d10; Mithril DC 18, 10d10. |
| 3–6 | **A lair.** Roll on any area's table for the party's rank. |
| 7 | **A riddle door.** A wrong answer sets off a trap (as 2). |
| 8 | **A cache:** crafting materials, rare ingredients or coins. |
| 9 | **A shrine** to one of the eight gods. The party can take a short rest here safely. |
| 10 | **A mirror room.** A shadow copy of one random character steps out (see *The Shadow Copies*, Bestiary). |
| 11 | **An ooze garden:** **gelatinous cubes**, **ochre jellies** and **gray oozes**, scaled to the party's rank. |
| 12 | **The pedestal.** This floor is done. Touch it to unlock teleporting here next time. |

*The Labyrinth gives items and materials, not experience (see the Platinum Chapter).*

{{swpilot-small
Entrance reference: [pp. 315](#p315). Shadow copies: [rules desk · p. 304](#p304). The promised printable/online crypt puzzle is a different prop, not the Labyrinth.
}}

}}

# Platinum gathering work

{{swpilot-flow

### Gathering: Spell Flowers

*Platinum Pass, ★★*

> *"Spellroot, Wizbloom and Manaberry grow on the slopes around the tower. Nobody has dared pick them in years. You've been inside. I think you'll manage."* — Mirella

**Where:** Around the Xanthos Tower.

**Spellroot** *(arcane, Rare)*, **Wizbloom** and **Manaberry** *(arcane, Ultra Rare)* each restore spell slots when brewed. The tower's guardians don't mind people picking flowers. Mostly.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Mirella)*
- **Done:** *"You've been inside that tower and come back with flowers. You're my favourite adventurers."*
- **Failed:** *"The guardians didn't agree with you? No. No, they rarely do."*

### Gathering: Shadows and Warps

*Platinum Pass, ★★★*

> *"Warpvine! Umbral Bloom! Ampersand has read about them in books, and now Ampersand wants them! They grow in the Labyrinth. Ampersand will not be going. You will."* — Ampersand

**Where:** The Labyrinth.

**Warpvine** *(eldritch, Ultra Rare)* teleports whoever drinks it a short way. **Umbral Bloom** *(eldritch, Ultra Rare)* cloaks the drinker in shadow. Use the Labyrinth room table.

*Without* Ampersand's Apothecaria*, use* Gathering Jobs *(Region file).*

**Handing it in** *(Ampersand)*
- **Done:** *"Ampersand has never been happier! Ampersand might even come out of the lab to say thank you! He won't, but he might!"*
- **Failed:** *"Nothing? From the LABYRINTH? Ampersand understands. Ampersand would not have gone either."*

{{swpilot-small
Mirella’s “been inside” line belongs to the completed job’s hand-in. It does not replace her established portrayal on an ordinary visit. Labyrinth access is the written optional thread, with its existing dangers.
}}

}}

# Platinum hunts and field work

{{swpilot-flow

### Hunt: a White Dragon's Scales

*Platinum Pass, ★★★*

> *"A young white dragon nests in the Frostfang Peaks. Scales, 100 gp each. The whole hide, 5,000 gp. The head, we'll talk."* — Tormund

**Where:** The Frostfang Peaks.

A **young white dragon** and its **ice mephits**. Use the cold rules. Groups using *Arcane Forge* will want every scale.

**Handing it in** *(Tormund)*
- **Done:** *"A dragon! You brought me a DRAGON! Now, about that head..."*
- **Failed:** *"Scorched by frost and nothing to show? At least you came back."*

### Pest Control: Rot Hounds at the West Gate

*Platinum Pass, ★★*

> *"Rotting hounds are coming out of the woods and testing the west gate. My guards can't hurt them. You can."* — Captain Raelyn Rivers

**Where:** Outside the west gate.

**4 rot hounds** (Bestiary). If the obelisks are already healed, these are the last of them.

**Handing it in** *(Captain Raelyn Rivers)*
- **Done:** *"The gate's quiet. The first quiet night in weeks."*
- **Failed:** *"They're still testing the gate. We'll hold. We always hold."*

### Capture: a Chimera

*Platinum Pass, ★★★*

> *"A chimera. Alive. All three heads. 3,000 gp, and a seat in the front row when it fights."* — Tormund

**Where:** The Feywoods.

**A chimera**, and whatever it's hunting. Three heads means three times the sedative.

**Handing it in** *(Tormund)*
- **Done:** *"All three heads! Front row, as promised. Bring friends."*
- **Failed:** *"Two heads and a corpse? I said all three ALIVE."*

# Legendary story plants

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note

These are given or discovered through the written story, not ordinary search checks. Keep the healed-obelisks gift, riddle reward and deepest-room discovery separate.

### Legendary Plants

These can't be found by searching. They're **story treasure**:

- **Lunapetal** *(radiant)* grows only in **Lysander's clearing**, under moonlight. The Willow Mother gives one to the party if they heal the obelisks. It makes light-based vision potions.
- **Lucid Bulb** *(psychic)* is **Lirien's** gift, for a riddle answered well. It restores lost memories.
- **Echo Spores** *(arcane)* hang in the air of the **Labyrinth's** deepest room.

{{swpilot-small
The companion book defines the resulting potions. No new rarity roll, exchange rate, spell-slot amount or unlock condition is added.
}}

}}

# Regional rules desk

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### COMPANION / FALLBACKS
Use the agreed companion rules when the companion books are in play. These are the campaign’s existing fallbacks, including cold and heat.
}}

These are where groups using *Arcane Forge*, *Genesis Eggs* and *Ampersand's Apothecaria* will spend much of their downtime. **Each of those books uses its own difficulty rules;** use them as written, alongside the Guild's ranks. Remember: side jobs pay in **coin, contacts and gear, but little experience** (see the Copper Chapter).

### Gathering Jobs

**With *Ampersand's Apothecaria*,** use its discovery and harvesting rules. The plant's rarity sets how hard it is to find.

**Without it,** a gathering job is a normal quest. Each hour of searching the right land, one character makes a **Wisdom (Survival)** or **Intelligence (Nature)** check:

| Rarity | DC to find | Pay per handful (good / excellent) |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| Common | 10 | 5 gp / 10 gp |
| Uncommon | 13 | 40 gp / 60 gp |
| Rare | 15 | 150 gp / 250 gp |
| Ultra Rare | 18 | 500 gp / 800 gp |
| Legendary | Story only | Not for sale |

Beat the DC by 5 or more, and the handful is **excellent**. Fail by 5 or more, and the plant fights back: the harvester takes **1d6 damage** of the plant's type (fire, cold, poison and so on). Mirella's **herb books** give advantage on these checks. Everyone who posts a gathering job also pays a little extra for anything else interesting the party brings back.

**Cold and heat.** In the **Frostfang Peaks** without warm clothing, or the **Cinderreach** without water and shade, each character makes a **DC 10 Constitution saving throw** at the end of every hour. On a failure, they gain one level of **exhaustion**. Cold-weather gear or a supply of water (as appropriate) gives advantage on the save. Creatures resistant to the damage type (cold or fire) pass automatically.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 293}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# Regional rules desk

{{swpilot-flow

### Hunting and Material Jobs

The town guard posts **pest control**, Tormund posts **hunts** and **captures**, and Ironclad's posts **material** jobs. Each one pays as its poster says.

**With *Arcane Forge*,** harvest parts using its rules. The creature's challenge rating sets the material tier:

| Creature CR | Material tier | Harvest DC |
|:--|:--|:--:|
| 0–2 | Common | 10 |
| 3–6 | Uncommon | 15 |
| 7–12 | Rare | 20 |
| 13–16 | Very Rare | 23 |
| 17+ | Legendary | 25 |

**Without it,** the party brings back the part named on the poster. A careful kill keeps it intact. Damaged parts are worth half.

**Live captures** need the creature unconscious or restrained, then caged. Tormund sells nets, cages and sedatives.

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 294}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM ONLY}}

# Platinum encounter tables

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### WORLD STATE BEFORE ROLLING
Wandering-table size adjustments do not automatically rebalance named obelisk defences or the refusal behemoth. Once both obelisks are healed or destroyed, apply the written once-daily Feywoods change and replace rot creatures with manticores/chimeras. This stops the spread even if rebuilding is still needed for farmland.
}}

*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

All creatures are from the free rules (SRD 5.1) unless marked **(Bestiary)**. See *Bestiary* at the back of the book.

## The Platinum Pass

### Levels 9–10

Balanced for four **level 9** characters. Anywhere not listed here, use the **Gold** tables: at this rank they're easy or medium.

}}

{{monster,frame
## Corrupt Heartwood Obelisk
*Huge construct, unaligned*
___
**Armor Class** :: 18 (natural armour)
**Hit Points** :: 250 (20d12 + 120)
**Speed** :: 0 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|

___
**Damage Immunities** :: necrotic, poison
**Condition Immunities** :: charmed, exhaustion, frightened, poisoned
**Senses** :: blindsight 60 ft. (blind beyond this radius), passive Perception 10
**Languages** :: understands Common but can't speak
**Challenge** :: 8 (3,900 XP)
___
***Corruption Aura.*** Creatures within 60 feet of the obelisk have disadvantage on saving throws against disease, poison and necrotic damage.

***Call the Rot.*** The first time the obelisk drops below each of these, the blight answers. Its creatures appear within 30 feet and act on its initiative.
- **Below 188 hit points:** a **rotworm swarm**.
- **Below 125 hit points:** **two rotworm swarms**.
- **Below 63 hit points:** a **rotworm swarm** and a **rot beast**.

***Rotworm Swarm.*** Use the **swarm of insects** from the free rules. A creature it hits is infested with **rotworms**.
}}

# The Shadow Copies

{{swpilot-flow

Lirien's third trial (Platinum Chapter). There's no stat block: **each copy uses its character's own sheet.**

- **Same everything:** level, hit points, AC from armour and Dexterity, ability scores, skills, class features, spells and spell slots.
- **Ordinary gear:** no magic items. Magic weapons become ordinary ones; magic armour gives only its normal AC; items with charges don't work.
- **No consumables:** copies don't drink potions or read scrolls.
- **Shadow-made:** at 0 hit points a copy fades away. It doesn't make death saves.
- **Tactics:** the copies fight as a real team. They focus fire, protect their healer and use the party's own favourite tricks against them.
- **Experience:** treat each copy as a creature of a CR equal to its character's level.
- **If it goes badly,** Lirien gets bored and waves it away.

{{swpilot-note
##### PARTY-MATCHED TRIAL / LABYRINTH EXCEPTION
Prepare one copy of each actual character sheet, apply the ordinary-gear and no-consumables exceptions, and coordinate them as a team. Lirien can end a trial that goes badly. The Labyrinth mirror room produces one random character’s copy; its no-experience rule remains.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / PLATINUM / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 304}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-platinum}

# Mithril at the table

{{swpilot-flow

The party knows the truth. Lord Xanthos is Vartius, the founder who never died. He has stolen body after body for six hundred years, bred his own descendants as spare parts and left the woman he did it all for chained in an attic.

Now they go into the tower from below, to end it.

{{swpilot-note
##### THE CHAPTER’S THROUGH-LINE
**From Platinum:** record whether Lysander was freed, whether the party tidied up and who touched the brass plates. **Approach:** cave, spider, shades and blood door. **Inside:** captives, evidence and the confrontation on L5. **Ending:** use Lysander’s actual fate and the written collapse clock; record rescues for the epilogue.
}}

{{swpilot-note
##### CONDITIONS / UNRESOLVED BRANCHES
The tidy-up answer controls whether he expects them. Lysander’s fate controls the ending; telling him she was freed triggers his Unbound change. These are separate conditions. No new outcome is invented for accepting his loyalty or memory-erasure offers.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 318}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-mithril}

# The cave: gelatinous spider

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### APPROACH / RESOURCE PRESSURE
The archived blueprints lead beneath the tower. The spider is the written resource-cost encounter, not a new wandering roll. Keep its misses-stick-weapons rule distinct from a successful hit; note actions spent recovering weapons and spells used before Vartius.
}}

## The Cave

The Guild's blueprints show a way in at the base of the tower, marked only *"extremely dangerous."* It leads **straight up into the tower.**

### The Gelatinous Spider

Deep in the cave lives a **gelatinous cube** that has eaten spiders for so many generations that it has **become one**: a huge, translucent, many-legged horror, with bones, weapons and skulls suspended inside it.

- **Resistant** to non-magical bludgeoning, piercing and slashing damage.
- **Stuck weapons:** when a melee weapon attack **misses it** (AC 17), the weapon sticks fast inside it. Pulling it free takes an **action** and a **DC 12 Strength check**.
- **Sticky webs** that grapple.
- **Regurgitation:** a cone of stone, bones, skulls and old weapons, spat out with crushing force.
- **Piercing bites** and **acid**.

**Stat block:** the **gelatinous spider** (Bestiary). On its own, it's an easy-to-medium fight for four level 11 characters. The real cost is the spells they spend.

{{note
##### The dilemma
Weapons barely hurt it and keep getting stuck. Spells work, but every spell spent here is one the party **won't have for Vartius**. Let them feel that choice.
}}

{{swpilot-small
Combat card: [Gelatinous Spider · p. 341](#p341). Operational cave map: [B2](#p350).
}}

}}

# Mirror shades

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### BOUND MIRRORS / STRENGTH DRAIN
Six shades, each bound to its own mirror. Track which mirrors are broken and the character’s current Strength separately from HP. The printed card supplies radiant-kill and re-forming details. Mirella’s monster-book payoff applies only if the party learned it.
}}

### Mirror Shades

Further up, the cave opens into a hall of old, polished mirrors. Shadowy hands reach out of the glass.

- Based on the **shadow** from the free rules: each hit **drains Strength**.
- **At 0 Strength, the target dies.**
- **Greater restoration** restores lost Strength.
- **The fight:** **6 mirror shades** (Bestiary). Each is bound to its own mirror: **break the mirror** (AC 12, 10 hit points), and its shade can't come back. Players who only fight the shades will watch them re-form. *Difficulty: medium for four level 11 characters, and much worse for anyone with low Strength.*

{{note
##### The payoff
Mirella sells **monster books** in the town. One describes a creature whose touch drains strength, *"and when your strength is gone, so are you."* Players who read it will recognise the shades and panic. That's the point.
}}

{{swpilot-small
Card: [Mirror Shade · p. 344](#p344). Mirrors retain AC 12 / 10 HP, and Strength 0 remains lethal as written.
}}

}}

# The blood door

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### RIDDLE / WRONG-ANSWER COUNTER
The hand, Infernal text, translated riddle and misleading shelves are different clues. Track actual wrong offerings to apply the existing hint schedule. The correct answer is willingly offered blood; do not replace this with a new skill check or forced offering.
}}

### The Blood Door

At the top of the cave, a round chamber. On the far side, instead of a door, a great polished **chrome mirror**. The only way into the tower is through it.

**It can't be forced.** Any attack against the mirror **rebounds**, just like the walls of the founders' tomb: if the attack roll equals or beats the attacker's own AC, it hits them instead.

**The hand.** When the party approaches, a **metal arm** slides out of the mirror, palm up, and makes a *"give it to me"* gesture.

**The shelves.** Around the walls are shelves of objects: gems, coins, keys, bones, a vial of red ink and, quite literally, a **red herring**. None of them is the answer.

**The riddle.** Words drift across the mirror's surface in **Infernal script**. Anyone who can't read Infernal sees only strange letters. Translated:

> *When it's found, it foretells war or woe;*
> *Its discovery's a sign, trouble in tow.*
> *When it's held within, life it shall bring,*
> *But when it's freed, death or sorrow will sing.*
> *In shades of crimson its presence may lurk;*
> *To proceed, reveal it. Within you, you must work.*
> *What am I?*

**The answer: blood,** offered willingly by someone in the room, into the open hand.

**Wrong answers.** Anything else placed in the hand is snatched into the mirror, and **spikes shoot out of the walls**, dealing piercing damage to everyone in the chamber: a **DC 15 Dexterity** save, taking **22 (4d10)** piercing damage on a failure, or half as much on a success. After certain wrong answers, a new line of hint appears on the mirror:

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-mithril}

# Is he expecting them?

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### TIDY-UP BRANCH / NONCOMBAT GOLEMS
Use the actual Platinum tidy-up answer. If he knows, the greeting golems escort rather than attack and do not join the final fight. If he does not know, the source says the party can surprise him; this is not a newly guaranteed surprise round.
}}

Check the players' answer from the end of the Platinum Chapter: **did they put everything back exactly as they found it?**

**If they tidied up:** Vartius doesn't know they're coming. The party can surprise him.

**If they didn't:** Vartius is a researcher. He knows when his things have been touched. He's **waiting**. As the party climbs out of the cave, golems meet them:

> *"The Master is waiting for you upstairs. We will take you to him."*

The golems do not attack, and they do **not** join the fight.

# The tower, floor by floor

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### LOCATION / CURRENT STATE
These are discovery and rescue locations. Keep them distinct from the first visit’s sealed floors and the second visit’s investigation. The final fight is L5; Lysander is L8 if left there. The tower has no wandering-monster rolls.
}}

## Inside the Tower

The tower holds everything Vartius has hidden for six centuries:
- **Captives:** criminals, the sick and the unlucky, kept for his experiments.
- **His descendants:** some imprisoned, some being raised in nursery rooms, some held for his breeding programme.
- **Soul-bound golems,** working, guarding, and some raising the children.
- **An old half-elf prisoner,** one of his own descendants, held so long that captivity has made him savage.

}}

# The tower, floor by floor

{{swpilot-flow

### The Tower, Floor by Floor

| Level | What's there |
|:--|:--|
| **B2. The caves** | The gelatinous spider, the mirror shades and the blood door. Beyond the door, a corridor of old rooms climbs into the tower. |
| **B1. The cellars** | The eight-pier undercroft and stores below L1. The northeast stair descends to B2; the two entrance-hall stairs rise to L1. No lift below L1. |
| **L1. The ground floor** | A clean entrance hall within the broad rectangular ground floor, many bare rooms, the lift room, a storeroom with a few supplies, a defensive gallery, and **the crematorium**: a skeletal golem-furnace beside racks of clothes and belongings taken from the dead. |
| **L2. Storage and golem works** | Towering shelves of ingredients, the golem lab, and the **wreck of the Retriever**, the soul-hunting construct he built in 829. |
| **L3. The cells** | Captives in five cells, a hall, experiment rooms and **the half-elf prisoner** in the end cell. |
| **L4. The portal room** | Six portals: the elemental planes of earth, water, fire and air, the realm of shadow and the Feywild. Two storerooms of planar curiosities. |
| **L5. The heart** | **The soul machine**, his **study** and an **armoury**. The final battle happens here. |
| **L6. The dining and state rooms** | The **dinner hall** (now bare: the table and chairs are gone), state rooms and their service rooms. |
| **L7. The nurseries** | Children being trained in magic and arms by golems, a golem nursing a baby, two pregnant women staring at the wall, a medical laboratory, rooms of weeping golems, and Vartius's plain bedroom. |
| **L8. The attic** | His old laboratory, and **Lysander**, if the party left her there. |
| **L9. The observation level** | The enclosed observatory. |
| **L10. The open viewing terrace** | The open terrace and its glass floor above the observatory. |

# Rooms, captives and discoveries

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### SEARCH / RESCUE / EXISTING REWARDS
Apply only the printed checks and rewards. Record captives the party actually finds and frees; save their names for the collapse record. Distinguish the ordinary stone-guardian reference elsewhere from the tower’s soul-bound constructs.
}}

**Rooms worth reading aloud or searching:**
- **B2, an old room by the corridor:** a DC 10 Investigation check finds a note scratched into the stone: *"Elara was here, 799."*
- **L2, the golem lab:** among the blueprints, a **memory crystal**, and beside it his note on making them (**Meridius, 1495**; handout). A DC 15 Investigation check in the parts store finds **a pouch of gems worth 500 gp**; a DC 15 check among the golem parts finds a **spell scroll** of 2nd level, and a DC 20 check finds one of 3rd level.
- **L3, the cells:** five captives: **Sallie** (starved and silent), **Josie** (rocking, in shock), **Marcus** (lucid, scratching what he's seen into the walls, and a good witness), **Flicker** (sitting in a trance) and **Arris** (paranoid and cowering). Freeing them is part of the collapse.
- **L3, the end cell:** **the half-elf prisoner**, chained. Years of experiments have changed him: he can fly. He doesn't speak, and he doesn't trust anyone.
- **L4, the storerooms:** a DC 15 Investigation check finds a ***ring of resistance*** (fire); a DC 20 check also finds a ***ring of resistance*** (cold).
- **L5, the study:** a ***spell scroll of planar binding***, and centuries of research.
- **L5, the armoury:** the stand is empty; he took the best for himself. A DC 15 check finds a hidden compartment with a ***cape of the mountebank*** and a ***ring of protection***.
- **L7, the golem rooms:** some of the golems cry without tears, and ask where their children are.

{{note
##### Content note
This is the darkest part of the campaign: imprisonment, forced breeding and children used as future bodies. For tables that prefer, keep the details off-screen. The players only need to understand that **he treats his own family as spare parts**.
}}

### Legendary Plants in the Tower

- **Soulseed** *(infernal)* grows in a sealed tray in Vartius's laboratory. It traps and holds souls. It's what made his machine work.
- **Codex Seed** *(arcane)* sits in a glass case in **Vartius's study**. Brewed and drunk, it lets a caster cast one spell they don't know.

{{swpilot-small
The memory-crystal note remains in the existing handout source. Soulseed and Codex Seed use the agreed companion-book potion rules; no extra brewing recipe or reward is added.
}}

}}

# Vartius: the confrontation

{{swpilot-flow

## Vartius

He meets them standing, calm and courteous, as if this were another dinner. Read or paraphrase.

### His Story

> *"Greetings, brave adventurers. Before you rush to judgement, I implore you to grant me a moment to explain. You've ventured deep into my tower, and it's only right that I shed some light on who I am and the purpose of this place.*

> *Many years ago, I, Vartius Xanthos, was a humble founder of Silverwood, working alongside my faithful companions. I was driven by an unrelenting desire to restore my beloved Lysander to life, and my studies of the arcane led me to the secrets of this tower.*

> *When she passed away in childbirth, I sought to bring her back from beyond the veil. My companions, such as Brolin, upheld their faith and their convictions, and refused to support me. And so I turned to powerful magics to call forth her soul, and with the best intentions, I placed her spirit within a golem. Alas, it was not the reunion I had hoped for. Her very soul rebelled against the artifice of her new existence. In my desperation to stop her destroying herself, I confined her, a decision I have come to regret deeply.*

> *I watched as the child of our union, Eamon, was raised by Brolin and others, and I saw companions like Elara meet their fate. Eventually, and regretfully too late, I tried to share my knowledge with Eamon, so he might carry on the legacy of this tower and the quest to restore his mother. But my magic could only move souls into golems, not back. One fateful day, Eamon, raised on the teachings of Brolin where I had abandoned him, sought to destroy the machine. In a cataclysmic twist, our souls exchanged places. My old body, with Eamon's soul inside it, gave way under the strain.*

> *This journey led me to discoveries that may seem horrifying in isolation, but were, in my view, vital for the greater good. I learned to move souls from one body to another. I harnessed the power of attunement, and I embarked on a path of selective breeding, cultivating the traits that would make the transfers easier.*

> *You may see my captives as nothing more than expendable, and I understand the appearance of cruelty. But I assure you, they are criminals, the terminally ill, those too broken to function in society, or my own lineage. For every life I took, I saved thousands from interplanar invasions, dragon attacks, plagues and lich awakenings. I bare all to you, and I ask you to consider the greater good I have dedicated my existence to."*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

# His debt and the three choices

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### PRIOR CONSENT / EXISTING OFFERS
A past rescue connection requires the player’s prior permission; it is not a new forced backstory. His offers are described as traps in the source. The manuscript does not provide separate loyalty or memory-erasure endings, and this layout does not add them.
}}

### His Debt to the Party

Vartius's heroism is real. He may have **saved one of the party, or their family**, in a past life.

{{note
##### Tying Vartius to the characters
At session zero, or during the campaign, ask players for permission to link one character's backstory to a **past Lord Xanthos**: a rescue from a cult, freedom from slavers, a dragon fought alongside their parent. Here, Vartius reveals that it was him all along. It makes the fight personal, and it makes it hard.
}}

### What He Has Lost

His gaze goes distant. His voice carries a profound sense of sacrifice, as if he has given up part of himself for the greater good:

> *"I have sacrificed much for this realm. In my pursuit to save it, I feel like I've lost parts of myself. I can no longer remember the taste of my favourite food, the colour that once brought me joy, or even if I had a cherished place."*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 330}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-mithril}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c20b0050"></span><span id="c20b0051"></span><span id="c20b0052"></span><span id="c20b0053"></span><span id="c20b0054"></span><span id="c20b0055"></span><span id="c20b0056"></span><span id="c20b0057"></span><span id="c20b0058"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# His debt and the three choices

{{swpilot-flow

A long pause, as if he's struggling to recall his fading memories.

### The Three Choices

*His tone turns friendly, just like at dinner:*

> *"Still, there are choices to be made. You can stay your weapons, join me in protecting this realm and swear your loyalty within a Zone of Truth."*

*His voice goes dispassionate, indifferent to the fate he describes:*

> *"Alternatively, you can surrender your weapons and magical items. Your memories will be altered, and you will become simple commoners in Silverwood. Lives of blissful ignorance, untethered by your past."*

*Then menace and resolve:*

> *"Or you can challenge the culmination of my lifetimes of work. Attempt to undo my research and raise arms against me. But beware, for I will not let the legacy of centuries crumble into oblivion. Not now, when I am so close to bringing back Lysander."*

{{note
##### He's forgotten
He truly believes he's close to bringing Lysander back. He has forgotten she was in the golem upstairs, and if the party freed her, he doesn't know it yet. **Every choice he offers is a trap.** None lets the party walk away free.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

# The final battle

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### COMBAT DESK
**Location:** the L5 soul-machine chamber. Use [Vartius’s complete card · p. 342](#p342), including legendary actions/resistance and lair actions. Choose a lair effect at initiative 20, losing ties, without repeating the same effect consecutively. Keep the original four-character difficulty label; no six-player recalibration is implied.
}}

In the chamber of the **soul machine** on Level 5, where it all began. One wizard against the whole party, and it should feel that way.

- **Legendary actions** and **legendary resistance**
- Powerful reactions and spells, including counterspells, shields and teleports
- A **robe of the archmagi**
- Multiple spells in a turn

**Stat block:** **Vartius Xanthos** (Bestiary), with his **lair actions** in the soul machine chamber. He's a hard fight for four level 11 characters on paper, and his counterspells and legendary resistance make it feel deadly.

# The two endings

{{swpilot-flow

How the tower falls depends on one thing: **did the party find and free Lysander** on their second visit (Platinum Chapter)?

### Ending A: They Freed Lysander

**The turn.** At some point in the fight, the players tell him the truth: **they found Lysander, and they set her free.**

Vartius breaks. He half-remembers her, the golem and the attic, but not all of it. What he does remember is **loving her.** He flies into a rage.

> *"How DARE you."*

*Ending A only — after Vartius learns that Lysander has been freed:*

{{descriptive
For a heartbeat, the young face goes slack, and something very old looks out of it: a chained golem, an attic, a woman's voice he hasn't let himself hear in six hundred years. His hands begin to shake. Every crystal in the machine flares white at once, and the floor starts to hum.
}}

From here, he fights with nothing held back. There's nothing left for him in this world.

**His last act.** When Vartius falls, he uses his last breath to **bring the tower down**. The collapse is **fast**.

### Ending B: They Didn't

Vartius fights to protect his life's work, still believing he's close to bringing Lysander back. When he falls, the tower doesn't come down at once.

**The prisoner.** Deep in the lower levels, **an old half-elf prisoner**, one of his own descendants, has been held there for longer than anyone can remember. Long captivity has made him savage. With Vartius dead, the bindings on his cell fail. He **breaks free**, and in his fury he starts tearing the tower apart from below.

The collapse is **slower**, but it ends the same way.

{{note
##### The difference
- **Ending A** is faster, but Lysander is already at peace.
- **Ending B** gives the party more time to save people, but **Lysander is still chained in the attic**. Players who remember her can race to the top and free her before the tower falls. If they don't, the falling tower crushes the golem, and her soul is finally released anyway. It's just that nobody was there.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 333}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-mithril}

# The collapse: clock and rescues

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### COUNTDOWN / RESCUE ACTIONS
Start the written clock when collapse begins: **8 rounds in A, 15 in B**. Resolve the tower at initiative 0. Record actions opening doors, movement while helping captives, golem orders and the chosen escape route. Keep both rescue choices and remaining rounds visible.
}}

## The Collapse

The tower falls, fast in Ending A and slower in Ending B. Either way, the party has a **countdown**: floors give way round by round.

**Ways out** (don't rely on any one):
- Back down through the **cave**.
- Order a **golem** to carry them.
- Ride the **levitating platform** as its magic fails and it sinks.
- Flying, *feather fall*, teleport or anything the party has saved.

**The captives and descendants.** They're still in their cells and nurseries as the tower falls.
- **Each person the party helps slows them down.**
- Saving everyone is **impossible**. Saving some is **possible**.
- What they choose, and who they leave, is the ending.

### The Countdown

| | Ending A | Ending B |
|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Rounds until the tower falls** | **8** | **15** |

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

{{pageNumber 334}}

\page {sw-pilot,sw-mithril}

<div style="position:absolute;top:0;height:0;overflow:hidden"><span id="c20b0084"></span><span id="c20b0085"></span><span id="c20b0086"></span></div>


{{swpilot-eyebrow SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM ONLY / CONTINUED}}

# The collapse: clock and rescues

{{swpilot-flow

- **Every round, on initiative count 0,** roll a d6 for the tower:
  - **1–2. Falling masonry.** Everyone inside makes a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 22 (4d10) bludgeoning damage on a failure, or half on a success.
  - **3–4. A floor gives way.** Pick one area. Anyone there makes a DC 15 Dexterity save or falls to the level below, taking 10 (3d6) bludgeoning damage.
  - **5–6. A breath.** Nothing falls this round.
- **Opening a cell or a nursery door** takes one character **an action**.
- **Leading or carrying captives** halves that character's speed.
- **The golems obey the party.** The tower still recognises them from the brass plates. A character can use an action to order a golem to carry **two people** out.
- **Getting out:** the cave route takes **3 rounds** from the soul machine. The levitating platform takes **1 round**, but it's sinking: everyone on it makes a DC 14 Dexterity save or falls.
- **Ending B, Lysander's attic:** it's on **Level 8, three floors above the soul machine**. Climbing there takes **2 rounds**; smashing the golem's core takes an action (AC 15, 30 hit points).
- **When the count reaches 0,** anyone still inside makes a DC 18 Dexterity save, taking 55 (10d10) bludgeoning damage on a failure, or half on a success, and is **buried** until someone digs them out.

**Count who they saved.** It goes into the epilogue.

**The half-elf prisoner.** In Ending A, he escapes on his own as his chamber breaks open. In Ending B, he's the one tearing the tower down. Either way, at the end he flies free into the dark. Where he goes is a hook for later.

{{swpilot-small
The source states that saving everyone is impossible, but does not fully quantify all nursery occupants or every route between floors. No new casualty quota or restriction on player spells is added here; unresolved rescue accounting remains in review notes.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

# Epilogue

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### PLAYER CHOICES / CONSEQUENCES
Use the actual saved-person count and world state. Restored woods, surviving mages, political succession, recovered remains and discovered airship are conditional outcomes to discuss, not automatic rewards or a rewritten ending.
}}

### For Silverwood

Let the players' choices shape it. Some questions to answer at the table:
- Who rules Silverwood now that the Xanthos line is gone?
- What happens to the people the party saved from the tower?
- Do the freed Tormented Mages stay?
- What grows in the restored Feywoods, and who farms it?
- What's left in the tower's ruins, and who goes looking?

### Laying Lysander to Rest

**Lysander's body** lay preserved at the top of the tower. If the party can recover it from the ruins, they can carry her to the **Tomb of the Founders** and lay her beside her friends, fulfilling **Saraphina's last request**: *"…uncover the truth and lay our beloved Lysander to rest here."*

### Loose Ends

For GMs who want to keep playing, or for a future book:
- **Selene and Andras** escaped. They're still out there, and they hold grudges.
- **Belial and Naamah** never appeared. They've lost a son, a nephew and a fortune.
- **Vashkar** will return from the Hells in time, and so will whatever sent him.
- **The dragon riders' caves** under Dragon's Obelisk.
- **The airship** in the southern ruins.
- **The Labyrinth**, still waiting.

### For the Characters

The ending is **open**. The characters walk away as heroes, carrying what they've seen. They can retire in Lysander's house or take their Mithril Passes and go wherever the road leads.

If they found the **airship** in the southern ruins (Platinum Chapter), this is where they can begin to rebuild it.

*The end.*

}}

# Mithril encounter reference

{{swpilot-flow

{{swpilot-note
##### AREA / WORLD STATE
Inside the tower, use the written encounters, not wandering rolls. Outside it, use Platinum one step easier except the printed Mithril table. The unresolved interaction between healed Feywoods and the later rot table is flagged; no new repopulation event is invented.
}}

*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD / MITHRIL / GM REFERENCE}}

### The Feywoods and the Deep Labyrinth

|:--|:--|
| Easy | **A rot beast** and **4 rotlings** |
| Medium | **A rot beast** and **3 rot hounds** |
| Hard | **A rot behemoth** and **4 rotlings** |
| Deadly | **2 rot behemoths** |

# Gelatinous Spider

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Gelatinous Spider
*Huge ooze, unaligned*
___
**Armor Class** :: 17 (rubbery hide)
**Hit Points** :: 195 (17d12 + 85)
**Speed** :: 40 ft., climb 40 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|22 (+6)|10 (+0)|20 (+5)|2 (−4)|8 (−1)|1 (−5)|
___
**Damage Resistances** :: bludgeoning, piercing and slashing from non-magical attacks
**Damage Immunities** :: acid
**Condition Immunities** :: blinded, charmed, deafened, exhaustion, frightened, prone
**Senses** :: blindsight 60 ft. (blind beyond this radius), passive Perception 9
**Languages** :: none
**Challenge** :: 9 (5,000 XP)
___
***Sticky Body.*** When a creature **misses** the spider with a melee weapon attack, the weapon sticks fast inside it. Pulling it free takes an action and a DC 12 Strength check.

***Transparent.*** While the spider stays still, it's nearly invisible: spotting it takes a DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) check.

***Spider Climb.*** The spider can climb any surface, including ceilings, without an ability check.
### Actions
***Multiattack.*** The spider makes two Bite attacks.

***Bite.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +10 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 17 (2d10 + 6) piercing damage plus 10 (3d6) acid damage.

***Web (Recharge 5–6).*** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, range 60 ft., one creature. *Hit:* The target is restrained by webbing (escape DC 16). The webbing has AC 10 and 15 hit points.

***Regurgitate (Recharge 5–6).*** The spider spits a 30-foot cone of stone, bones, skulls and old weapons. Each creature there makes a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw, taking 44 (8d10) bludgeoning damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.
}}

}}

# Vartius Xanthos

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame,wide
## Vartius Xanthos
*Medium humanoid (human), lawful neutral*
___
**Armor Class** :: 17 (robe of the archmagi)
**Hit Points** :: 247 (33d8 + 99)
**Speed** :: 30 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|10 (+0)|14 (+2)|16 (+3)|22 (+6)|16 (+3)|18 (+4)|
___
**Saving Throws** :: Con +9, Int +12, Wis +9
**Skills** :: Arcana +18, History +12, Insight +9
**Condition Immunities** :: charmed, frightened
**Senses** :: truesight 30 ft., passive Perception 13
**Languages** :: Common, Draconic, Elvish, Infernal, Sylvan and many more
**Challenge** :: 15 (13,000 XP)
___
***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If Vartius fails a saving throw, he can choose to succeed instead.

***Robe of the Archmagi.*** Vartius has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Spellcasting.*** Vartius's spellcasting ability is Intelligence (spell save DC 20, +12 to hit with spell attacks). He can cast:

At will: *mage hand*, *minor illusion*
3/day each: ***counterspell***, ***shield***, *misty step*
2/day each: *dimension door*, *fireball* (cast at 6th level, 10d6)
1/day each: *chain lightning*, *cone of cold*, *globe of invulnerability*, *wall of force*
### Actions
***Multiattack.*** Vartius makes two Arcane Bolt attacks, or casts a spell and makes one Arcane Bolt attack.

***Arcane Bolt.*** *Ranged Spell Attack:* +12 to hit, range 120 ft., one target. *Hit:* 22 (4d10) force damage.

***Soul Wrench (Recharge 5–6).*** One creature Vartius can see within 60 feet makes a DC 20 Charisma saving throw, taking 44 (8d10) necrotic damage on a failure, or half as much on a success. Vartius regains hit points equal to the damage dealt.
}}

# Vartius Xanthos

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Vartius Xanthos
### Legendary Actions
Vartius can take 3 legendary actions, choosing from the options below. Only one option can be used at a time, and only at the end of another creature's turn. He regains spent legendary actions at the start of his turn.

***Bolt.*** Vartius makes one Arcane Bolt attack.

***Blink.*** Vartius teleports up to 30 feet to a space he can see.

***Unmake (Costs 2 Actions).*** Each creature within 15 feet of Vartius makes a DC 20 Strength saving throw. On a failure, it takes 16 (3d10) force damage, is pushed 20 feet away and is knocked prone.
### Lair Actions: the Soul Machine
On initiative count 20 (losing ties), the soul machine acts. Choose one; don't use the same one twice in a row:
- **Soul Flicker.** One creature makes a DC 15 Charisma save or swaps places with another creature of Vartius's choice within 30 feet.
- **Grinding Gears.** A 20-foot square of floor lurches. Each creature there makes a DC 15 Dexterity save, taking 22 (4d10) bludgeoning damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.
- **The Machine Hums.** Vartius regains 20 hit points.
### Ending A: Unbound
When the players tell him they freed Lysander, Vartius stops defending himself and fights to destroy. From then on, **Soul Wrench recharges on a 4–6**, but he **no longer casts *counterspell* or *shield***. Telling him the truth makes him deadlier and easier to hit.
}}

}}

# Mirror Shade

{{swpilot-flow

{{monster,frame
## Mirror Shade
*Medium undead, chaotic evil*
___
**Armor Class** :: 13
**Hit Points** :: 45 (10d8)
**Speed** :: 40 ft.
___
|STR|DEX|CON|INT|WIS|CHA|
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
|6 (−2)|16 (+3)|10 (+0)|6 (−2)|12 (+1)|8 (−1)|
___
**Damage Vulnerabilities** :: radiant
**Damage Resistances** :: acid, cold, fire, lightning, thunder; bludgeoning, piercing and slashing from non-magical attacks
**Damage Immunities** :: necrotic, poison
**Condition Immunities** :: exhaustion, frightened, grappled, paralysed, petrified, poisoned, prone, restrained
**Senses** :: darkvision 60 ft., passive Perception 11
**Languages** :: none
**Challenge** :: 3 (700 XP)
___
***Mirror-Bound.*** Each shade is bound to one mirror (AC 12, 10 hit points). While its mirror is whole, a shade that drops to 0 hit points re-forms in the glass at the start of the next round, at full hit points, unless radiant damage killed it. **Break the mirror, and it stays dead.**
### Actions
***Strength Drain.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 10 ft., one creature. *Hit:* 10 (3d6) necrotic damage, and the target's Strength is reduced by 3 (1d6). **If this reduces its Strength to 0, the target dies.** Only *greater restoration* restores the lost Strength.
### Bonus Actions
***Mirror Step.*** The shade steps into one mirror and out of another within 60 feet.
}}

{{sweyebrow 21 · Encounter Tables}}

{{swbody

# Encounter Tables

## Wandering monsters by area and rank

*Silverwood doesn't scale to the party. These tables tell the GM what lives where at each rank, and how dangerous it is. Named fights (the goblin camp, the abomination, the battle) are in their chapters.*

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD | Encounter Tables}}
{{pageNumber 363}}

\page {sw,sw-body}

{{sweyebrow 21 · Encounter Tables}}

{{swbody

{{swcolumns

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0003

{{note
##### How to use these tables
- **When to roll.** Outside the walls, roll a **d6** once per half day of travel, and once at night unless the party is at a safe camp. On a **1**, there's an encounter.
- **How hard.** Roll a **d8**: **1–3 easy, 4–6 medium, 7 hard, 8 deadly.** Areas marked *dangerous* use their own roll.
- **Party size.** Balanced for **four characters**. For five, add one of the weakest creature listed; for six, add two. For three, remove one.
- **Experience.** Wandering monsters give **half experience**. The main story is where real progress is made (see the Copper Chapter).
- **Not every encounter is a fight.** Hungry beasts can be scared off or fed; goblins flee at half strength; fey would rather play than kill.
}}

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0004

All creatures are from the free rules (SRD 5.1) unless marked **(Bestiary)**. See *Bestiary* at the back of the book.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0005

## The Copper Pass

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0006

### Levels 3–4

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0007

Balanced for four **level 3** characters. At level 4, every row is about one step easier.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0008

### The Eastern Path and the Roads

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0009

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **4 goblins** raiding a broken-down cart |
| Medium | **3 goblins** riding **2 worgs** |
| Hard | **A bandit captain** and **4 bandits** "collecting a toll" |
| Deadly | **An ogre** herded by **3 goblins**, with **2 worgs** |

}}

}}

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0010

### The Goblin Hills

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0011

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **4 goblins** out foraging |
| Medium | **5 goblins** and **a worg** |
| Hard | **A bugbear** leading **6 goblins** |
| Deadly | **A war band:** **2 bugbears**, **2 worgs** and **6 goblins** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0012

*Goblins are hungry, not brave. They flee once half of them are down.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0013

### The Mining Village and the Mines

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0014

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **2 swarms of rats** and **4 giant rats** in a store shed |
| Medium | **3 darkmantles** in a disused working, or **4 thugs** spoiling for a fight in the village |
| Hard | **2 ankhegs** burrowing into a fresh working |
| Deadly | **A wererat** with **2 swarms of rats** and **6 giant rats** in the old tunnels |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0015

*In the village itself, roll only once a day. Most trouble here is people, not monsters.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0016

### The Rook's Nest

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0017

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **3 swarms of ravens**, furious about the party touching their nests |
| Medium | **2 harpies** roosting in the ruins |
| Hard | **3 harpies**, singing from the broken walls |
| Deadly | None. |

}}

}}

}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD | Encounter Tables}}
{{pageNumber 364}}

\page {sw,sw-body}

{{sweyebrow 21 · Encounter Tables}}

{{swbody

{{swcolumns

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0018

*The Rook's Nest is a **safe camp**. Don't roll for night encounters here.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0019

### The Xanthos Crypt: the Catacombs

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0020

*Until Lilith's pearl wakes them (Silver Chapter), the dead rest quietly. What moves down here now is what cleans up after them.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0021

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **4 giant centipedes** in the burial niches |
| Medium | **3 gray oozes** on a narrow walkway |
| Hard | **A gelatinous cube** filling a walkway, with **2 swarms of rats** fleeing ahead of it |
| Deadly | **The cleaners:** **a gelatinous cube**, **an ochre jelly** and **2 gray oozes** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0022

*Remember the walkways are **one person wide**. A gelatinous cube in one of them is a wall that moves.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0023

### Dragon's Obelisk and the Miners' Graveyard

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0024

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **4 wolves** by the graves |
| Medium | **2 dire wolves** on the cliff path |
| Hard | **3 dire wolves** hunting together |
| Deadly | **At night only: the banshee.** Her wail can kill. |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0025

*By day, roll on this table as normal. At night, any encounter at the Obelisk itself is the **banshee**. At Copper rank she should be a warning, not a fight: let the party hear the wail from a distance first.*

}}

}}

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0026

### Greenhart Grove

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0027

No wandering monsters. The barns are busy and safe, and the plants don't stir until the **Gold Chapter**.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0028

### Swamps and Wild Forest (dangerous)

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0029

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Medium | **2 giant toads**, **4 lizardfolk** hunting, or **an owlbear** guarding its territory |
| Hard | **2 crocodiles** and **a will-o'-wisp** luring travellers into deep water |
| Deadly | **A green hag** and her **2 giant toads** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0030

*For this area, roll a **d4** instead of a d8: **1–2 medium, 3 hard, 4 deadly.***

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0031

### The Feywoods (very dangerous)

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0032

At Copper rank, **every encounter here is deadly.** Warn the players before they go, and mean it.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0033

| d4 | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| 1 | **2 manticores** |
| 2 | **A chimera** |
| 3 | **2 owlbears** |
| 4 | **Lirien,** if the party has heard the bard's rhyme about her. Otherwise, **3 satyrs** who lead the party in circles until dark, then reroll. |

}}

}}

}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD | Encounter Tables}}
{{pageNumber 365}}

\page {sw,sw-body}

{{sweyebrow 21 · Encounter Tables}}

{{swbody

{{swcolumns

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0034

## Bait Attacks

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0035

The Blackwoods' bait draws creatures onto marked wagons (see the Copper Chapter). The bait grows stronger as the campaign goes on.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0036

| Chapter | Creatures | Difficulty |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Copper** (early) | **4 wolves**, or **2 swarms of rats** and **4 giant rats** | Easy |
| **Copper** (late) | **2 worgs** and **3 wolves** | Medium |
| **Silver** | **6 dire wolves** | Medium at level 5 |
| **Gold** | **A chimera** (see the Gold Chapter) | Medium at level 7 |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0037

*Bait attacks give full experience. They're part of the main story.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0038

## The Silver Pass

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0039

### Levels 5–6

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0040

Balanced for four **level 5** characters. At level 6, every row is about one step easier.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0041

### The Eastern Path and the Roads

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0042

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **A bandit captain** and **4 bandits** |
| Medium | **2 ogres** driven by **4 goblins** |
| Hard | **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** in Blackwood colours, "inspecting" travellers *(if the council vote passed, use this as a medium encounter instead)* |
| Deadly | **A troll** and **2 ogres** |

}}

}}

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0043

### The Goblin Hills

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0044

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **A bugbear** leading **6 goblins** |
| Medium | **A goblin rider** on **an ogre**, with **6 goblins** (Bestiary) |
| Hard | **2 bugbears**, **2 worgs** and **8 goblins** |
| Deadly | **2 goblin riders** on **2 ogres**, **a goblin hexer** and **6 goblins** (Bestiary) |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0045

### The Mining Village and the Mines

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0046

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **2 ankhegs** |
| Medium | **2 wererats** and **2 swarms of rats** |
| Hard | **4 gricks** in a collapsed working |
| Deadly | **An earth elemental** and **a xorn**. Something has been eating the mythril. |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0047

### The Rook's Nest

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0048

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **3 harpies** |
| Medium | **5 harpies** |
| Hard | **2 gargoyles** perched among the ruins, and **3 harpies** |
| Deadly | None. It's still a safe camp. |

}}

}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD | Encounter Tables}}

{{swblock,#c21b0049

### The Xanthos Crypt: the Catacombs

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0050

*Once Lilith has used her pearl, the dead walk until the abomination falls. Afterwards, go back to the Copper table: it's now easy.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0051

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **2 ghouls** and **4 zombies** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0052

### Dragon's Obelisk and the Miners' Graveyard

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0053

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **4 dire wolves** |
| Medium | **6 dire wolves** |
| Hard | **4 griffons** nesting on the cliff |
| Deadly | **At night:** **the banshee** and **6 specters**. **By day:** **2 wyverns** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0054

*At full moon, the banshee can be talked to (Silver Chapter). Don't roll the deadly row on a full-moon night.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0055

### Swamps and Wild Forest

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0056

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **2 giant toads** and **4 lizardfolk** |
| Medium | **A green hag** and her **2 giant toads** |
| Hard | **A shambling mound** and **2 crocodiles** |
| Deadly | **2 trolls** |

}}

}}

{{swcol

{{swblock,#c21b0057

### The Feywoods (dangerous)

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0058

Roll a **d4**: **1–2 medium, 3 hard, 4 deadly.**

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0059

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Medium | **2 manticores** |
| Hard | **3 manticores** |
| Deadly | **2 chimeras** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0060

*If the party has heard the bard's rhyme, Lirien may turn up instead of any roll.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0061

### Greenhart Grove

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0062

Still no wandering monsters.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0063

## The Gold Pass

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0064

### Levels 7–8

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0065

Balanced for four **level 7** characters. At level 8, every row is about one step easier.

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0066

### The Eastern Path: the Blockade

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0067

*Once the blockade starts, the road belongs to the Blackwood army. Every encounter here is a patrol.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0068

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **A veteran** and **4 thugs** |
| Medium | **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** |
| Hard | **A knight**, **2 veterans** and **4 thugs** |
| Deadly | **A mage**, **2 veterans** and **2 thugs** |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0069

*Patrols that lose a fight send for more. Raid the road too often and the army comes looking.*

}}

}}

}}

}}

{{footnote SHADOW OVER SILVERWOOD | Encounter Tables}}
{{pageNumber 367}}

\page {sw,sw-body}

{{sweyebrow 21 · Encounter Tables}}

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0071

| Difficulty | Encounter |

| Medium | **A goblin rider** on **an ogre**, **2 bugbears** and **6 goblins** |
| Hard | **2 goblin riders** on **2 ogres**, **a goblin hexer** and **6 goblins** |
| Deadly | **2 hill giants** driving **6 goblins** ahead of them |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0072

### The Mining Village and the Mines

}}

}}

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0076

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0078

| Difficulty | Encounter |

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0080

*From the blockade until the greenhouse is destroyed. Afterwards, no wandering monsters.*

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0081

| Difficulty | Encounter |
|:--|:--|
| Easy | **A bloodvine shambler** and **4 Greenhart drones** (Bestiary) |
| Medium | **2 bloodvine shamblers** and **3 drones** |

}}

### The Feywoods (dangerous)

}}

Roll a **d4**: **1–2 medium, 3 hard, 4 deadly.**

}}

{{swblock,#c21b0085
